# Changelog

All notable changes to Fenolite are documented here. The format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project uses
[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) (pre-1.0: minor versions may break).

The authored offline example inventory explicitly records CC0 ownership, file hashes and release archive member hashes. Entry notes preserve authored/vendored/built-in precedence and distinguish registered public facts from pending external seed/release proofs.
- Add cited datasheet parameter proposals, independent agreement for dense packages, deterministic library generation and a document-bound human review gate (c0265). Human review stays outside MCP; entry notes record the current guide counts and active budget deltas.

- Add offline library rules, waivers, deterministic HTML review sheets, stable-id comparisons and human acceptance with automatic review staleness (c0262). Review metadata uses `[[reviewed_by]]` and binds the accepted sheet SHA-256; the entry separates implemented APIs from archival.
- **Public URL paths in residue scans** (c0316): distinguish HTTP(S) path components from local user paths while retaining file URI, query, fragment, private-token, blob and other structural checks across supported encodings, archives and history.

### Added

- **Local library import** (c0264): `fenolite library import` reads local KiCad, Altium and STEP files or bounded zip archives into unverified project devices, with source digests, source terms, non-shippable origins and family-generator screening. Plans preserve imported definitions and STEP bytes; re-import protects hand edits. `catalog import` remains a byte-compatible alias for one minor release. Entry notes identify shared importer mechanics, the current dispatcher and the remaining formal import-edge union.
- **Portable batch validation** (c0311): library generation rejects native rooted output folders; library Git fetches preserve payload bytes independently of the caller's global newline settings. Portable JSON/path/command proofs retain exact byte comparisons, and the KiCad 10 corpus job uses a temporary directory available inside its container with matching artifact collection. Heavy import census rows retain their measured CI provenance, with a fast table-coverage preflight.
- **Tests shipped with blocks** (c0270): `fenolite.testing.stimulus`, `source` and `draw` turn typed port inputs into rig records for `instantiate`; cached requirement rows, generator achieved ranges and integer placement fields support plain assertions. `fenolite blocks test` checks usage builds, canonical circuit/rules goldens and zero-argument tests without tools or pytest; golden regeneration uses reviewed plan replay. Authored fixtures, a project example, CI hooks and the `block-testing` guide page document the convention. Convention paths come from `blocks show` without decorator fields; entry notes record the final catalog verbs and testing names. The L9 CI block step measured five usage builds, five equal goldens and sixteen tests on Linux, macOS and Windows; the complete unit jobs failed other checks, so full CI and KiCad read-back gates remain open.

- **Library block packages** (c0273): `blocks index` writes checked static block records, and `list`, `show` and `doc --script` discover vendored blocks without importing them. Builds isolate script-local package imports, preserve item pins and report stale records. `blocks upgrade` reviews instance versions and optional circuit differences, then writes only the lock through a source-bound plan; library licences, provenance and missing-item restoration keep the existing `get` protocol. Entry notes distinguish builtin/library/file sources and implemented namespace isolation from still-active dependency specifications.
- **Signal topologies** (c0258): ordered pad trees across explicit series bridges, exact femtosecond/milli-decibel inputs, protocol bundles without numeric defaults, and tool-free `topology.rules` delay, skew, loss, stub and order checks with subjects. Conservative conversion exposes eligible whole-net delay and pair skew as existing KiCad rules; other constraints stay in Fenolite. Rule specificity is informative by default, opt-in through `design.rules.precedence("specificity")`, with `rules.shadowed` and `check --explain-rules`. Includes a routed catalog-only clock/USB example and a tested guide page.
- **Supplier lookup framework** (c0267): optional keyring credentials, bounded HTTPS/rate/cache machinery, static provider readiness, identity drafts with grammar review suggestions, and transient offers excluded from stored plans. Existing supplier plugins remain supported. Authored offline mappings for all four sources include per-lookup OAuth token redaction, basic Nexar fields without assumed add-ons, and strict error/container checks. Four unavailable production policies preserve source-rights gates; a primary Mouser terms read confirms its storage restriction. JLC remains unregistered.

- **Value ranges** (c0246): `Range` values with exact bounds built at the DSL boundary: `q.pct(p)` and `q.pct(lo, hi)`, `q.max()`, `q.min()` and `between(a, b)`; worst-case `+ - * /` over a fixed table of unit products and quotients (no solver), `within()` and `text()`. A part given a range keeps its value text (the nominal) and records the range in the new optional model field `Component.value_range` as `ValueRange` bounds of canonical exact decimals (`Decimal10`, up to 15 digits and an exponent of ±30, rounded outward). KiCad and Altium outputs do not change.
- **Connection helpers** (c0247): `series(net, part, ..., net)` connects a two-terminal chain, naming the joints `<left>-<right>`; `decouple(a, b, *caps, near=, within=)` also records a `near()` rule per capacitor; `pull_up` and `pull_down` take a net or an `I2C` bus; `part.a`/`part.b` are the ends of a two-pin part; `part.unused_no_connect(keep=())` marks at build every open pin except `power_in` and must-connect pins. New build errors `build.not-two-terminal` and `build.must-connect-unconnected`. The blink example uses `series` with unchanged pinned output bytes; source-edit regressions retain their original circuit mutations.
- **Design lock** (c0249): a part can be created without a ref (`design.part(...)`, `module.part(...)`, `name=`, `prefix=`); the build assigns the lowest free number per prefix in a natural path order and records it in `<script stem>.lock` (schema `fenolite.lock.v0`, sections `[refs]`, and `[picks]`/`[pins]` reserved), written through the build's plan and confirm. `fenolite build --lock` and `--frozen` (exit 7, `FEN-7004`, when the lock would change); `design.moved()` carries entries to the new path; `lock.*` issue codes. Designs with explicit refs only get no lock and build as before. Entry notes confirm device-prefix support, current section order and FEN-7004 ownership while retaining the complete gate.
- **Manual edit merge** (c0250), phases 1 and 2: a three-way merge per property, keyed by component path, between the base (what the last build was given and wrote), the code (the script plus `edits.toml`) and the CAD side (the board, and the schematic for values and footprints), for `position`, `rotation`, `value` and `footprint`. One side changed: it wins; both changed differently: `sync.conflict` (error, exit 5, every write refused). Every build writes the base record `<out>/fenolite-base.json` (schema `fenolite.base.v0`, recorded in `.fenolite/build.json`; Altium builds too, without a CAD side). `edits.toml` (schema `fenolite.edits.v0`) beside the script replaces and extends `placements.toml`, records the script value each edit was made over (`over`), and is applied to both targets and to `--discard-layout`; `placements.toml` stays readable (`edit.legacy-file`) and `fenolite sync` migrates it. `fenolite build --take code|cad|board|schematic[:PATH[#PROPERTY]]` resolves conflicts (`result.preserved.edits`); `sync` records edits, never resolves, refuses `--take`, and `sync --check` exits 5 on a conflict. New codes `sync.conflict`, `edit.take-unused`, `edit.legacy-file`, `edit.base-invalid`, `edit.unrecorded`, `edit.code-wins`, `edit.base-missing`. Behaviour change: once a base record exists, a changed unlocked `place()` moves a part that nobody moved in KiCad (`edit.code-wins`) instead of being ignored (`layout.place-overridden`); a part moved by hand keeps its position, and a footprint locked in KiCad under a changed script is a `cad-locked` conflict. The base record changes the digests of `.fenolite/build.json`; every other output keeps its bytes. Phase 2: `reference` and the user properties the script names (`property:<name>`; one removed in KiCad counts as `""`, one added in KiCad stays opaque) are merged too; the base record gains them, so `fenolite-base.json` and `.fenolite/build.json` change their bytes once. A `reference` of `edits.toml` recorded over the part's explicit or locked ref is a hand ref of the design lock, so the lock records a part re-annotated in KiCad; `sync` reads the lock for the code side of references. `build --frozen` refuses any `--take` and any `edit.unrecorded` as a change of the lock (exit 7, `FEN-7004`, one `lock.changed` per take or key). `fenolite view --check` reads `build` and `sync` replies and marks each `sync.conflict` on its footprint with the two `build --take ... --dry-run` command lines. A footprint swapped in KiCad for another catalog footprint resolves, and a code value equal to the unchanged CAD value (an edit recorded by `sync`) no longer gives `edit.code-wins`. New fixture `tests/data/lens/edit_merge`, its acceptance loop and KiCad oracle; the blink example README shows the edit loop. The TypeScript viewer shows structured conflict values and offers finite takes through source-bound plan review, confirm and scoped receipt/undo; every input/write and source execution context is reviewable. `view --edit` aliases `--allow-actions`.
- **Interface parameters** (c0252): `Rail` (`Power` stays its other name, with the same model bytes), `Ground` and `Logic`, `design.rail()` and `design.ground()`; closed parameter tables (`fenolite.dsl.interfaces.PARAMETERS`: `alias` voltage, `sum` current that needs an explicit `in` or `out` port, `bound` thresholds and `abs_max`, `link` reference) with exact c0246 ranges on interfaces and block ports (`b.port(..., voltage=, current=, requires=)`, `port.set()`); `reference=` on the buses and `pullup=` on `I2C`; `connect()` between whole interfaces through the port nets (UART ports cross, a closed compatibility table, `DslError` at the call) and net-like rails; device records declare their ports in `[[interfaces]]` (`part.port("vdd")`, `part.vdd`), recorded as model `Port`s of the component; `pull_up` takes `i2c` and `logic` ports; the default stage `circuit.rules` of `fenolite check` (skips `no-model`, `no-parameters`) and `fenolite build` judge `circuit.rail-voltage`, `circuit.rail-current` (loads against sources), `circuit.rail-sources`, `circuit.rail-unset`, `circuit.logic-level` (high, low and withstand), `circuit.logic-unknown`, `circuit.bus-pullup`, `circuit.requires-missing` and `circuit.port-split`, an error stopping the build; `build.i2c-pullup-missing` also accepts a pull-up to a power port; `examples/sensor_rails/` in a good and a bad variant.
- **Pin assignment at the interface level** (c0257): device records may offer interface options on several pin sets (`[[options]]`: `i2c`, `spi`, `uart`, `usb2`, `diff_pair` and the single-pin `pin`); `part.take(I2C, option=, pins=, name=)` takes one, and `fenolite build` chooses the pins by a deterministic solve (valid locked choices first, then part path, take key, option name, alternative index; `--seed` does not enter), lists them in `result.pins` and records them in the `[pins]` section of the design lock through the plan; `--frozen` refuses a changed choice. New build findings `pins.unsatisfiable`, `pins.option-unknown`, `pins.pinned-conflict`, `pins.search-limit`, `pins.lock-stale`, `pins.unused-take` and `pins.lock-dropped`; `examples/pins/` and `docs/pins.md`.
- **Blocks** (c0251): `@block` functions with typed ports, local nets, recorded parameters and allocated refs; `design.use`; `build.port-unused` and `build.block-net-escape`.
- **Device catalog** (c0248): device records (`fenolite.device.v0` TOML: symbol, footprint, pin functions and their pad map, MPN, datasheet, ratings, `ends`, `ref_prefix`) in the new model `DeviceDef` and `Library.devices`; ten generic shipped devices (`INFERRED`, no MPN) and project devices in `devices/*.toml` beside the script. `Part(device=...)`, `design.part(...)`, `module.part(...)` and `b.part(...)` take `device=` (exactly one of `lib_id` and `device`); pins are named by function with checked access (`PinNameError`, closest names), `part.pad(function)`, `part.a`/`part.b` follow the device's `ends`, its `must_connect` pins are never marked by `unused_no_connect()`, and its `ref_prefix` names the parts the design lock numbers. The build resolves functions, writes the `Datasheet` field, refuses a device that disagrees with its symbol or footprint (`build.device-mismatch`) and lists `result.devices` and `result.device_files`; `device.edited` and `device.overrides-builtin`. `fenolite catalog list|show --kind device` and `fenolite catalog import` (a stamped template, `catalog.pin-without-pad`, `catalog.pad-unmapped`, `FEN-7001` over a hand edit). The exact unit parser moved to `fenolite.core.quantities`; `fenolite.dsl.quantity` keeps its names.
- **Electrical requirements** (c0274), phases A to F: device limits are split into `abs_max` (absolute maximum rating) and `operating` (the range the part needs to work), each a `{ min, max }` table or a string read as the maximum; c0248's single `{ min, max }` form is refused (`FEN-3004`, hint "say abs_max or operating"), as is an `operating` range outside its `abs_max`. `[[limits]]` states a voltage limit between two functions (`between = ["BST", "SW"]`); pins take the logic thresholds `input_high_min`, `input_low_max`, `output_high_min`, `output_low_max` and `output_current_max`; the optional `class` key names a part class of the roles' vocabulary. Device temperatures are stored in millikelvin (`"-40degC"` and `"233.15K"` read alike). Model: `Rating.limit`, `DevicePin.ratings` and `thresholds` (replacing `voltage` and `current`), `Threshold`, `PinPairLimit`, `DeviceDef.limits` and `cls`; `RangeUnit` gains `kelvin`. DSL: `kelvin()` and `celsius()` with `temperature_text()`, the products V·V, A·A, A²·Ω and V²/Ω (DSL-only units `volt_squared`, `ampere_squared`), and one-sided ranges in `+ - * /` while the result keeps a bound. Phase B to F: the part classes `ferrite`, `fuse` and `jumper` (prefixes `FB`, `F`, `JP`, read before the value) and the device record's `class` as evidence of `roles.classify`; a ferrite's current is judged like an inductor's (derating key `ferrite_current`); a device port takes a logic threshold it omits from its member pins (the strictest). Circuit waivers: `design.waive("circuit.rating-voltage", "C3:voltage", reason=..., digest=...)` accepts one finding by its row and the digest of what was judged; every `circuit.*` error or warning's hint ends with `digest <16 hex>`, `fenolite check` and `fenolite build` apply the same waivers, and a changed value or limit gives `check.waiver-expired` (`result.waivers.expired`); `Waiver.digest` in `findings.json`. A net with no stated voltage takes the voltage of the stated nets it reaches through ferrites, fuses, net ties, stated jumpers and 0 Ω parts (`design.contracts(propagate=False)` turns it off; `examples/circuit/ferrite/`), and every applied value records its origin (`value.origin`). Requirement rows gain `decidable` (`build`, `place`, `route`), `digest` and `waiver`; a row whose stage is not reached and that does not fail is `deferred` (`summary.deferred`), and a role net with a current on an open path lists a deferred `roles.width-current:<net>` row. `fenolite check --matrix` returns the verification matrix (`result.matrix`: rows with stable ids and a receipt of the model and device digests), read-only, also as a table with `--text`. Row ids: `circuit.rail-voltage:<rail>:<port path>`, one `roles.width-current:<net>` per net. Resistance parsing accepts uppercase K and Greek micro, preserves m/M, and handles unmapped matched prefixes safely.
- **Fidelity proof** (c0278): `fenolite verify-source SCRIPT --against ORIGINAL` builds the script in a temporary folder and compares it with the original at every level a side can reach (`fidelity.*` codes, skipped levels named), with accepted differences in `PROVENANCE.json` (`fidelity.accepted`, unused entries reported) and `--record` of `fidelity.proof` through plan and confirm. `fenolite equivalent` gains `--strict` (level 5 item by item), `--level 6` (rules and stack-up), `7` (exact geometry), `8` (rendered layer masks within `--pixel-ppm`) and `S` (actual native schematic geometry and resolved pin connectivity); its default level and its output for existing calls do not change. Add strict authored all-level round trips and contract tests, licence-checked external `rtf` project descriptors, count ratchets, recorded strict corpus refusals/differences and a prepared zone-outline probe; native/GUI gates and unreviewed baselines remain open. Explicit S works in the Python API and on raw schematic/project sides without a netlist export; active v0 schemas accept only the added literal S, and nine pre-S integer/default results retain identical bytes. Entry notes distinguish implemented source-drawing APIs from archival and keep independent corpus, native and full proof limits.
- **Imported boards proved as copies** (c0281): `PROVENANCE.json` of `fenolite import` holds `"copy": true`, and `verify-source` and `build` of such a folder run the copper guard in `warn` (findings listed, never refused); `verify-source --copper-check refuse|warn` and `result.copper_check.mode_source` of `build`. The KiCad copper guard accepts a gap down to 500 nm below the clearance, as KiCad 10.0.6's DRC does (`H-K-COPPER-EPSILON`, measured). A pad on no net upstream stays on no net (no symbol pin; level 2 reads an `unconnected-(…)` net of one element as no net); the import reports `outline-width` and `layer-table`; level 6 pairs layers by name (`equiv.layer-field`), level 7 compares graphic widths (`equiv.graphic-field`), leaves out `fenolite.path` and counts opaque items such as teardrops (`equiv.opaque-count`); `--against x.kicad_pro` reads only that project; `--record` writes `fidelity.proof.skipped`; a footprint derived from a bottom-side instance keeps its pad and field angles.
- **fenolite-library and `fenolite get`** (c0279): third-party material lives only in the separate repository `fenolite-library` (AGENTS.md, ADR-0003, ADR-0008), one folder per item under `<kind>/<licence family>/` with `item.json` (`fenolite.library-item.v0`), `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `PROVENANCE.json` (`fenolite.provenance.v0`). `fenolite get KIND ID` copies an item and its dependencies into `fenolite_library/` beside the script (a board into `--into DIR`), regenerates `fenolite_library/ATTRIBUTION.md` and pins version, release, index and tree digests in the design lock's new section `[library.items]`; licences are shown in the plan and any id but `CC0-1.0` is accepted by name (`--accept-licence`, `FEN-4003`); an id outside the shipped allow-list (`cli/data/library-licences.toml`) is refused (`FEN-3021`). Releases come from `--from` (a release folder, a library checkout or a `file://` git URL), the offline cache (`FENOLITE_LIBRARY_DIR`) or, only under `--confirm`, the library's release assets checked against pinned digests; `--offline` opens no connection (`FEN-6006`). Also `--list`, `--search`, `--status`, `--locked`, `--remove`, `--refresh`. `fenolite build` exits 6 with `FEN-6004` when a pinned folder is missing. `fenolite library audit|replay|release|gallery` are the library's CI tooling: layout, licence, notice and provenance rules with `--base` version rules, replay of the recorded conversion steps (a step never holds `--plan`), byte-reproducible releases, the proof gate (a skipped level passes only with its recorded reason), and a gallery of attributed cards. See `docs/library-repo.md`. Fetched Git checkouts override ambient automatic newline conversion so pinned payload hashes remain stable on Windows. The shared authored offline device example lives in `examples/library-devices/`, with local release, lock and native-byte proofs.
- **Placement in code** (c0255): `part.place()` takes references besides `x, y`: the relations `right_of`, `left_of`, `above` and `below` with `gap` and `align`, the edge keys `left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`, `center_x` and `center_y` (a length or an edge expression such as `board.left + mm(2)`), and `by=part.pad(n)`; `design.board(...)` now returns the board as a reference (`design.board_ref`). A block instance takes a pose (`inst.place(...)`, absolute or relative) and moves as one rigid group; `row`, `column`, `grid` and `pack` arrange a frame's unplaced parts (`b.layout(...)`, `inst.layout(...)`, `design.layout(...)`); `layout(reuse=PATH, copper=True)` places a block from a board laid out for it alone, parts by block-local path and nets by port, its copper copied and locked. The build resolves everything to integer nanometres in the order of the references and records it in `result.placement.relative` and `result.placement.blocks`, and the block poses and a digest in `.fenolite/build.json`; a block whose pose changes moves its parts kept from the board and the copper joined only to their pads. The written precedence (`docs/lens.md`, "Placement precedence"): a locked `place()`, the board, `edits.toml`, an unlocked `place()`, the frame's strategy or reuse, staging; a relative request follows the merged position of its reference. New codes `layout.relative-cycle`, `layout.relative-unresolved`, `layout.strategy-overflow`, `layout.block-moved` and `reuse.port-unmapped`; `reuse.fit_block(transform=...)`, `reuse.copy_copper(nets=...)` and `reuse.copy_block`; hypothesis `H-G-REL-RESOLVE`; examples `examples/relative/`. Designs without placement in code build byte for byte as before.
- **Drawn schematics** (c0276): `design.load_schematic("schematic.toml")` draws the schematic from a file beside the script (schema `fenolite.drawn-schematic.v0`, `docs/drawn-schematic.md`) instead of generating it: symbol and field poses, wires, junctions, local, global and hierarchical labels, power symbols, buses, bus entries and aliases, sheet references with pins, repeated sheets (one sheet shown by several module instances) and verbatim opaque items. Code stays the only source of the nets: the build checks the coverage, writes the sheets, reads them back, resolves them without a tool (`fenolite.model.drawn_connect`) and refuses a drawing that means other nets (`build.schematic-netlist-differs`); `result.schematic` gains `source` and `drawn`. A new stage `schematic.drawn` of `fenolite check` compares the sheets on disk with the recorded circuit; `fenolite sync --to-source` copies a drawing edited in KiCad back into the file and refuses changed nets, and `--freeze-schematic` writes the file from a generated schematic. The resolver equals `kicad-cli sch export netlist` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 on the authored fixtures (connectivity and names); the writer cuts wires at junctions and buses at their entries, which 9.0.9 needs. New model entities `Junction`, `BusWire`, `BusEntry`, `BusAlias` and `SheetPin`, sixteen `drawn.*` issue codes. A drawn sheet marked `generated = true` is drawn by the generator with c0269's block rules for each module instance that references it (written at `sheets/<module>.kicad_sch`, byte for byte as a generated build; the reference must carry the generator's sheet pins, else `drawn.sheet-pin-unmatched`), and a label on a sheet pin's point joins that pin. `fenolite verify-source` now runs level S: the sheets of the original and of the build are extracted and compared item by item (`fenolite.checks.equivalence.compare_drawn`: sheet instances by path, symbols by component and unit, wires and buses as maximal collinear segments, other items as multisets, and the first pin whose drawn group differs), joined by reference; `drawn-unavailable` now means a side's sheets were not read. KiCad project imports now preserve source drawings in `schematic.toml`/`load_schematic` and vendor the embedded native symbol geometry and hidden power pins; the import self-check runs the written-byte drawing guard, and conflicting source definitions refuse before staging. Board-only imports keep their emitted bytes. The authored `import_python` example is refreshed through its original import recipe with retained drawing, native symbols, provenance and matching byte guards. Mirrored source power symbols retain their pose through canonical TOML and both KiCad targets, and level S compares the mirror. Legacy power instances without a native power flag preserve their one hidden global input; malformed lookalikes refuse. Pinless native graphic instances and their embedded definitions survive. Explicitly board/BOM-excluded visible passive instances also survive as opaque; other unsupported electrical symbols refuse. Explicit instance exclusions are honored even when library defaults differ, without changing those defaults. Bare embedded aliases retain native geometry under their original library namespace, with collision checks. Compatible per-unit native aliases survive import, build and sync, including pin-pad variants; incompatible or missing bindings refuse and the actual written connections remain guarded. Root-sheet parts retain code-module prefixes during extraction. KiCad drawing connectivity honors native slash aliases while keeping their drawn text and distinct-code-net refusal. Active source symbols remain electrical parts when their native footprint carries board-only metadata; the footprint attribute survives independently, while duplicate and unmatched references keep their strict handling. Altium drawn builds now write native sheet geometry in ASCII or binary, flatten repeated instances explicitly, report unsupported graphics/poses and coordinate rounding, and verify actual native coverage, pin maps and connectivity before staging and after templates. Per-unit compatible aliases retain their native bodies and library entries; their files and the drawn source bind reviewed plans. Automatic/Global scope qualifies colliding local names with reported text changes. The behavior stays INFERRED; native application acceptance remains pending because Altium is unavailable. The drawn corpus driver verifies manifest inputs and records strict source/lowered/native partition and naming results or refusals, retaining value-free reports on CI failures without accepting differences. Corpus child rows retain every registered project owner, complete annotation/instance context and hashed project inputs; native export uses the real owner root, with strict orphan/missing/unsupported outcomes. Source-bound local 9.0.2 observations retain 51 equal row contexts, 41 explicit refusals and naming warnings; required corpus/CI closure remains pending.
- **Block schematics** (c0269): `b.schematic(schematic_group(key, *members, title=, anchor=, after=, flow=), ...)` declares, once per block, the groups of its own parts, reused by every instance: KiCad's readable layout orders the block's sheet by them, and Altium block composition gives one `schematic_block` `<instance path>/<key>` each (never unused intent, never refused by a KiCad build); `fenolite.dsl.block_schematics(design)`. A block instance's KiCad sheet carries a hierarchical label per port member (`<port>` or `<port>_<member>`) and its reference the sheet pins, while the parent keeps a global label of the circuit net at each pin, so net names do not change (measured with `kicad-cli` 10.0.6); instances of one block share one arrangement. The build's own netlist reads hierarchical labels and sheet pins. In Altium (`sheets="modules"`) a block's ports and sheet entries are its declared ports, stubs keep the circuit net names. New codes `build.block-port-merged` (info) and `build.block-sheet-name-clash` (error); `b.port` refuses a port whose sheet name another port has. Designs without blocks keep their bytes. Entry notes cover the implemented Rail/Power membership rule and preserve the deferred Altium direction proof.
- **Net roles, phase 1: role copper** (c0243): `fenolite copper PATH` (KiCad board or built project, through the reviewed-plan protocol, with `--gate drc`) and the DSL call `design.role_copper(...)` (resolved by the build after placement, for the KiCad and the Altium target) generate the copper of the confirmed and declared net roles (c0222) of a placed board. Each `power` net gets one locked zone per layer, the union of bands along a rectilinear tree between its pads, hot-loop legs first, sized from the role's current by the S-0851 rule of thumb or, with a temperature rise, the S-0269 capacity fit, and widened up to twice that where the clearance allows; a `switch_node` keeps exactly its required width and the switching distance from its victims (`role.sw.*`); ground gets a plane per reference layer with stitching vias (at least two per overlap, 5 mm pitch by default); pads connect solid for power, switch-node and high-voltage nets and by thermal relief on through-hole ground pads only; via arrays where a net changes layer are counted from its current (`milliamps_per_via`); each `high_voltage` net gets a clearance rule and a keep-out band at the user's own distance (`roles.limit-missing` without one). Phase 2's locked routes and `role.corridor.<group>.<net>` areas are obstacles; run first, phase 1 writes provisional corridors of the same names, which `route --roles critical` replaces. The result is judged by c0222's role judge on the simulated pour (`roles.copper-worse`, nothing written); a manifest of fingerprints in `.fenolite/role-copper.json` keeps items the user edited (`roles.copper-kept`, `--regenerate`) and makes a second run plan nothing. The Altium build now resolves generated and role copper in its in-memory KiCad build (zones as polygons poured by c0215, keep-outs, Polygon Connect Style rules); `kicad.generate.altium` remains only for `--copper-from`. Each reference board of `examples/roles/` gains `good_roles.py` and `bad_roles.py`; eight issue codes (`roles.copper-*`, `roles.corridor-blocked`), hypotheses `H-G-ROLE-COPPER-*` and sources S-0860 to S-0863.
- **Net roles, phase 2: critical routes** (c0244): `fenolite route PATH --router octilinear --roles critical [--group NAME]... [--rip] [--gate drc]` routes the gate loops and Kelvin pairs of the confirmed or declared net roles (c0222) before anything else, through the reviewed-plan protocol. c0228's octilinear router gains a role mode (feature `roles`, `RoutingJob.roles`, one `RoleOutcome` per route): gate loops first, then Kelvin pairs; a Kelvin branch enters its sense pad on the inner half, a gate return starts at the source or Kelvin-source pad, branches touch their own power copper only at their end pads, keep the profile's distance from switching copper, take the smallest gate-loop area or the shortest Kelvin pair, and the pattern-routing Z shapes join the five shapes. A `role_check` extra judges the roles before and after the job and removes a group that gets worse (`route.role-refused`); every written item is locked and every branch gets a corridor `role.corridor.<group>.<net>` that pours fill around. `result.roles` gives each group's status, findings before and after and its copper; a group that cannot be routed gets `route.role-unrouted` with a reason and a proposal from closed sets, and unconfirmed inferred groups are listed and not routed (`route.role-unconfirmed`).
- **Boards to code** (c0275): `fenolite import PATH --to python --out DIR` turns a KiCad project, board or schematic, or an Altium project or PCB document, into a Fenolite project folder through the reviewed-plan protocol: `design.py` (board and cut-outs, nets with their source names, net classes and minimums, parts with explicit refs and c0249 keys in the modules of their `fenolite.path`, connections, placements with the source lock flags, zones, rule areas and drawings, in the frame of `place()` so the build writes the source coordinates exactly), `copper.py` (tracks, arcs and vias as tables), the vendored `lib/` (footprints brought back from the board through the library derive, variants as `<name>_2`, symbols drawn from their pads) with project tables, `design.lock`, `PROVENANCE.json` (`fenolite.import-provenance.v0`: inputs with SHA-256, upgrades, tools, findings, self-check) and copies of `--licence-file`; every `.py` file starts with the SPDX, copyright and `Converted from …` lines (`--licence`, `--copyright`, `--source`, `--revision`). Before anything is planned the code is built in a private folder and compared with the source at equivalence levels 1 to 5 (new profile `import`) and copper item by item (`import.unequal`, exit 5). An item the DSL cannot express refuses the import (exit 7, `FEN-7001`) unless `--allow-lossy`, each kind reported (`import.item-unexpressed`; the kinds of c0277 stay lossy until it is merged). A board below the read floor is upgraded by `kicad-cli pcb upgrade` on a private copy (`--no-upgrade` refuses it). Ten `import.*` codes, result schema `fenolite.import.v0`, user guide `docs/import.md`, the authored example `examples/import_python/` and a residue test for converted code. The DSL gains `Design.track_table`, `arc_table` and `via_table` (rows of integer nanometres; row `i` keeps the copper uuid of item `i` of its key) and `key=` for unnamed zones and rule areas; `fenolite.library.derive_from_model` derives one library per nickname from a board read once. `--adopt` also writes `adopted/<name>.kicad_pcb`, the source board stamped by token edits with the uuids and `fenolite.path` a build of the code derives (twins found in the built board), so a build over it matches every footprint and changes nothing (KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 load it with the source's DRC result). An Altium `.PrjPcb` or `.PcbDoc` is read by Fenolite's reader, converted in memory and verified, then emitted alike. A hierarchy emits `sheets/*.py` factories from actual source-sheet files, one function reused for repeated instances with explicit refs and physical placements; nets are created in the deepest sheet containing their pins and passed across boundaries as keywords. Two fixes found on the way: `fenolite library` (and the derived libraries of a conversion) now turns back a pad, property or text angle of 0 that a board omits in a turned footprint, and a design script's sibling module (`copper.py`) is no longer shadowed by a module of the same name loaded before the build. The KiCad 10.0.6 demo boards that are not heavy import equivalent (`docs/evidence/import-corpus.md`). The agent guide's generated pages may now hold 800 lines.
- **Library data format** (c0259): the shipped catalog moves from Python tables to TOML records under `src/fenolite/catalog/data/`, byte for byte (every definition's canonical JSON and every build output of the catalog examples are unchanged, proved by `tests/data/catalog/golden.json`). Four record kinds share one header (`id`, `version`, `summary`, `licence`, `evidence`, `sources`, optional `reviewed_by`, `origin`, `replaces`, `deprecated`): `fenolite.pins.v0`, `fenolite.package.v0`, `fenolite.device.v1` (c0248's `v0` with the header and `[[part]]` tables) and `fenolite.part.v0`. `record_uid` is a UUIDv5 of kind and id; versions follow the interface rule (an interface change is a new id with `replaces`), recorded in `catalog/data/VERSIONS.toml`. A part inherits its device and sets only orderable facts; `design.part(device="<id>@<mpn>")` places it. The project folder `devices/` is read recursively and holds all four kinds. `catalog list` and `catalog show` add `version`, `licence` and `uid`. New codes `library.deprecated`, `library.review-stale` and `library.generator-missing`; user guide `docs/library-records.md`, example `examples/catalog/` and the record tree of `examples/devices/devices/`. Shipped authored records use CC0-1.0; `fenolite fmt` rewrites v0 device records as v1 while preserving their devices and refuses unrepresentable comments.
- **Symbols from pin tables** (c0261): a pin table (`fenolite.pins.v0` rows of `number`, `name`, `type`, optional `group`, `side`, `bus`, `unit`, `shape`, `hidden`, `stacked`, `alternates`, a `[symbol]` table and `[[variant]]` rows) gives a symbol through a deterministic layout rule (`fenolite.catalog.generate.symbol`): supply on top, ground at the bottom, inputs left, outputs right, buses together, every connection point on the 2.54 mm grid, an automatic split into units above 100 pins. New model value objects `PinTable`, `PinRow` and `PinVariant`; `SymbolGraphic` gains `unit` and `body_style`; `flatten_symbol` resolves an `extends` chain. The DSL gains `Symbol.from_pins`, `Symbol.derive`, `Symbol.unit` and `Symbol.pin(..., unit=, hidden=, alternates=)`. The KiCad symbol writer writes units, unit names, hidden and stacked pins, alternates and `extends`; the Altium SchLib writer writes per-unit bodies and derived symbols flat. `fenolite library symbol PINS... --out FILE.kicad_sym|FILE.SchLib` plans the generated library (the first positional of `fenolite library` is now an action word or the board path). Codes `symbol.units-split`, `symbol.bus-split` and `symbol.bus-gap`; user guide `docs/symbols.md` and example `examples/symbols/`. Devices naming a pin table now get a separate generated drawing with their package properties; explicit and imported drawings keep precedence, and authored evidence and provenance stay unchanged.
- **Parametric part selection** (c0254): `resistor(...)`, `capacitor(...)` and `inductor(...)` describe a passive by value (a quantity, or a range the row's worst-case span must lie in), tolerance, ratings, case, series and dielectric; `design.part(device=query)` and `b.part(name, device=query)` make a query part (pins `1` and `2` until the pick); `design.defaults(...)` and `b.defaults(...)` cascade design → block → call. The build picks each query from a local, versioned catalog (shipped `fenolite.family.v0` families: E24 5 % and E96 1 % chip resistors, C0G and X7R 50 V chip capacitors in 0402 to 1206, every number with a public source, no MPN; and project rows in `devices/`: families, or device records with the new optional keys `kind`, `case`, `tolerance`, `dielectric`), ranks the matches deterministically and records each pick in the `[picks]` section of the design lock through the plan; a pick never changes by itself (`pick.stale`, `pick.row-missing`, `pick.no-match`, `pick.row-changed`, `pick.query-changed`, `pick.new`, `pick.attribute-unknown`, `pick.row-unverified`). `fenolite pick PATH` lists the picks and their state, ranks candidates (`--candidates`), re-picks through the plan (`--update`, `--update-all`) and writes `UNVERIFIED` proposals under `devices/proposed/` from a CSV or JSON file or a `fenolite.suppliers` plugin (`--allow-offsite`; none shipped, no network in the core). Generators `voltage_divider`, `rc_lowpass`, `led_resistor`, `i2c_pull_ups` and `shunt` choose values with exact arithmetic and rational enclosures, and report `gen.achieved` or `gen.unachievable`. `fenolite catalog list --kind family`; `fenolite.catalog.CATALOG_VERSION`; `docs/picking.md`; `examples/picks/`.
- **Passive families from ordering codes** (c0263): a grammar family (`fenolite.family.v1`, TOML) describes a whole manufacturer series once: its `pattern` of literals and `{field}` placeholders, the codes of each field with closed attributes (the c0254 constraint keys, `footprint`, `symbol`, `packing`; `ordering` fields with a `prefer` code), one value field in `eia3`, `eia4`, `eia96` or `rkm` (exact integers, one canonical spelling, `docs/formats/library/value-codes.md`), a validity matrix of `[[valid]]` and `[[exclude]]` rows, an optional opaque tail and the record keys of c0259. `fenolite.pick.grammar` lints it statically (prefix-free codes, so every MPN parses one way), expands it lazily in canonical order with per-field pruning by a query (refused above 100 000 combinations), encodes a query into ranked exact MPNs and decodes an MPN back into its parameters. A grammar family in `devices/` is a pick row source: a query picks `<family id>@<MPN>` with its manufacturer, recorded in the lock with the SHA-256 of the candidate's canonical text. New read-only command `fenolite family list|show|lint|decode|encode|expand` and the codes `family.*`; illustrative grammars of an invented manufacturer in `examples/families/`; the core ships no real series.
- **Electrical contracts** (c0253): the stage `circuit.rules` judges each device part's `abs_max` ratings (worst case, derated by the script's `design.derating(capacitor_voltage=ratio(50), ...)` policy; Fenolite ships no derating number) and `operating` ranges (exact) against the voltages and currents stated by the test rig (`design.rig(supply(...), sink(...))`, a record that reaches no output), the rails and the declared or confirmed roles: the voltage across two-pin parts, pin voltages from the device's ground function, `[[limits]]` pin pairs, device current by class, resistor power (V²/R, I²R) and temperature against `design.operating(ambient=...)`; every row that reads a rig supply is also judged at its corners. `design.expect(...)` and `b.expect(...)` record `within`/`at_least`/`at_most` tests over `value_of`, `rating_of`, `voltage_of` and `current_of`, judged pass, unsure or fail on every check; `design.contracts(unknown="warning")` makes each unjudged row a warning. Thirteen `circuit.*` codes; `fenolite build`, `check` and `roles` resolve the device records and the classes of `roles.classify` and inject them. One requirement table (`fenolite.core.requirement_table`) with value, limit, source and status: `summary.requirements` of `circuit.rules` and `roles.rules`, and `result.requirements` of `fenolite roles --check`. `requirements.toml` takes unit keys (`clearance = "1.2mm"`, `current = "2A"`, `temp_rise = "10K"`) beside the raw ones, and a `source` text; `core.units.parse_area`. Example `examples/circuit/sensor/`; guide `docs/contracts.md`.
- **Design testing** (c0256): `fenolite.testing` (standard library only, experimental) builds a design script privately through the reviewed plan and confirm protocol and asserts on it in ordinary tests: `load(path, *, seed, timestamp, kicad_version, workdir)` returns a `TestDesign` (a context manager that removes its private folder) with `net()`, `ref()` (also by block path, such as `dut/U1`), `connected(a, b, copper=False)` and `findings(pattern, *, stages, severity)`, which runs the tool-free check stages, `circuit.rules` included, and refuses an oracle stage before anything runs; `instantiate(block, **params)` builds one c0251 block alone as `dut`, its ports read as the nets `dut/<port>`; `assert_render` compares a canonical SVG golden byte for byte and prints a bounded diff and the reviewed regeneration commands. `fenolite render --net NAME` (repeatable) highlights nets on a whole-board model render and `--layers` keeps some copper layers; `render` takes a design script as `PATH` with `--net` or `--region` and builds it in memory with no tool. `check` replies carry `result.subjects`: for each issue index the model entities (`kind`, `id`, `name`) it names, given by `copper.clearance`, `length.rules`, `placement.rules`, `roles.rules` and `circuit.rules` (a requirement row's part and nets), and the viewer resolves findings by them. A Part, Net or Interface that the script created and that never reached the design gives the build warning `build.unattached` at its `file:line`. The command reference of the agent guide is split into bounded pages (`commands-1` to `commands-5`); user guide `docs/testing.md`, example `examples/testing/`, hypothesis `H-G-RENDER-DETERMINISTIC`.
- **Footprint generators** (c0260): `fenolite.catalog.footprints.generate` draws a footprint from a package record (`fenolite.package.v0` with a `family`: `chip`, `molded`, `gullwing`, `nolead`, `grid`, `to`, `dip`, `header`, `connector`) whose dimensions are exact length ranges (`LengthRange` in `fenolite.core.quantities`); the manufacturer's `[land]` wins, else a worst-case land calculator with Fenolite-authored, versioned constants `lands/1` back-solved from 27 cited manufacturer lands (`docs/formats/library/lands.md`, `INFERRED`, no IPC table); versioned drawing conventions (`drawing/0` reproduces the 0.6 catalog, `drawing/1` adds rounded pads, a chamfered fabrication outline, silkscreen clear of copper and pin-1 marks); extruded bodies from the same numbers and a STEP writer of Fenolite's own (`fenolite.backends.step`, AP214, stdlib only); `Footprint.from_package(text)` in scripts, whose build writes `lib/<library>.3dshapes/<name>.step` beside the footprint; `fenolite library generate PACKAGE... --out DIR` plans the `.kicad_mod`, `.step` and one Altium `.PcbLib` (`--no-step`, `--no-pcblib`, `--positions`); `package.*` findings; a naming rule for generic packages (`docs/formats/library/naming.md`); `examples/generated/` and `docs/footprint-generators.md`. The `.kicad_mod` writer now writes the corner ratio and the 3D model path of authored footprints, and an Altium build takes the corner ratio of a rounded pad from the model (`Pad.corner_ratio`), so generated footprints reach the PCB library and document. Catalog footprints keep their bytes (`tests/data/catalog/footprints.sha256`); they gain bodies in `.fenolite/board.json` only when a later version of their record gives heights. Rooted output folders are rejected on Windows as well as drive-qualified absolute paths.
- **Block records** (c0268): `@block(id=, version=, summary=, category=, licence=, evidence=, sources=, replaces=, deprecated=)` gives a block a record in the code that defines it; `param(default, *, doc=, unit=, min=, max=, choices=)` documents a parameter, and `use` refuses a value of another unit, outside its limits or not a choice before the function runs; `b.port(..., doc=)` documents a port. `fenolite.dsl.describe(block)` reads the record without a build (canonical JSON `fenolite.block.v0` with record, definition and interface digests). A recorded block is named by its id; the model `Module` gains `block_version`, and the design lock the section `[blocks]` (`<id>@<version>` per instance): another version stops the build with `lock.block-version` (error, nothing written), and `lock.block-replaced`, `lock.block-released` and `block.deprecated` report the rest. `fenolite blocks list|show|doc|where` reads the catalog (the empty package `fenolite.blocks` with its `VERSIONS.toml`, and `--from FILE.py`), and `fenolite inspect FILE --blocks` lists the block versions of a lock or a `circuit.json`. Designs without a recorded block keep their bytes. Fixture loaders leave authored block inputs free of bytecode caches during validation.
- **DSL board coverage, part 1** (c0277): teardrop settings of vias: `teardrop(...)` (ratios in parts per million) and `teardrop=` of `design.via()` and `design.via_table()`, written as the via's `teardrops` child for KiCad 9 and 10 and replacing the child on a rebuild; `Teardrop` on `Pad` and `Via` of the model, left out of the canonical JSON when unset. A via table takes a blind via over both outer layers, as KiCad writes them. `fenolite import --to python` keeps the teardrop settings of vias (`teardrop=` of their tables), writes such blind vias instead of leaving them out (`via-span`), and reports the `padvia` teardrop zones KiCad draws again as `import.teardrops-regenerated` (info) instead of unexpressed items. A byte baseline of every example (`tests/data/build_bytes.json`) guards the existing builds. Board texts may sit on a copper layer (`copper.text-unchecked`, info: KiCad's DRC judges them) and take `knockout`, `bold`, `italic`, `face`, a `(width, height)` size, several lines or no character; `dimension()` takes `extension_offset` and a `(width, height)` size. The import writes those texts and the aligned and orthogonal dimensions (`dimension-style` for a format the DSL does not say), and the Altium writer reports the new text styles instead of dropping them. `Part(..., dnp=True)` marks a part not populated (`(dnp yes)` on its symbol, the `dnp` attribute on its footprint), and the import writes it; a net tie comes back with its vendored footprint, so neither is unexpressed any more. `design.group(name, *members, locked=False, key=None)` groups parts, drawings, zones, rule areas and earlier groups (`Group` of `Board.groups`, id prefix `grp`), written after the zones for KiCad 9 and 10 and replaced on a rebuild; an Altium build reports each group (`altium.not-lowered`); the import writes the groups whose members it writes (`group` for the others). `Part(..., board_only=True)` is a part on the board only (KiCad's `board_only` attribute, no schematic symbol, a ref that may repeat, be empty or read `REF**`); `compare_designs` pairs references both sides hold equally often by placement (`REF#1`, `REF#2`, …), so the import writes footprints that share a reference (`import.board-only`, info). `design.user_layers(count, names=)` declares `User.1` … `User.<n>` for drawings; graphics take a copper layer and `net=` (the copper check judges them) and `width=None` for a filled shape without an outline; `via()` and `via_table()` take `free=True`; `zone()` takes `smoothing=` and `smoothing_radius=`; dimensions take `units="mils"` and `"auto"`; `stack.silkscreen()` takes `material=`. The import writes all of them. `design.verbatim(key, text, target="kicad_pcb"|"kicad_dru")` and `part.verbatim(key, text)` keep an item as KiCad text: the build checks it (`build.verbatim-invalid`, `-head`, `-layer`, `-net`; `build.verbatim-item`, info), gives its uuids of its own, writes its nets in the target's form and gates its tokens as a read board's; an Altium build reports it (`altium.verbatim-skipped`). The import writes the root items no call says (a `generated` tuning pattern, an image…) as verbatim items, their `members` following the copper tables. `rule_area(..., placement="/CH1/")` writes a placement rule area of KiCad's multichannel tool, and area names may hold a slash; a chamfered pad of a bottom footprint is mirrored instead of refused. `zone(outline=…)` takes `arc_to` steps (`Zone.arcs`), written as `arc` items of the outline, polygonised by the copper check and written as segments for Altium; the import writes zone outlines with arcs. `dimension(..., text_at=)` places a dimension's text by hand. A board outline edge that a footprint's `Edge.Cuts` draws is not written again, so the import declares an outline a USB connector closes. The equivalence check counts an `unconnected-(…)` net of one pad number, two pads of a pin header included, as a pad on no net. `board(copper=…)` takes every even count from 2 to 32 (KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 load, export and re-save each table; `In30.Cu` is 62), with planes, zones, script copper, exports and the Specctra hand-off on every layer; `copper=1` stays refused, with the hint that a single-sided board is `copper=2` with nothing on `B.Cu`, because KiCad refuses a table of one copper layer (`H-K-PCB-ONE-COPPER`). An Altium build of more than 16 signal layers (the count less its planes) or 16 planes gives `altium.stack-signal-limit` (error) instead of the default stack of the two outer layers, and a document of twenty layers with four planes is written and read back. `design.radial_dimension()`, `design.leader()` and `design.center_mark()` declare the dimension kinds `radial`, `leader` and `center` (`Dimension.leader_length`, `text`, `frame`), written in the forms of KiCad's demo boards and read back as modelled dimensions; the import writes them. `design.teardrops(where=…)` writes teardrop settings on every pad and via a selection matches (`build.teardrops-overlap`, info, when two calls select one item). `design.image(key, file, at, layer=, scale=)` puts a PNG picture on the board: the build reads the file (an input of the plan; `build.image-missing`, `build.image-format`), the model holds an `Image` with the base64 data (`Board.images`), the KiCad writer and reader handle the root `image`, an Altium build reports it (`altium.image-skipped`), and the import writes `design.image()` with the PNG beside the code. `Footprint.pad()` takes shaped pads: `shape="trapezoid"` with `delta`, `chamfer` with `corners`, `radius_ratio`, and `shape="custom"` with `anchor` and `primitives` made by `poly()`, `segment()`, `circle()` and `arc()` of `fenolite.dsl.footprint` (`Pad.trapezoid_delta`, `chamfer_ratio`, `chamfer_corners`, `anchor_shape`, `primitives`, which a KiCad reader projects from a read pad). `Part.field()` takes `bold`, `italic` and `face` (`FootprintField.bold`, `italic`, `face`), written into the field's font. `fenolite verify-source` builds with the copper guard warning instead of refusing, and level 6 retains the integrated comparison by layer name, kind and relative order instead of comparing shifted ordinals. `Part.override()` sets the footprint-level clearance, margins, paste ratio, zone connection and thermal relief of one instance (`FootprintInstance.overrides`), and `Part.edit_pad()` changes the pads of one instance (`build.pad-unknown` for a number the footprint lacks). `Part.model(name, offset=, rotation=, scale=, hidden=)` names the 3D models of a part's footprint instance (`FootprintInstance.models`, `ModelRef`), written in place of the footprint's own; the name is never read. Current import-emission observations now cover all 18 declared demo boards with input and measurement hashes, while historical full-conversion evidence stays unchanged and CI still builds and checks equivalence independently. Refresh the entry inventory/source notes and prove the unchanged byte baseline while keeping the full and native gates open.

- **Net roles, phase 4: route proposals** (c0280): `fenolite route --router octilinear --propose` lists every
  open connection left after the power copper and the critical routes with candidate shapes and their facts,
  without a score; `--accept ID` locks a candidate through a reviewed plan, `--reject ID` remembers a refusal,
  `--replay` restores locked decisions (c0280). Subjects (`fanout`, `join`, `role-net`, `group`, `connection`,
  `fenolite.roles.proposals`) come from the open connections on the pour, taken along a role net's power pads,
  and from the groups phase 2 leaves open; the octilinear router answers `RoutingJob.subjects` with
  `RoutingResult.candidates` (feature `propose`; families `shape`, `z-shape`, `via-l`, `fanout`, `role` with one
  relaxation at a time). Each candidate's facts: connections closed, open connections before and after, new
  copper-check and role findings, vias, length, layers, bends, loop, clashes, fill and what was not measured;
  with `--gate drc`, KiCad's DRC in batches. Decisions live in `fenolite.route-decisions.v0`
  (`.fenolite/route-decisions.json`); `--forget ID` drops one. Eight `route.proposal-*` codes. A role-mode entry
  left open names `propose`. `fenolite view --proposals run|FILE` joins a reply to the board
  (`fenolite.view_proposals.v0`), a served page may run `proposals` and plan `route-accept` and
  `route-reject`. On the six role-copper reference variants the accepted proposals leave no open connection.
- **Net roles in the viewer** (c0305; the role view of c0245-engineering-viewer, brought into this viewer by
  the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-29): `fenolite view BOARD --roles run` runs `fenolite roles BOARD
  --check` read-only (or `--roles FILE` reads its reply) and joins it to the board as `scene.roles`
  (`fenolite.view_roles.v0`, `fenolite.view.roles`); `result.roles` counts roles and findings, and the reply's
  evidence is the lower of the board's and the roles'. A Net roles panel lists a legend of the role kinds with
  colours and glyphs, each net's role, the hot loops, gate loops and Kelvin pairs and the role findings; the
  Layers tab gets role colours of the copper, hatched loops with their measured area, dashed Kelvin sense lines
  and role markers; the inspector shows a Role section after a net's Net section. `check_overlay` places
  `roles.*` findings (stage `roles.rules`) on the group or `group:pad` they name. A served page runs `roles`
  (`roles --check`, no option) and, with `--allow-actions`, plans Confirm roles (`roles --accept`,
  `--accept-group`, only names its role data shows as inferred) through the plan, confirm and undo of c0303.
- **Checks run from the viewer** (c0302): a page served by `fenolite view --serve` can run `check`, `analyze`, `ready` and `equivalent` itself, read-only, one job at a time. Each Project review card whose stages the server can run gets a **Run** control, and **Run from this page** picks the stages, the analyze kinds, a readiness check or a comparison with another document. A strip shows the command, its progress, the elapsed time and **Cancel**; a cancelled or timed-out run changes nothing. A `check` run replaces, stage by stage, the stages of the same name (`fenolite.view.project_review.merge`), each stage keeping its own input hash and evidence, so a stage of an older revision reads Limited; `analyze` paths become finding groups, `ready` and `equivalent` findings join the review list with Locate. Server routes `GET /runs`, `POST /run`, `GET /run/<job>`, `POST /run/<job>/cancel` behind the guard of `POST /marks` (`Host`, `Origin`, `X-Fenolite-Run: 1`, JSON, 16 KiB); the allow-list `fenolite.view.runs` takes a command name, a document id and typed options, never a command line or a path. Each run is a child process through the runner core shared with `fenolite mcp`, now `fenolite.cli.childrun`. New options `--run-timeout SECONDS` (default 600) and `--no-run`; `result.serve.runs` counts the runs by final state. `docs/viewer.md`, "Running checks from the page".
- **Writing actions from the viewer** (c0303): `fenolite view --serve --allow-actions` lets the page fill zones, tidy copper (with `--gate drc`), place designators or clip silkscreen, place test points, waive a KiCad DRC finding with a reason (`check --exclude`), move or rotate a part (`place --move`), shift or turn parts with their copper, and export fabrication files or the BOM under the project. Each action is a dry run first: a plan dialog lists the files (sizes, new and replaced SHA-256), the issues and the evidence, and draws the staged board's changes over the current one with the revision compare; Confirm writes exactly that plan (`--confirm --plan`), a changed file asks for a new review (`FEN-4002`), and the receipt follows, kept in `view-receipts/` of the state folder so that Undo plans `fenolite restore` on it. Routes `GET /actions`, `POST /action/plan`, `/action/confirm`, `/action/undo`, behind the run guard with `X-Fenolite-Action: 1` and a per-session token; the allow-list `fenolite.view.actions` fills fixed templates with typed options, confines outputs to the project root and writes no Altium document. `result.serve.writes` lists the session's receipts. `docs/viewer.md`, "Actions: writing from the page".
- **A professional shell for the viewer** (c0300): one set of interface tokens (spacing, type, radii,
  surfaces, text, accent, focus, severity) with a dark and a light theme that follows the system until the user
  picks one (`fenolite.view.theme`), each text colour at 4.5:1 or more on its surface; a document keeps its own
  colours. The toolbar is grouped as View (2D/3D, Fit, Top/Bottom with the far side's opacity), Tools
  (Measure with Point and Edge gap, Mark, mm/mil) and Share (Copy link, Export: SVG and Print at a scale), each
  button with an icon drawn for Fenolite, an accessible name and a tooltip with its shortcut; the duplicate
  "Fit" and "Clear" controls are gone. One command registry (`viewer/src/core/commands.ts`) feeds the toolbar,
  the shortcuts, the shortcut sheet (`?`) and a command palette (`Ctrl+K`, `Cmd+K` on macOS) that also jumps to
  parts, nets, documents and library items (`>` commands, `@` parts, `#` nets). The side bar opens with the
  Project review and its card states, then one remembered disclosure per panel with a count; Components and
  Nets sort (reference in natural order, value, footprint; name, pads, routed length) and finding groups at 0
  fold into one line. The status bar shows the cursor, the grid, the zoom, the layer, the selection, the unit
  and the live state; the SHA-256, size, backend and evidence moved to "About this document". Loading shows a
  progress bar, a failed read offers Retry, empty lists say why. Run checks of c0302 is in the toolbar and the
  palette. The visual tests run in the dark interface theme, `kicad_board_light.png` covers the light one, and
  the references were regenerated and re-reviewed byte-identically with the exact CI browser. Documentation proof now names the actual tested control modules. `docs/viewer.md`, "The page".
- **The viewer's inspector, Layers panel and 3D controls** (c0301): a dock at the right of the drawing, never over
  it, with a Properties tab (the inspector) and, on boards and footprints, a Layers tab that the 3D panel replaces
  in 3D; `I` hides it, its edge resizes it (240 to 480 px), and at 760 px and below it is a bottom sheet that a
  selection opens and `Esc` closes. The inspector shows the selection in sections (Part, Pad, Track, Arc, Via,
  Zone, Net, Keep-out, Finding; sheets, libraries and drawings alike), each value in the page's unit with a copy
  control, "Copy all", values the file does not hold left out, and the document's summary when nothing is
  selected; its actions are registry commands also listed in the palette: Zoom to, Highlight net, Select part,
  Find similar, Show in schematic or Show on board, Open in review, and with `--allow-actions` Move part and
  Waive finding (c0303). A selected net, part or filter result is drawn in its own colours with an outline in the
  `--emphasis` token instead of being lightened, its zone fills lighter and outlined, the rest dimmed at 22 %.
  The Layers panel lists every layer by its full name in groups with visibility, opacity, the current layer and
  the presets, so the layer tabs leave the bottom bar of boards (sheets keep their document tabs). In 3D the
  2D-only controls are hidden, a view cube sets the six face views and the corner views (animated), the light is
  an ambient, a key and a fill term, extruded bodies take a charcoal body colour with a lighter top, and the 3D
  panel shows or hides bodies, board, silkscreen and copper. New visual tests (`test_inspector.py`,
  `test_layers.py`, `test_3d.py`) and the reference `kicad_board_net.png`; the references were regenerated with
  the dock. Their eleven-image local re-review changes no baseline; the final batch CI remains required. `docs/viewer.md`, "The page" and "Units and measurement".
- **Waive and stale results in the viewer** (c0303, two fixes found in testing): Waive is not offered on a
  finding of a check of an earlier revision of the file, and the page says why; after an action writes a project
  file the checks read (a waiver writes the `.kicad_pro`), the document's review results are marked stale until a
  check runs again, and the run strip, the Project review and the inspector offer Run again.

### Changed

- **The viewer page is built from TypeScript modules** (c0304; the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-29, before
  the redesign of c0300 and c0301): the old single `src/fenolite/view/page.html` (188 KB of inline CSS, markup
  and vanilla JavaScript) is now about fifty modules under `viewer/src/` (`core`, `board`, `measure`, `three`,
  `sch`, `panels`, `dialogs`, `table`, `library`, `export`), type-checked by the TypeScript compiler and linked
  by `viewer/build.mjs` into one script whose top-level names stay page globals; the board, sheet and WebGL
  renderers are moved, not rewritten. Preact (MIT, bundled, listed in `NOTICE`) renders the Changes, Messages,
  Findings and Navigator panels. The build writes `src/fenolite/view/static/` (`page.html`, `viewer.js`,
  `viewer.css`, `manifest.json`), which is committed and inlined by `fenolite.view.export.template()`, so the
  served page and `--export` stay one offline file and installing Fenolite needs no Node. `make viewer`
  rebuilds (Node 22, `tools/build_viewer.py`), `make viewer-check` fails on a stale bundle and runs in the
  `viewer` CI job, and `tests/unit/view/test_viewer_bundle.py` checks the recorded digests without Node.
  Nothing changes on screen: the page's unit tests and the 69 visual tests with their references pass unchanged.
  The page code of c0302 and c0303 lives in `review/runs.ts`, `panels/run-strip.tsx` and the Run controls of
  `panels/project-review.ts`, and in `actions/actions.ts`, `dialogs/plan-review.tsx` and `panels/receipts.tsx`.
- **The viewer changes of v0.7 renumbered** (2026-10-29): written as c0243 to c0247 (and c0255 for
  `viewer-roles`), renumbered c0250 to c0255 (which freed c0243 to c0245 for another session's changes), then
  c0268 to c0273, they are now c0300 (`viewer-shell`), c0301 (`viewer-inspector`), c0302 (`viewer-run-checks`),
  c0303 (`viewer-actions`), c0304 (`viewer-build`) and c0305 (`viewer-roles`): on 2026-10-29 the range c0300 to
  c0305 was reserved for the viewer, away from the c0246 to c0279 of the maintainer's `circuits-as-code` line
  (which continues from c0280 in its own line). New changes on this branch start at c0306.

### Fixed

- **Exact copper arcs and bottom chamfers** (c0282): copper pairs, rule areas and curved board edges use exact circular distances instead of polygonisation bands, removing arc-band false clearance findings near limits while preserving the injected tolerance. Copper graphic arcs retain the same exact cores. Engineering analyses retain their bounded arc polylines. Bottom placement and derivation mirror chamfer corner names and retain their ratios; builds using this new mirror carry inferred evidence pending KiCad oracle confirmation.
- **KiCad-job tests after c0250, c0252, c0253 and c0269**: the expected `fenolite check` stage lists of `test_canary` and `test_built_blink_before_routing` hold `circuit.rules` right after `model.validate`; the device build oracle test and the `sensor_rails` read-back accept the info findings `circuit.rating-missing` (shipped devices carry no ratings) and `build.block-port-merged` and nothing else; `tools/catalog/golden.py` leaves out the files later changes added (`LATER`: the base record `fenolite-base.json`) and their entry in the `files` map of `.fenolite/build.json`, whose other bytes still equal the c0259 golden.
- **ERC fact fixture after c0247**: the `pin_not_driven` control of the ERC facts (`tests/kicad/check/_erccases.py`) no longer edits the blink's former `no_connect(...)` line, which c0247 replaced with `u1.unused_no_connect()`; it connects the two input pins and lets the build's unused-pin request leave them unmarked. The recorded probe outcomes do not change.
- **Design lock path on Windows** (c0249): the build plan and its receipt name the design lock with forward slashes on every system (`ex/design.lock`, never `ex\design.lock`).
- **Acceptance rebuild test after c0250**: `test_rebuild_is_the_identity` expects the base record `fenolite-base.json` among the files a first build adds to the recorded v0.1 projects, which predate it; a second build is still the identity.

- **The viewer's Node-run tests on Windows**: the tests that run `node` (`tests/unit/view/`, `tests/_visual.py`)
  decode its output as UTF-8 instead of the locale's code page, so `×` and `²` no longer turn into cp1252
  garbage in `unit (windows-latest, py3.12)`.

## [0.6.0] - 2026-10-29

### Added

- **Net roles, phase 0** (c0222): Fenolite infers what each net of a power board does (`power`, `switch_node`, `ground`, `kelvin`, `gate`, `signal`, `high_voltage`) and its hot loops, gate loops and Kelvin pairs from the circuit, with a part class, a rule and a confidence for each, and judges the copper against them through the analyses of `dev` (`loop_areas`, `analyze_power`, `path_network`, `analyze_necks`, `analyze_split`, `analyze_distances`, `analyze_returns`, `analyze_coupling`, `analyze_ties`), defining no area, section, capacity or gap of its own. `fenolite roles PATH [--check]` shows and checks them, read-only until `--accept`, `--accept-all [--min-confidence]`, `--set NET=KIND[:KEY=N]`, `--reject`, `--accept-group`, `--reject-group` or `--current NET=AMPS` plans the role document `<board stem>.roles.json` (schema `fenolite.roles.v0`) or `.fenolite/rules.json` of a built project; `--check` takes the measuring options of `analyze` and runs no tool. The stage `roles.rules` of `fenolite check` (both pipelines, after `placement.rules`) runs the same judge on a board with stored or declared roles and is skipped with `no-roles` otherwise. A design script declares roles with `design.net_role`, `hot_loop`, `kelvin`, `gate_loop`, `role_limits` and `part_class`, stored as `declared` in `RuleSet.roles`; a rebuild keeps the confirmed ones. Seventeen `roles.*` codes (with `fenolite explain`) and the check code `check.roles-unreadable`; a finding against a default of the role profile (`fenolite.roles.profile`, each default with its source, `docs/roles.md`) is at most a warning, an error only against your limit on a confirmed or declared role. Three reference boards authored for Fenolite (`examples/roles/buck_shunt`, `half_bridge`, `bms`, good and bad) with CC0 parts in `tests/data/libs/Roles.*`; the good variants pass KiCad DRC and give no warning, the bad ones exactly their designed findings, on the KiCad and the Altium build. Six `H-G-ROLE-` hypotheses registered. The agent guide's generated pages may now hold 750 lines.
- **Release 0.6.0: the MCP server and the board engineering tools** (c0242): `fenolite mcp` and `fenolite edit` (c0195, c0196); the board engineering tools of c0175 in their own changes: router input fidelity, ground ties, barrier crossings, return paths, practice checks, pour shapes, region placement, layout reuse and group moves, copper tidy, DRC trials and `--gate drc`, diff gates, region renders, designators and silkscreen clipping, test-point placement, the octilinear router, generated copper, library fidelity, file safety and fabrication read-back (c0197 to c0214, c0223 to c0229); the Python API (c0206); computed Altium pours, experimental (c0215); the release from a tag (c0221); reliability, viewer review, the catalog browser and the public presentation (c0232 to c0240); and the hypotheses and kit Part J of the Altium edit sessions (c0241). The record `docs/release/v0.6.md` gives, change by change, what ships, its proof and its open tasks; the maintainer's tests (the Altium kit runs, the viewer reviews, a KiCad GUI save, the runs of a real agent) are deferred to v0.7, and the v1.0 freeze (c0216 to c0220) is held by his decision of 2026-10-29. Every Altium write kind stays experimental.
- **Altium edit sessions, measured first** (c0241, task 1.1; package 12 of c0175, moved on 2026-10-29): the hypotheses of the edit session (`H-A-EDIT-*`, fourteen rows: what Altium Designer changes in a document when it moves, turns, flips, deletes, copies, places, re-layers or re-rules one object, and what a plain save rewrites) and of the integrity rules (`H-A-INTEG-*`, seven rows: polygon sub-indices, pour indices, unique ids, parameter groups and orphans, the model store, two layer fields) are registered, each `INFERRED` with its kit case, before any write code. Kit Part J (`docs/evidence/altium-edit.md`): phase A, an authored two-layer board (`tests/data/altium_edit/edit.py`) and cases J1 to J22 that the maintainer edits once each and saves in Altium Designer, built outside the repository by `FENOLITE_ALTIUM_PART_J=<folder> uv run pytest tests/unit/backends/altium/test_part_j.py -k phase_a` with `expected.md`, the steps in Brazilian Portuguese and `SHA256SUMS`; phase B (J23 to J33) is described for the documents the session will write and for planted faults. No edit is written yet.
- **Public presentation (c0237):** a workflow-oriented README, first-board tutorial and documentation index; actual viewer screenshots, cross-probing animation, social preview and reviewed launch material. Package metadata describes shipped KiCad/Altium features; experimental limits remain visible.
- **Designators placed on the silkscreen** (c0212): `fenolite silk BOARD --designators [--keep] [--refs GLOB] [--sizes LENGTHS] [--angles 0,90] [--clearance LENGTH] [--gap LENGTH] [--measure auto|kicad|estimate] [--drc]` puts the reference of every part beside its courtyard, clear of pad copper, holes, silkscreen, other texts, other parts' courtyards and the board edge, at 0° or 90°, mirrored on the bottom; a row or a column of equal parts shares one place, so their texts line up. The ink of each text is measured with `kicad-cli` (one scratch board, cached per tool version, `fenolite.silk.extents`, `backends.kicad.textink`), or estimated from a glyph table that `tools/measure_glyphs.py` measured (`silk.estimated`). Every written designator is checked again (`silk.check-failed`), `--drc` has KiCad judge the board before and after (`KICAD-VERIFIED`; `H-K-SILK-DRC`, `H-K-SILK-INK`, `H-K-SILK-ESTIMATE`), and a part without room keeps its text (`silk.no-place`). Facts in `docs/formats/kicad/text.md`, user guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Designators". The generated page `commands` of the agent guide may now hold 700 lines, and the skill folder 300 000 bytes. Silkscreen clipping (c0226) and test-point placement (c0229) followed as changes of their own.
- **Silkscreen clipped at the mask openings** (c0226): `fenolite silk BOARD --clip [--margin LENGTH]` cuts every silkscreen line, arc, circle and rectangle or polygon outline of the board and of its footprints where its ink comes near a pad's mask opening (its copper grown by its mask expansion) or a pad's hole, keeping the margin (default: the project's `silk_clearance` rule, else 0) and writing the rest as lines and arcs; filled shapes and texts are not cut (`silk.clip-skipped`). Each piece is checked again (`silk.check-failed`), and `--drc` has KiCad judge the board (`H-K-SILK-CLIP`). A footprint's own outline is cut alike on every instance, so `fenolite library --out` makes one footprint of them; each changed footprint is named (`silk.footprint-changed`) with the `fenolite library` line in `result.library`, and a cut at another part's pads is `silk.cut-by-other`. `--clip` and `--designators` run together, the clip first. Measured first: KiCad's `silk_over_copper` measures to the pad's copper, only for pads with a mask opening, with the limit of a `silk_clearance` rule and not `min_silk_clearance` (`H-K-SILK-MASK`; `docs/formats/kicad/drc.md`, "Silkscreen over pads"). User guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Clipping".
- **Test points placed** (c0229): `fenolite testpoints BOARD --place --side top|bottom [--grid LENGTH] [--reach LENGTH] [--size LENGTH] [--nets GLOB]... [--drc]` adds a probe of `design.test_point` and one straight track for each net the side does not reach, on the nearest grid site near the net's copper that keeps the clearance from other copper, holes and earlier probes, the edge clearance, and stays off courtyards, silkscreen and forbidding rule areas, the probes `--min-pitch` apart. Fenolite's copper check judges the board before it is written (`testpoint.check-failed`); `--drc` has KiCad judge it (`testpoint.drc-new`, `H-K-TP-PLACE`; the library-table type `lib_footprint_issues` aside). The probes are board-only footprints, so a rebuild keeps them and their tracks (`testpoint.board-only`); a net without room is `testpoint.no-site`. `result.place` holds the coverage before and after. `fenolite.silk.board.silkscreen_ink` gives a side's silkscreen as shapes. User guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Placing test points".
- **Group moves, pads paired by net, and zones of a reused block** (c0227): `fenolite place --turn REFS --about X,Y --angle DEGREES` and `--shift REFS --by DX,DY` (the strategy `group`) move a module and its satellites as one rigid piece: each part's angle comes from the move composed with its placement, so bottom parts turn right, and the tracks, vias and zones that join only the group's pads move with it. Every moved pad is measured and the copper check compares the board before and after (`place.group-failed`, error; `place.fill-stale`, info). `--pose-search --within LENGTH` ranks the four turns and a grid of shifts by the demand across the group's border, without writing. `place --strategy reuse` now pairs pads that share a number (three `GND` pads) by the nets of their pads (`result.reuse.parts[].by_net`), and with `--copper` copies a zone of the block whose fill joins paired pads only, with its fill, rebuilding the outline of a zone read without one from its fill (`zone-beyond-block`, `zone-unfilled`, `pad-lost`). Evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-GROUP-MOVE`, `H-G-REUSE-ZONE`, `H-K-GROUP-DRC`); `docs/placement.md`, "Group moves".
- **Octilinear router** (c0228): `fenolite route BOARD --router octilinear` is a built-in router of short connections, such as gate loops and sense pairs, with no tool and no via. For each open connection (a minimum spanning tree over the pads of a net, pads joined by its copper counting as one) it tries a straight segment, 45° then straight, straight then 45° and the two L shapes on every allowed layer, and keeps the first that keeps the clearance in force from other nets' copper and holes and the edge clearance and makes no acute joint. `--router-option max-length=LENGTH` (20 mm) leaves longer connections open (`route.unrouted`); `--router-option rounds=N` (3) routes the failed connections first again. A differential pair of two pads per net is routed as two tracks at the pair gap (`route.pair-not-routed` otherwise). `route` now gives every router the clearance in force and the copper check of the project's rules in the job's `extra`, and the router removes a connection the check refuses (`route.check-refused`). Router feature `short` in `fenolite capabilities`. Evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-OCTI-CHECK`, `H-K-OCTI-DRC`; `KICAD-VERIFIED` with `--gate drc`); `docs/routing.md`, "octilinear".
- **Pour shapes and the split of the current** (c0213): `fenolite analyze` gains three opt-in kinds on the raster of the zones in their copper states. `necks` finds the places where a pour narrows below the `min_width_nm` of a `[[neck]]` row (a neck between wider copper, a long thin tip, a sliver), each an `analysis.neck-narrow` error. `loops` measures the area of the closed path through the pads of each `[[loop]]` row, along copper where the pads share a net, judged by `max_area_nm2` (`analysis.loop-area`), and `--loop-baseline FILE` gives its change against another board. `balance` gives the copper at each pad of every two-pad SMD part and their ratio, for tombstoning. `--kinds power --split` solves each power path on the raster and gives every track, fill and via its share of the current, with the resistance checked against the interval of `power`.
