A STEP gets the 3D models that Fenolite locates, reported with their source and SHA-256 in `result.models`; a missing model is a warning. A schematic whose hierarchy names a sheet that cannot be given to the run is refused (`export.sheet-missing`), and a board that does not fit its page gives the warning `export.page-too-small`. `result.repeat` says which hashes compare between exports. New command `fenolite models` lists the models of a board and copies them into `3dmodels/` with `--vendor`; `tools/kicad_libs_fetch.py --models` fetches single files of the official 3D library (no fetch was run yet). `LibraryResolver.missing_models` now also finds a model in the project's `3dmodels/` folder, in the install and in a fetched cache, so it can report fewer warnings than before. The document kinds and `fenolite models` carry `INFERRED`: their probes are recorded for `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 and not yet for 9.0.9.
- Board stack-ups (c0101): `read_board` fills `Board.stackup` from a KiCad stack-up that KiCad uses, created boards whose model holds a stack-up get the complete node and a matching board thickness, and scripts declare one with `design.stackup()` and `fenolite.dsl.stack` (the board's stack-up wins over an unlocked script one). `analyze` takes its thicknesses from it, `export` notes a job file that will state KiCad's default, and `inspect` shows it. New warnings name a stack-up that KiCad ignores and a board thickness that differs from its layers. An Altium build writes a stated core or prepreg, passes masks over and names what the document does not hold. A design without a stack-up builds the files it built before. **Changed: a board whose model holds a stack-up and that is created for KiCad (for example a model imported from Altium) now gets the `stackup` node in `setup`, and is refused with `kicad.board.stackup-invalid` when its copper entries are not the board's copper layers.** **Changed: `export --gerbers` of a board without a stack-up reports one info, `export.stackup-default`; an info does not stop the writes of `export`: only an error does (a warning does not either, since c0116).** **Release 0.2.x cannot read a model document that carries the new keys** (`dielectric_kind`, `color`, `impedance_controlled`). Stack-ups are written for 2, 4, 6 and 8 copper layers. `stack.preset(name)` gives the entries of a stack-up that one public fabricator page states, for `design.stackup(*stack.preset(name))`: `two-layer-1.6mm`, `four-layer-1.6mm` and `six-layer-1.6mm` (`stack.PRESETS`; sources S-0720 to S-0722, `INFERRED`), and the guide page `rules` teaches them. The job file, completeness and default rows of the stack-up facts are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, the re-save rows `KICAD-VERIFIED (10.0.x)`, and so is `backends.kicad.stackup.EVIDENCE`.
- `fenolite analyze` gains power paths (`[[path]]`, `--path`, `--kinds power`): the copper on the path, its narrowest sections and via groups, capacities judged where the whole current flows, and a bounded resistance and drop from a resistivity you give; insulation between layers (`--kinds insulation`, `insulation_nm`); creepage that bridges grooves narrower than your groove width (`groove_nm`, `--groove-width`). Creepage and clearance now cross copper of other nets, or of none, at no length and name it, so they can be shorter than before; a judged creepage that passes a groove without a groove width now warns. The capacities and the drop are estimates against your limits, not a simulation, and Fenolite ships no resistivity, groove width or insulation distance. **A reply without the new kinds changes too:** each measure holds `over`, `result.inputs` holds `paths`, `resistivity_pohm_m` and `groove_nm`, the evidence names `H-G-AN-OVER`, and a board with a graphic on a copper layer now gets a warning in the distance analysis. **Not yet usable on a real board:** the insulation between layers and the resistance of a via group need the depths of the stack-up, which the model gets with change c0101; until then the reply says that the stack-up is missing. A requirements file that holds a `[[path]]` row, `insulation_nm` or `groove_nm` is refused by 0.2.x and 0.3.0 (c0115).
- Via protection (c0112): `protect()` and `protection=` on `Design.via`, `via_step` and `Design.stitch`, and `Design.via_protection()` for the board default. KiCad boards read and write tenting, covering, plugging, capping and filling per via and in `setup`; target 9 holds tenting only and refuses the others (exit 7). `inspect` reports `via_protection`. **Changed:** a KiCad 9 board with via tenting, rebuilt for target 10, now keeps the mask that 9.0.9 plots, and a protection set in KiCad on a script via is reported when the build replaces it. Altium: the build writes a via's tenting where the design states it, the import reads the tenting flags into the model (**every imported via gains explicit tenting**), and the four other features are named and not written. A design that states no protection builds the files it built before, for KiCad and for Altium. 0.2.x and 0.3.0 cannot read a `board.json` that carries `protection` or `via_protection`.
- **The agent guide ships in the package** (c0079): `fenolite guide` prints it, `fenolite skill install` copies it to an agent's skill folder or adds a pointer to `AGENTS.md`, and `fenolite init` writes a starter project that builds with the built-in catalog and that the built-in router closes; `capabilities --brief` and `capabilities --command NAME` give a small first reply (6.0 kB for 35 commands, against 21.9 kB) and a command's arguments as data. **`agent/SKILL.md` moved into `src/fenolite/agent/skill/`**, and its loop block now starts from an empty folder: `capabilities --brief`, `init blink`, `build`, `place`, `route --router direct`, `fill`, `check`, `export`, `render`. `inspect` left the block for the page's section on small questions. A command can print a text of its own in text mode (`Result.text`), which is how `fenolite guide start --text` prints a page; the JSON output of every existing command is unchanged, and the default result of `capabilities` gains only the entries of the three new commands. `skill install --agent claude-code` writes to `.claude/skills/fenolite`, and `--dir DIR` serves every other agent. The block is proved on KiCad 10.0.6 for projects of both majors and on KiCad 9.0.9 for target 9 (`H-K-GUIDE-STARTER`, `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, by the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI run 37772583226).
- A yardstick example of about 370 parts (`examples/yardstick`), taken through the loop every night by `tools/yardstick.py` with time and memory budgets per step, and a record of its runs in `docs/evidence/yardstick.md` (c0119). The board is an invented controller of eight identical 48 V buck channels with an isolated high-voltage sense section, on KiCad's official libraries; it is at stage 5 of five: six copper layers with a declared stack-up and inner planes, a board outline with rounded corners, slots under the isolators, plated mounting holes with keep-outs, a high-voltage rule area, the USB pair's rules and impedance target, thermal via arrays, placement rules, a net tie, fiducials, tooling holes and test points (388 parts). The runner also routes it (KiCadRoutingTools for the pair, Freerouting for the rest, with a budget of 3600 s), refills it, judges the routed board against ratchets of open connections and DRC errors, runs the analyses of the high-voltage gap, exports the document kinds and drawings and the test-point report, and reads and round-trips the two heavy demo boards of the corpus. `make yardstick` runs it locally. The job `yardstick` of `nightly.yml` is not a merge gate; its first passing run (37836196244, dispatched on the release branch) reached stage 5 with 459 open connections and 78 other DRC errors on the routed board, within the ratchets, but one run is not three: the budgets of `tools/yardstick_budgets.toml` are provisional, from local runs, and the ratchets are bounds until the first three scheduled runs. Building it found that `Design.stackup()`, `Design.rule_area()` and the board drawings refused a board declared with `board(outline=…)` (fixed, see below), so the example had a rectangular outline until that repair.
- **`fenolite route --timeout` now bounds the whole routing step for every router** (c0109): it bounded each net for KiCadRoutingTools, and 600 stood for the default. `route` keeps the copper of every router run that finished in time and reports the rest with `route.budget-exhausted` (a warning, exit 0; it was `route.tool-failed`, exit 5, and no copper); run `route` again to continue with the nets still open. KiCadRoutingTools routes groups of nets of equal sizes in one process, with `--escalation off` and without `--clearance` (`--router-option group-nets=N` splits a group); a net that does not fit at the requested sizes now stays open instead of being routed narrower. Freerouting is given only the nets of the job (a net outside the job whose class clearance is larger than the default rule stays declared, `route.net-declared`) and runs without its optimizer unless `--router-option optimize=on` (`route.optimizer-cut`), so its routes may hold more vias and detours than before. New `--order GLOB` routes the matching nets first, in tiers; new result keys `budget`, `runs` and `not_attempted`. For plugin authors: `RoutingJob.budget`, `JobNet.tier`, `RouterRun`, `RoutingResult.runs` and `not_attempted`, `routing.budget.Budget`; the constructors of both plugins take `budget` where they took `timeout`, and `write_dsn` takes `others`. No model or schema change.
- **Pads keep KiCad's fabrication marks, scripts place fiducials, test points and tooling holes, and a new command reports them** (c0118). Pads keep KiCad's fabrication marks (fiducial, test point, BGA, heatsink, castellated, mechanical, press-fit) in the model as `Pad.fab_property`, read and written on boards and in footprint files; authored pads take `fab_property=`. New command `testpoints`: test points, fiducials, holes, net coverage and the user's targets (`--min-coverage`, `--min-pitch`, `--min-fiducials`), and a CSV, with `--manifest` its entry of kind `testpoints`. Placement tables mark fiducial rows (field `fiducial`, key `fiducials` of `[placement]`). An Altium build writes a part with a marked pad as before and names the unwritten marks in one `altium.not-lowered` info ("pad properties"), also for library footprints such as BGA packages. `Ipcd356Record` gains `covered`, the mask code of a record. A script places fiducials, test points and tooling holes with `design.fiducial()`, `design.test_point()` and `design.tooling_hole()`, whose footprints and symbols are generated in `Fenolite_Assembly` and whose fiducials and tooling holes get a copper keep-out (`clear_<ref>`, through `rule_area`) and are locked. An Altium build writes test points as parts, leaves fiducials out with one `altium.not-lowered` info ("assembly features"), and writes a tooling hole as a board hole, as every hole of `design.hole()`. Releases 0.2.x and 0.3.0 cannot read a model document that carries `fab_property`. The KiCad facts are recorded for 10.0.6 and 9.0.9.
- `tools/agent_eval`: five authored tasks, a judge that uses `fenolite check` and the expected nets, a log of every `fenolite` call, and a runner that starts a fresh agent on one task in a clean environment. Reference solutions are replayed by the test suites; runs with a real agent are started by hand and recorded in `docs/evidence/agent-eval.md` (c0081). Nothing of it is in the package and no command changes. `make agent-eval TASK=<name> RUNNER=replay` plays a task's solution without any agent. A real agent costs money: the runner refuses one while `CI` is set, and without `--yes` it prints the budget and starts nothing; the first row for a real agent is written from its public documentation and stays `unconfigured` until the maintainer has checked it. No run with a real agent is recorded: **the first runs with a real agent are deferred to the next release** by the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-08. Writing the tasks found one defect, fixed since by c0143 (released in 0.2.2 and 0.3.0): a design that names the built-in catalog and a `Power(...)` interface does not build (`build.vendor-unsafe-name`, the libraries `Fenolite` and `fenolite` differ only in letter case), so a catalog part with a power input pin cannot pass KiCad's ERC from a connector supply; the task `regulator-zone` uses a Zener shunt regulator for that reason.
- `route` joins the SMD pads of plane nets to their planes with fan-out vias before the router runs, and never traces a plane net (c0107); `--no-plane-fanout` skips the step, and `result.plane_layers` and `result.plane_fanout` report it. Copper layers of type `power` are plane layers, and **`board(planes=…)` now sets that type on KiCad boards: the board of a script that declares planes changes in those rows, also on a rebuild; a script without planes builds the bytes it built before.** A plane without a zone of its net gives `build.plane-zone-missing`. The Specctra file declares plane layers and planes, keeps classes to their layers, and carries class clearances, class-to-class clearance and per-layer widths; the rules it cannot carry, the edge clearance among them, are reported (`specctra.rule-not-sent`). `route` now reads the project's rules file. New rule kind `no_tracks` (`disallow track` in KiCad, for target 10 until its probe is recorded on 9.0), which an Altium build reports as not lowered. **Releases 0.2.x and 0.3.x cannot read a model document that holds a `no_tracks` rule.** `build.plane-not-lowered` is no longer given.
- **Net ties, waivers and check severities** (c0114). Net-tie pad groups are read, written and authored (`Footprint.net_tie`), and the copper check no longer reports two pads of one group: a board whose tied pads touch, or sit closer than the clearance, loses those `copper.short` and `copper.clearance` findings in `check` and in the build's copper guard. `design.waive()` accepts one `copper.*` or `kicad.drc.*` finding with a reason, which `check` and the build's copper guard then list as `info`, and `check` reports waivers and KiCad exclusions that no longer match (`check.waiver-unmatched`, `check.exclusion-stale`); `design.rules.severity()` sets the severity of a KiCad DRC check in the project file. Waivers also apply to `check` on a built Altium project and to the copper guard of the Altium build; the Altium build reports net-tie groups and severities instead of writing them. **Output changes:** the result of `check` gains `waivers`, `result.copper_check` of `build` gains `waivers`, the summary of `copper.clearance` gains `net_tie_pairs` and that of `drc.kicad` gains `exclusions`, and the issue of a DRC entry that the KiCad project excludes now ends with ` (excluded in the project: <comment>)`. The model gains three keys, each omitted when unused, so a design that uses none of them builds the same bytes: `net_ties` (`board.json`, `library.json`), `waivers` (`findings.json`) and `severities` (`rules.json`); 0.2.x cannot read a document that carries one of them. KiCad's verdicts are proved on `kicad-cli` 10.0.6; the 9.0.9 runs are owed.
- Script copper can be given in a part's frame (c0111): `part.at(dx, dy)` and `part.pad(n).at(dx, dy)` stand wherever a point is taken, and the build resolves them after placement, so vias, waypoints and stitches follow a moved part at the next build. `design.stitch(region=part.pad(n))` makes a thermal via array inside a pad. `part_frame` gives the same points to the Python API. `via_step` and `Design.via` also take their point as one argument, an anchor or an `(x, y)` pair; the two-length forms work as before, and a script without anchors builds the same bytes. No model, schema, CLI or issue-code change: an anchor that cannot be resolved and a pad that cannot hold an array use five existing `kicad.copper.*` codes (`docs/copper.md`). A via of another net anchored inside a pad passes KiCad's DRC, which gives it the pad's net; the copper guard of `build` refuses it. Measured on `kicad-cli` 10.0.6; the same probes on 9.0.9 are pending, so `H-G-FRAME-ANCHOR` and `H-K-VIA-IN-PAD` are `INFERRED`.
- `export --fab-drawing` writes a fabrication drawing (outline and its dimensions, board, stack-up, drill and
  impedance tables, numbered notes) with KiCad's drill maps and report, and `--assembly-drawing` top and bottom
  assembly drawings, all plotted by `kicad-cli` from a copy of the board; `--drawing-spec FILE` sets paper, sheet,
  tables, notes and assembly options; the drill counts are checked against KiCad's report (c0117;
  `docs/drawings.md`). The impedance table has no row yet, and the stack-up table waits for stack-ups read from
  KiCad boards (c0101). The kinds are `INFERRED`: their probes hold on `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 and are not yet recorded
  on 9.0.9.
- **The agent guide teaches how to design a board** (c0080): eleven pages (`fenolite guide <topic>`) on the design script, parts, footprints, placement, routing, rules, checks, reading and comparing files, fabrication files, the Altium target and recovery, plus generated command and DSL references (`tools/gen_agent_guide.py`, whose `--check` runs in `make check-fast`, with the lint step). Every command line, design script and recovery recipe of the guide is run by tests, and **a new command, DSL name or error code must be added to it**: `tests/unit/agent/test_pages.py` names what is missing. The start page keeps its loop and gains the index of the pages; its line on `fenolite manifest` no longer names an artefact folder outside the project, which that command refuses. The page `design-script` says that a `Power` builds with catalog parts (c0143), and the page `routing` teaches the anchors of script copper, `part.pad(n).at(dx, dy)` and `part.at(dx, dy)` (c0111).
- Rule areas and keep-outs (`design.rule_area`), board texts, drawings and dimensions (`design.text`, `line`, `rect`, `circle`, `arc`, `polygon`, `dimension`) and rules scoped to an area (`select.area`, written as `intersectsArea`) from the script (c0103); script items are regenerated on every build. `check` and the copper guard of `build` apply area rules and report copper in a keep-out as `copper.keepout` (`--copper-check warn` writes anyway); a rule naming no rule area stops the build (`build.area-unknown`). With `--target altium` rule areas with a restriction and centred texts are written, and an area's name, an area for rules only, a justified text, a dimension and a rule with an area selector are reported. **Release 0.2.x cannot read a model document that carries the new keys** (`name` of a keep-out, `h_justify`, `v_justify`, `dimensions`, the selector `area`). A rule with an area selector is written for KiCad 9 and 10: its probe `dru-cond-area` is recorded for 10.0.6 and, since the image run of 2026-10-08, for 9.0.9. The rule-area, text, dimension and keep-out facts are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)` (the re-save of an area's name and of a dimension's text on 10.0.6 only), confirmed by the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI run 37772583226; every name a created dimension writes is a name of the 8.0 format (the 8.0.0 demo boards, S-0723, and the format page, S-0001). **`fenolite check` can exit 5 where it exited 0**: on a board whose copper lies in a keep-out, and on a board whose hand-written `intersectsArea` rules are now applied by the copper stage. The summary of the copper stage gains `keepouts` and `rule_areas`, `result.pcb` of an Altium build gains the kind `dimension`, and a KiCad board read with a named rule area, a justified text or a linear dimension now holds them in the model.
- Differential pairs in KiCad (c0104): class pair width, gap and via gap (`diff_pair_width`, `diff_pair_gap` and `diff_pair_via_gap` on `design.rules.netclass()`, written to and read from the project file); rule kinds for pair gap, uncoupled length, skew and length (`diff_pair_gap`, `diff_pair_uncoupled`, `skew`, `diff_pair_skew`, `length`); `select.pair()` and `design.rules.pair()`; the copper check lowers the clearance inside a pair as KiCad does, so `check` stops reporting a coupled pair that is below its class clearance and at or above its class pair gap; pair names with digits after the P or N are recognised (`D_P0`/`D_N0`), so `build.diff-pair-name` stops warning on them. `build.interface-not-lowered` is now given only for a pair that no rule selects, and the new warning `build.diff-pair-gap-shadowed` names a pair that KiCad would report at its class gap. A hand-written `.kicad_dru` with pair rules now lifts them into the model. The pair kinds and the pair selector are written for KiCad 10, where every probe is recorded on 10.0.6; for target 9 they are refused (`rules.kind-unchecked`, exit 7) until their probes are recorded on 9.0.9. **0.2.x and 0.3.0 cannot read a `circuit.json` or a `rules.json` that carries the new keys, kinds or the `diff_pair` selector.** An Altium build names pair rules, class pair values and pair interfaces (`altium.not-lowered`) and writes none of them.
- **Outlines with arcs and cut-outs, holes, and rebuilds that follow a new outline or copper count (c0102).** A script can give the board an outline with arcs and cut-outs (`board(outline=…)`, `cutout()`, `fenolite.dsl.shape`) and holes (`design.hole()`); authored footprints accept unnumbered hole pads. A rebuild now follows a new outline or copper count: copper that no longer fits, or that lies on a removed layer, is dropped and reported, and a stale stack-up is removed. Outline edges carry new uuids from the first build after this change. With `--target altium` an outline with arcs plans no PCB document, a round hole that is not plated is written, and a slot or a plated hole is reported. **Release 0.2.x cannot read a model document that carries the new key `arcs`.** Rebuilds that stopped with `layout.copper-mismatch` for a count change, or kept an old outline with `layout.outline-kept`, now apply the script: run them with `--dry-run` first to read the counts of `kicad.outline.copper-dropped` and `kicad.layers.removed`. An outline edited in KiCad still wins unless the script says `board(..., locked=True)`. The symbol library writer of authored symbols now writes `in_bom`, `on_board` and `exclude_from_sim` of the definition, where it wrote `yes`, `yes` and `no` for every symbol. The outline, hole and layer-change facts are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)` (the zone box `KICAD-VERIFIED (10.0.x)`): KiCad 9.0.9 reports `invalid_outline` for two overlapping cut-outs only, where 10.0.6 also reports a cut-out across or touching the edge and a self-crossing ring, and Fenolite's own check refuses all of them for both targets.
- Placement rules (c0113): `design.near()` for decoupling, crystal and gate-driver proximity. `fenolite check` gains the default stage `placement.rules` (rules of a built project, wire length and congestion of every board), on KiCad projects and on Altium documents; `place` reports the same numbers (`result.rules`, `result.measures`) and refuses a part in a keep-out that forbids footprints, as KiCad's DRC does (`place.keepout`), and its grid leaves such areas free; `build` reports rule findings as warnings (`result.placement.rules`). On a built project with rules, `check` can now exit 5. An Altium build stores the rules and says in one `altium.not-lowered` info that it did not judge them. A `rules.json` with `proximity` cannot be read by 0.2.x or 0.3.0. `rule_area(forbid=…)` takes `footprints`, which was refused: the area is then a placement keep-out (`(footprints not_allowed)` in KiCad; one `altium.not-lowered` info of kind `keepout-footprints` in an Altium build). The keep-out rule is recorded on KiCad 10.0.6 and waits for the KiCad 9 run of CI (`H-K-PLACE-KEEPOUT`).
- **A board whose zone or rule area holds `(layer "*.Cu")` can be rewritten** (c0145): the layers of a zone written under the singular head with a wildcard or a mask (`(layer "*.Cu")`, `(layer "F&B.Cu")`) were read, and then every rewrite of the board, unchanged, was refused with exit 7 (`field 'layers' cannot be written from the model`): `route`, `place`, a rebuild and `write_board`, in 0.2.1 too. The child is now kept as written, as under `layers`. A single layer written under `layers` is kept as well instead of being respelled, and a changed layer set over a wildcard is refused under both heads. KiCad 10.0.6 itself does not load a wildcard under `layer` (`docs/formats/kicad/board.md`, `H-K-ZONE-LAYER-HEAD`), so `fill` and `check` still fail on such a board with the tool's message.
- Net lengths as KiCad 9 and 10 count them (tracks, arcs, via heights from the stack-up, die lengths), pin-to-pin lengths and pair skew in `fenolite analyze --kinds length --net GLOB [--from REF]`, a `length.rules` stage in `check` for length and skew rules, and `Design.meander` to bring a script track to a target length or to the length of another script track (c0106). The total follows the major of `--kicad-version`: the two majors count a via apart, and a file without a stack-up is counted on the default stack-up KiCad assumes, with the info `kicad.length.default-stackup`. `result.lengths` gives each net's parts, its paths from a start pad and the copper on no path; new codes `analysis.length-open`, `analysis.length-stub`, `kicad.length.bad-die` and eight `kicad.meander.*`. `fenolite build` resolves meanders right after script copper and reports `result.copper.meanders` when a script holds one; a script without meanders builds the same bytes. The routed lengths of `fenolite net` and of `equivalent --level 5` now come from the same kernel functions (`geometry.arc_length`, `segment_length`), with the same values to the nanometre. `result.pairs` gives the skew of each differential pair (a pair interface of the design, or two nets the pair name rule couples). The default stage `length.rules` of `fenolite check`, right after `copper.clearance`, judges the `length`, `skew` and `diff_pair_skew` rules of change c0104 on those totals without `kicad-cli`, as KiCad does (`min` and `max`, not `opt`; a net of pads only at length 0; skew against the longest net of each group, each pair apart for `diff_pair_skew`), with the new codes `length.out-of-range`, `length.skew-out-of-range` and `length.input-missing`; it runs on KiCad input only, and `check` on Altium input keeps its stage list. The canaries are recorded for `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 and not yet for 9.0.9, so every reply carries `INFERRED`; an Altium document is measured without via heights and is `UNVERIFIED`.
- **A reviewed plan is written as reviewed, a command writes all its files or none, and a long step reports progress** (c0120). A dry run returns `result.plan_id`, and `--confirm --plan ID` writes the reviewed bytes without running the command again (`FEN-4002`, exit 4, when a target or an input changed). A command writes all its files or none; a failed write gives `FEN-1002` (exit 1, retryable) instead of a traceback. **Changed:** a command that reports an error finding writes nothing, `place --force` excepted (`manifest` and `kit record`, whose write is the report of the findings, still write); a writing command that reports an error without `--confirm` now exits 5, not 4. A stopped command (SIGTERM, Ctrl+C) stops its tools and gives `FEN-1003`. `--progress` writes progress records on stderr. `route` reuses the copper of router runs that finished before a stop (`result.resumed`, `route.resumed`). **Changed:** exit 1 is a bug only when `retryable` is false; the hint of `FEN-4001` names `--confirm --plan <id>`; every plan carries `result.plan_id` and every receipt `plan`; `capabilities` lists `global_flags` and `mutation_flags`; `route` returns `resumed`. Dry runs now write under `~/.cache/fenolite/state` (`FENOLITE_STATE_DIR`; `off` turns it off).
- `fenolite route` gives differential pairs and escape requests only to a router that routes them (`features` in `fenolite capabilities`), or the pair nets as single nets with `--pairs-as-nets`; every other pair gives `route.pair-skipped` (c0110). `--escape REF[=grid|perimeter]` names parts to escape (`route.escape-skipped` without the feature); Freerouting runs without automatic neck-down and takes `fanout=off`; routed copper narrower than asked gives `route.width-below-job`. The KiCadRoutingTools pair (`route_diff.py`) and escape (`bga_fanout.py`, `qfn_fanout.py`) steps exist; after the gate of c0110 KiCadRoutingTools declares `pairs` and not `escape` (below); Freerouting's design file holds no `pair` list. No model or schema change.
- Impedance targets per net class or pair (`design.rules.impedance()`, `trace()`), checked by derived rules on KiCad 9 and 10 and written as KiCad 10 tuning profiles; build warnings for shadowed rules, class values, gaps and an unmarked stack-up; `fenolite impedance` with the table for the fabricator and `INFERRED` estimates of single-ended lines; an Altium build keeps targets in the model and names them; 0.2.x and 0.3.0 cannot read a `rules.json` that carries the key `impedance` (c0105). The KiCad probes of the change (`pro-tuning-*`, `dru-impedance-width`) are written and not yet recorded.
- A part can state its height (`Part(height=…)`): it becomes a body of its footprint, kept in `.fenolite/`, and `design.height_limit(area, max=…)` limits the parts under a named rule area. `check` (stage `placement.rules`), `place` and `build` report `placement.too-tall` and `placement.height-unknown`. A part's height has one source, its bodies, read by `outward_height`. A `rules.json` with `heights` cannot be read by 0.2.x or 0.3.0 (c0140). **Not yet complete:** the signed body bounds of change c0099 are not on this line, so `outward_height` reads each body's `height`; on the Altium target a limit gives `placement.rule-unresolved`, because a PCB document holds no rule-area name, and a script height is reported as a body without an outline (`altium.not-lowered`, `body/<id>`) and not written.
- Copper findings carry hashable rule candidate explanations for the actual clearance judgment; relation/source grouping preserves every finding without advice or exemptions, with unchanged numeric verdicts and CLI output (c0097).
- **Signed component body volumes:** known Altium imports serialize z_min/z_max equal to source standoff/height; negative standoff no longer produces model.body-height. Mounted-face volume analysis reports intersections and missing inputs conservatively (c0099).
- **Retained unknown bodies:** malformed heights (stored as 0), reversed extents, unsupported model types and mismatched projection sides are retained with projection_unknown and a warning, without model.body-height or an unmapped census entry (c0099).
- **Body document compatibility:** v0.2.0 body documents retain defaults, bytes and legacy validation until reimport; the readers of releases 0.2.x and 0.3.0 reject documents with new body keys (c0099).
- Add mechanical intent metadata (`MechanicalIntent`, `Part.place(..., locked=True, anchor=...)`), group-bounded constrained placement with injected checkers (`fenolite place --strategy constrained --constraints FILE`, schema `fenolite.placement-request.v0`), explicit incomplete assessment and confirmed readback copper previews for both faces. Holes and keep-outs of a script stay `design.hole()` and `design.rule_area()`: the hole and keep-out calls of the c0096 branch are not part of this integration (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-07). `Hole.intent`, `Keepout.intent` and `FootprintInstance.anchor` are model fields that builds do not persist (c0096). Releases 0.2.x and 0.3.0 cannot read a `board.json` that carries `intent` or `anchor`.
- The explanation of a copper finding between the two nets of a differential pair holds the row `pair-gap:<class>` that governs it (c0104 with c0097), and `fenolite place --strategy constrained` judges supplied volumes with the body-volume check of c0099 and takes each copper finding's relation from c0097, where it computed its own (c0096, task R5). No verdict changes.

### Changed

- The layer-table benches of c0100 write the created test board without its stack-up, so they judge the layer table alone; a stack-up whose copper entries are not the board's copper layers is refused by the board writer on a created and on a read board alike (`kicad.board.stackup-invalid`), which the requirement of c0101 now states and a test pins. No behaviour of the library changes (c0101).
- The README of the agent evaluation tasks says in one sentence which licence covers what: the task scripts carry the `Apache-2.0` header that the SPDX guard demands of every `.py` file, the task texts are `CC0-1.0` (c0081).
- The probes that the v0.4 changes registered for KiCad 9 have their 9.0.9 outcomes, measured in the pinned image (57 rows of `docs/evidence/kicad/probes/9.0.9.json`). **A rule of the kind `no_tracks` is now written for target 9 too** (`dru-kind-no_tracks` is `present` on 9.0.9); it gave `rules.kind-unchecked` there before. Three outcomes differ from their tests on 9.0.9 and are recorded as measured: `drc-limit-hole_clearance` and `drc-limit-keys` (`different`), and `route-fanout-t9` (`inconclusive` in a container without `kicad-cli` 10).
- The fan-out oracle of c0107 is judged on KiCad 9 wherever it runs: the two target-9 boards of its case, filled by KiCad 10.0.6, are committed under `tests/data/kicad/routing/`, and a run on 9.0 without a `kicad-cli` 10 reads them after checking that they are the boards it built and routed. `route-fanout-t9` is `equal` on 9.0.9 in the pinned image (it was `inconclusive`). No behaviour of the library changes (c0107).
- Three probes of c0108 are pinned on KiCad 9 as well: `pcb-lock-load`, `copper-open-kicad` and `copper-open-parity` are registered for both majors and recorded `equal` on 9.0.9 in the pinned image; they were registered for KiCad 10 alone and measured on 9 by hand. No behaviour of the library changes (c0108).
- The probes that the changes stacked after the first 9.0.9 run registered for KiCad 9 have their 9.0.9 outcomes, measured in the pinned image (173 more rows of `docs/evidence/kicad/probes/9.0.9.json`, 723 in all). **Rules with an `area` or a `diff_pair` selector and rules of the kinds `diff_pair_gap`, `diff_pair_uncoupled`, `skew`, `diff_pair_skew` and `length` are now written for target 9 too** (`dru-cond-area`, `dru-cond-diff_pair` and the five `dru-kind-*` probes are `present` on 9.0.9); they were refused there before (`rules.unsupported-selector`, `rules.kind-unchecked`). One outcome differs from its test on 9.0.9 and is recorded as measured: `outline-invalid-selfx` is `absent` (9.0.9 reports no `invalid_outline` for the self-crossing ring of the bench).
- The height of a part (`fenolite.model.board.outward_height`, c0140) now takes the `z_max` of a body with signed bounds and skips a body of unknown projection, so the height limits of `check`, `place` and `build` judge an imported Altium body by its signed bounds (c0099).
- **The copper check on Altium input passes copper up to 9 nm inside a clearance rule, where it passed 5 nm** (c0152). Altium Designer 26.5.0's own rule check reports none of the seven pad-to-track findings that Fenolite gave on the public document `altium-third-party-pcbdoc-03`, 8 and 9 nm short of a 5 mil rule (S-0616). The cause is not Fenolite's reading: the document's own integers put the track 3.5 file units (8.89 nm) inside the rule, and Fenolite reads the gap to within 0.11 nm; Altium allows at least that much. Every clearance rule of a PCB document is now lowered by 9 nm, that observed tolerance in whole nanometres (`CLEARANCE_SLACK_NM`; the unit's 5 nm of c0131 is inside it), documented in `docs/formats/altium/import.md` as `ALTIUM-VERIFIED(author-report)`. On the eight public PCB documents the class of findings 8 to 20 nm short goes from 25 to 9 (0 on `-03`, 1 on `-01`, 8 on the heavy one), all 10 to 20 nm short, and they stay errors. KiCad boards are judged strictly at the nanometre, as before.
- The fact rows of differential pairs (c0104) in `docs/formats/kicad/rules.md`, `project.md` and `copper.md` say `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, as their five hypothesis rows do: the `kicad-9` job of CI run 37772583226 passed the pair rules, names, classes, copper parity and rule design tests on 9.0.9.
- Impedance targets (c0105): `dru-impedance-width` (both majors) and eight `pro-tuning-*` probes (KiCad 10) are pinned in the probe files from CI run 37772583226, and `H-K-DRU-IMPEDANCE` is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`. The KiCad 10 GUI save that settles the keys of a tuning profile (`H-K-PRO-TUNING-KEYS`) is deferred to the next release, so tuning profiles stay `INFERRED`.
- Net lengths (c0106): `H-K-NETLEN-TOTAL`, `-STACKUP` (2 to 8 copper layers), `-RULES` and `-MEANDER` are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)` and `-VIA9` `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x)`, from the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI run 37772583226; the roadmap moves matched-length and skew analysis from v0.6 to v0.4.
- `tests/routing/test_krt_gate.py::test_planes` (c0107) routes the plane bench through KiCadRoutingTools with and without a `no_tracks` rule, needing a checkout and no `kicad-cli`; v0.22.1 put every track on `F.Cu` in all three runs, so `H-K-KRT-PLANES` is recorded `inconclusive` and the plugin keeps `route.constraint-not-sent`. The plane fan-out is `KICAD-VERIFIED` (`backends.kicad.fanout.EVIDENCE`), and the routing page of the agent guide shows `route` on a board with `planes=` and `result.plane_fanout.failed`.
- The design script of the routing page of the agent guide locks one script track (`locked=True`, c0108); `test_krt_outcome_is_recorded_either_way` prints the outcome `krt-partial`, so the `routing` job's log shows it; the MODIFIED "Net selection" of c0108 keeps the scenario names of the living spec.
- KiCadRoutingTools on nets that already hold copper is recorded (c0108): `krt-partial` is `different` on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 in the `routing` jobs of CI run 37803522539 (one of the bench's six open connections stays open, every earlier copper item is kept); `H-K-KRT-PARTIAL` records it and stays `INFERRED`. Nothing depends on it, since `route` takes its verdict from the board.
- `H-K-KRT-GROUP` (c0109) is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`: the `routing` jobs of CI run 37772583226 passed `test_group` on both majors. S-0216, S-0221 and S-0222 name the tag pages of Freerouting 2.4.1 and the `route.py --help` of KiCadRoutingTools v0.22.1 that c0109 relies on.
- The feasibility gate of c0110 is written: the pair, name, QFN and BGA benches (authored parts in `tests/routing/_gateparts.py`), `tests/routing/test_pair_gate.py` and `test_escape_gate.py` run in the `routing` jobs and print each outcome, each tool run bounded by `FENOLITE_GATE_SECONDS` (900 by default). `krt-pair-names` is recorded `equal` on a local checkout (`H-K-KRT-PAIRNAMES`); `H-K-DRU-PAIRCOUPLE` is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)` and its four `dru-pair-couple-*` probes are pinned for both majors.
- **KiCadRoutingTools routes differential pairs** (c0110): the gate ran in the `routing` jobs of CI run 37803522539 on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6. `krt-pair-t9` and `krt-pair-t10` are `equal` (no unconnected item, no gap or uncoupled finding), so `kicadroutingtools` declares `pairs` and `fenolite route --router kicadroutingtools` gives it the pairs, routed with `route_diff.py` (the gate ran the tool by script; the run through `fenolite route` under KiCad is still to be recorded); `H-K-KRT-PAIR` is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`. Its escape is not declared: on the BGA bench the dog-bone escape left more connections open than routing without it and put vias in pads (`krt-escape-bga-t<M>` `different`, `H-K-KRT-ESCAPE` refuted by `H-K-KRT-ESCAPE-2`), so `--escape` still gives `route.escape-skipped` with every router. Freerouting's fanout stage stays on by default (better than `fanout=off` on both benches and majors, `H-G-DSN-FANOUT-2`); `H-G-DSN-PAIR` and `H-G-DSN-NARROW` are `ORACLE-VERIFIED(freerouting 2.4.1)`. The outcomes are in `docs/evidence/routing.md` and the results files `krt-v0.22.1.json` and `freerouting-2.4.1.json`.
- The agent guide's `routing` page teaches a thermal via array: its tested design block adds `design.stitch(…, region=d1.pad(2))`, one via inside pad 2 of `D1` (c0111). The two fact rows of `docs/formats/kicad/frame.md` on anchored points and on vias inside an SMD pad are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, from the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI.
- **`fenolite place` and `fenolite check` judge a keep-out that forbids footprints at `KICAD-VERIFIED`** (c0113): `H-K-PLACE-KEEPOUT` holds on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 in the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI, so `placement.legality.KEEPOUT_EVIDENCE` no longer lowers the level of a reply on a board with such a keep-out.
- The agent guide teaches net ties, waivers and check severities (c0114): the `footprints` page declares a net-tie footprint with `net_tie()`, the `rules` page calls `design.rules.severity()` and `design.waive()`, and the `checks` page says how to accept one finding. The fact rows of net ties and of stored DRC exclusions are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, from the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI.
- The power benches of c0115 take a target: `neck-plain`, with its fill stored, and `layers` also run on KiCad 9 (`tests/kicad/analysis/test_power_nine.py`), whose 9.0.9 outcomes the `kicad-9` job prints for tasks 1.2 and 11.1. No behaviour of the library changes.
- The 9.0.9 outcomes of the power benches are recorded (c0115): the `kicad-9` job of CI run 37803522539 printed `equal` for the plain neck (nothing at 1.95 mm, 2.0000 mm at 2.05 mm) and for the inner layers, so `analysis-neck-plain` and `insulation-layers` are registered for KiCad 9 too (two more rows of `9.0.9.json`), and `H-K-AN-LAYERS` is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`.
- **An export that selects a document kind, and `fenolite models`, carry `KICAD-VERIFIED`** (c0116): the four hypotheses of `exports.DOCUMENTS_EVIDENCE` and `H-K-EXPORT-MODELS` hold on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 in the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI. The first real fetch of official 3D models (the three of `examples/blink_official` at tags 9.0.9 and 10.0.6, with the maintainer's consent of 2026-10-08) is recorded in `docs/evidence/kicad-libs.md`, and `H-G-MODELS-FETCH` is `CORPUS-VERIFIED`.
- **The drawing kinds of `fenolite export` carry `KICAD-VERIFIED`** (c0117): `H-K-DRAW-ITEMS`, `-PAGE` and `-DRILL` hold on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 in the `kicad-9` and `kicad-10` jobs of CI, so `exports.drawings.EVIDENCE` is raised and the fact rows of the drawings follow.
- The agent guide teaches the assembly features (c0118): the `placement` page calls `design.fiducial()`, `design.test_point()` and `design.tooling_hole()` in its tested design block, and the `fabrication` page names them beside `fenolite testpoints`. New KiCad tests build the features from the script (`tests/kicad/assembly/test_built_features.py`, with the two outcomes `asm-tooling-drill` and `asm-features-pos`) and run the probes of both majors on KiCad 9 (`test_features_nine.py`).
- **`fenolite testpoints` carries `KICAD-VERIFIED` evidence for its own part** (c0118): the `kicad-9` job of CI run 37803522539 printed the 17 probes of the pad marks, fiducials, keep-out and test points that are stated for both majors, each with its 10.0.6 outcome, so they are registered for KiCad 9 too (17 more rows of `docs/evidence/kicad/probes/9.0.9.json`), the five rows `H-K-PAD-FABPROP`, `-LIB`, `H-K-FIDUCIAL-FORM`, `-KEEPOUT` and `H-K-TESTPOINT-D356` are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)` (the keep-out refill on 10.0.x only) and `exports.testpoints.EVIDENCE` is raised; the reply still takes the lower level of the board read. The features built from a script passed their checks on both majors.
- Development: the release record of 0.4.0 and its guard (`docs/release/v0.4.md`, `tests/unit/test_release_record_v04.py`, change c0154): the verdict per change of v0.4, what is deferred to the next release by the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-08 (everything that depends on his own tests: KiCad GUI saves, a run of a real agent, Altium Designer checks) and the follow-ups found that day.

### Fixed

- The RT-A2 test of the examples (`tests/unit/lens/test_altium_rta2.py`) classifies `examples/yardstick`, which c0119 added and which made three of its tests fail: no Altium build of the yardstick writes anything, so it is listed as refused with the measured reason. Without the official KiCad libraries its symbols do not resolve (exit 3); with them the build is refused in the binary and in the ASCII form (exit 5) by one `altium.text-unwritable`, the description of `Isolator_Analog:AMC1200BDWV` holding `±`. A test holds the entry to that reason where the libraries are installed. No behaviour of the library changes (c0119).
- The DRC report limits of c0141 say what each KiCad major measured. KiCad 9.0.9 writes no `hole_clearance` entry for the construct of the limits bench (a pad beside an unplated hole), so that limit is not measured there and stays the assumed 199; `backends.kicad.drc.MEASURED_TYPES` names the measured types per major (thirteen on 10.0, twelve on 9.0) and follows the probe files. The 9.0.9 report has nine top-level keys, without `ignored_checks`; `drc-limit-keys` compares with the keys of the running major and is `equal` on 9.0.9 (recorded again), and `drc-limit-hole_clearance` stays `different` there. The limits, the marks and every reply of `check` are unchanged (c0141).
- **A board declared with `board(outline=…)` takes a stack-up, rule areas and board drawings** (c0101, c0103;
  found by c0119). `design.stackup()`, `design.rule_area()` and `design.text`, `line`, `rect`, `circle`, `arc`,
  `polygon` and `dimension` refused such a board ("call board() first") because they tested the size that only
  `board(width, height)` sets; they now accept a board of either form, as `zone()` and `cutout()` do. A rounded board
  writes the stack-up, rule areas and drawings, with their uuids, that a rectangle of the same script writes, and a
  rectangle's files are unchanged.
- The yardstick runner (`tools/yardstick.py`, c0119) judges `rebuild.board-unchanged` on the board as the rebuild
  left it. It hashed the board at the end of the run, after `route-pairs`, `route` and `fill-routed` had rewritten it,
  so the first nightly run (37820561099) failed the rule although the rebuild wrote the bytes it found (SHA-256
  `8886fb6a…` before and after). No command changes.
- The yardstick runner judges the errors of `length.rules` on the routed board as it judges KiCad's DRC errors there:
  `length.out-of-range` and `length.skew-out-of-range` are counted once, by the `drc_errors` ratchet, when KiCad's
  `length_out_of_range` and `skew_out_of_range` have the same counts, and fail the stage when they differ. The first
  nightly run failed on the routed USB pair (`USB_DN` 121.7 mm and `USB_DP` 114.2 mm against a 60 mm maximum, 7.5 mm
  of skew against 0.5 mm), which KiCad reported with the same counts (c0119).
- **A `docker:<image>` value of `FENOLITE_KICAD_CLI` or `--kicad-cli` runs that image on Windows.** The marker went
  through a `Path`, which spells the slashes of `kicad/kicad:9.0.9` as backslashes on Windows, so the runner was given
  the image `kicad\kicad:9.0.9`. `cli.docker_image` turns them back (an image reference holds no backslash), and the
  runner, the pull hint and the tests' resolver use it.
- The tests that CI runs on KiCad 9 and on Windows match what those platforms do: the routing benches that `kicad-cli`
  judges are written for the major of the local `kicad-cli` (a 10.0 board is one that KiCad 9 does not read, and the
  `Mini.pretty` footprints are in the 10.0 form, so a target-9 bench takes `Mini_v9.pretty`); the fake `java` of the
  Freerouting tests and the stand-in agent of the time-budget test start on Windows; the files of a built project are
  compared by POSIX names; a test board names a Windows model path as an escaped KiCad string; `docs/hypotheses.md` is
  read as UTF-8. `H-K-OUTLINE-INVALID` and `INVALID_ON_9` of `test_outline_shapes.py` say what 9.0.9 reports: no
  `invalid_outline` for the self-crossing ring of the bench (`outline-invalid-selfx` `absent`, as recorded in
  `9.0.9.json`); the 10.0.6 expectation is unchanged.
- **Parallel `kicad-cli` runs no longer share KiCad's instance lock file** (c0153). Every run of the package runner now gets its own temporary, runtime, cache and state folders (`TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`) beside its own `KICAD_CONFIG_HOME`, removed with the run, so `fenolite` commands run side by side, and the oracle tests on parallel workers, no longer race on `/tmp/org.kicad.kicad/instances/kicad-cli-<major>.0` ("Invalid lock file"). The Docker runner is unchanged (each container has its own `/tmp`). Outputs do not change; a project folder named `.fenolite-state` is now a reserved name, like `config`.
- A schematic symbol that is mirrored and turned by 90 or 270 degrees connects its pins where KiCad does (c0137): KiCad turns the symbol first and mirrors the turned symbol, and Fenolite mirrored first. A build whose `schematic-placements.toml` asks for such a pair (`rotation = 90` with `mirror = "x"`, for example) set the labels of that unit where KiCad does not connect its pins, and the own netlist, the netlist guard and `fenolite netlist` read such an instance of a KiCad sheet with its pins swapped; all of them now follow `schlayout.pin_point`, which rotates first. The order is read from the demo sheets of the corpus (`docs/formats/kicad/schematic.md`, `H-K-SCH-PINFRAME-ORDER`, `CORPUS-VERIFIED`), which a sheet with such an instance now names in its evidence; the probes `sch-pin-frame-*` could not tell the two orders apart (their symbol is symmetric) and keep their outcomes. **A build without such a pair is written byte for byte as before**; the stacked-pin test over the corpus no longer leaves those instances out.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-10-08

### Added

- v0.3, the write part of the second backend: `fenolite build --target altium` writes a complete Altium project (PCB document, schematic documents, both libraries, project file, output job and drawing sheet), a model with a board is written back as Altium documents with the round-trip level RT-A3, `fenolite check` judges copper and parity on Altium input without a tool, `equivalent` compares routing at level 5, and `fenolite kit` builds, checks and records the Altium verification kit. **Every Altium write stays experimental in 0.3.0**: by the rule of change c0092 no write kind has the evidence to leave it, and no kit run is recorded. What is proved, the verdict per write kind and what is not in this release are in `docs/release/v0.3.md`.
- A pin may be bonded to several pads (c0123): `pad_map={"3": ("3", "EP")}` in a script, and the pads that a footprint model lists for one pin in an Altium import. Every pad takes the pin's net on the board; the generated KiCad schematic holds one hidden pin per further pad, so KiCad's netlist, ERC and parity test see them; `netlist.assignment_compare` and level 2 of `equivalent` name every pad. `Component.pin_pad_map` holds such a pin in several pairs, and `Component.pads_of(pin)` reads it. A design whose pins have one pad each is written byte for byte as before. **The model version stays `"0"`, at a cost: Fenolite 0.2.x and earlier read a document that holds a pin of several pads without an error and apply one pad per pin.** New finding `model.pin-pad-map` (error) for a pad that two pins name; a build of a script with such a pad keeps its own error beside it (`altium.pin-pad-map-invalid`, `build.pin-pad-map-invalid`).
- An Altium build bonds a pin to every pad its `pad_map` lists (c0123). The release 0.2.1 fixed the build for a map of one pad per pin; now each pad of a pin carries the pin's net in the PCB document, script copper and `--copper-from` are checked against every pad, and the map record of the pin in the schematic and its library lists all its pads. RT-A2 compares the map. `fenolite check`, the parity test and `equivalent` know the map of a built Altium project from 0.3.0: they report nothing for the mapped pins, where 0.2.1 reports `netlist.assignment-differs`. A map that names a missing pin or pad, or that leaves one pad to two pins, is refused with `altium.pin-pad-map-invalid`, as in 0.2.1. **The files of an Altium build change only for a script whose `pad_map` gives a pin several pads.**
- The Altium import names the components of a sheet that several sheet symbols name per channel: by the project's PCB document, else by the project's designator format (`D9_1` … `D9_12` instead of `D9` twelve times). On the public project with such a sheet, the schematic and the board now share 694 pad assignments instead of 508. The import instantiates repeated sheets: a sheet symbol `Repeat(NAME, first, last)` gives one channel per index, each with its own components, nets and designators (`$ChannelIndex` and `$ChannelAlpha` are resolved; a sheet entry `Repeat(NAME)` takes one member of the parent's bus per channel; a net inside a channel is named per channel); `altium.import.repeated-sheet` is now only the statement that cannot be instantiated. Imported components carry the pin-to-pad map of their footprint model (`pin_pad_map`), which `check` and level 2 of `equivalent` apply; `altium.import.pin-map` (info) counts the map records that one pad per pin cannot say. The `Repeat` part rests on Altium's documentation and authored sheets (no public file holds one), and a board links to such channels by designator only; the annotation file is still not read (c0083).
- The Altium PCB document holds stacks of up to 16 signal layers, blind and buried vias, board texts, graphics, keep-outs and non-plated holes; polygons are written unpoured and repoured in Altium. `result.pcb` of an Altium build counts every model item as written or not lowered, and an item without a record is named by `altium.not-lowered` (`where` = `<kind>/<id>`). Changed: a blind or buried via is written instead of refused; `altium.via-unsupported` is a warning for micro vias only, and the build goes on without them; a board's keep-outs, texts, graphics and holes are written instead of reported per kind; a copper count that is odd or above 32 gives the stack of the two outer layers and `altium.not-lowered` (`stackup`) instead of `altium.copper-stack`. A keep-out holds its restrictions in the key Altium saves and in the key KiCad's importer reads, so a KiCad import carries them. Component bodies are not written yet (reported one by one), and the model's board holes have no slots (c0085).
- `equivalent --level 5` compares routing per net: which pads the copper joins, the vias per layer pair and the routed length per layer. Differences are located at the net (`equiv.route-missing`, `equiv.route-connectivity`, `equiv.route-vias`, `equiv.route-length`); copper that reaches no pad and nets that depend on a zone without a fill are reported and fail nothing (`equiv.route-stub`, `equiv.route-unjudged`, under `result.notices`). `--tolerance-ppm` and a profile's `tolerance_ppm` set a relative tolerance for routed lengths. **Behaviour change:** without `--level`, `equivalent` now runs level 5 when both sides hold a track, an arc or a via, so two routed boards that differ only in their routing exit 5 where they exited 0; pass `--level 4` for the earlier result. The triangle holds at level 5 on `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 for the routed sample and the seven public PCB documents (c0089).
- An Altium build writes an output job set up from the export preset and draws the script's drawing sheet on its schematics; `template build --target altium` writes a `.SchDot` from a sheet specification. The job (`<name>.OutJob`, on by default beside a PCB document, `--altium-outjob off` to leave it out) holds Gerber, NC drill, pick-and-place, bill-of-materials, schematic-print and PCB-print outputs with their source documents and two containers, and no output setting: the options of `--altium-outjob-preset` are listed in `result.outjob.defaults` to be set in Altium. The drawing sheet of `design.sheet(drawing_sheet=…)` is drawn on every Altium schematic as lines and texts on a custom sheet of the paper's size, with the title block as sheet parameters; a logo and a text that mixes a token with other text are losses that need `--allow-lossy`. Both files are proved by Fenolite's own readers only and stay `INFERRED` until Altium has opened them (c0087).
- An Altium build writes the script's rules by kind and scope, names every rule it cannot lower with its reason, and `export --altium-rul` writes them as a rule file. Seven Altium rule kinds carry the neutral ones (Clearance, Width, Routing Via Style, Hole Size, Board Outline Clearance, Hole To Hole Clearance, Minimum Annular Ring), scoped to all objects, a net, a net class or their conjunction; a rule is written exactly or not at all. **Changed:** the single `altium.not-lowered` info with `where` `design-rules` is gone; each rule that is not written gives one `altium.not-lowered` warning with `where` `design-rules/<kind>`, and `result.rules` lists what was written and what was not. A `clearance` or `edge_clearance` minimum of the script now reaches the PCB document and precedes the class rules; a width, via or hole-size minimum is reported, because Altium's record also holds a maximum. The import maps Board Outline Clearance, Hole To Hole Clearance and Minimum Annular Ring (c0084; that Altium applies the rules is not yet confirmed).
- `fenolite kit` builds the Altium verification kit, checks the files a run leaves and records the run: the base of the label `ALTIUM-VERIFIED(kit)`. `kit build` writes five sample projects from the scripts under `examples/kit/`, a checklist (`STEPS.md`), a result form and a script that a user may start inside Altium for the compile steps; the kit is the same byte for byte on every machine. `kit verify` reads the documents that Altium saved with Fenolite's readers, compares them with the kit's own at the levels of `equivalent`, checks the typed values and lists strings that look like a home folder or a login name. `kit record` writes the archive of the results and a run record without any path or user name; `kit status` lists the runs and the rows whose run is stale. No run was made yet, so no row carries the label; the copper check of a re-saved board waits for c0088 and is reported as pending (`docs/altium-kit.md`, c0091).
- A model with a board can be written as Altium documents; RT-A2 compares footprints and copper, and the new level RT-A3 measures an import, write and re-import over the public corpus. `AltiumBackend().write(design)` (and `fenolite.lens.altium.write_model` for a design read from a KiCad board, whose rounded pads need KiCad's corner ratio) writes the PCB document from the model alone and a schematic generated from the circuit; what the writers do not carry is counted per kind and reported as `altium.not-lowered`, and a loss of copper, pads or footprints is refused (`FEN-7001`) without `allow_lossy`. The write is experimental and is not in the capability report. An Altium build now stores the board it wrote in `.fenolite/board.json` (footprints with their pads, tracks, arcs, vias, zones), so `roundtrip.rta2` compares every kind of the written scope and no longer has `summary.not_in_model`; a project built earlier is skipped with `model-predates-board` until it is built again. No project file of a build changes. New: the opt-in check stage `roundtrip.rta3` with the codes `check.rta3-failed` and `check.rta3-unwritten`, and `fenolite roundtrip PATH --level rta0|rta1|rta2|rta3` for Altium input. On the seven public PCB documents that are not heavy the two imports are equal inside the written scope and KiCad's importer reads each rewrite as Fenolite does; a rewrite is not a copy: footprint graphics, bodies, shapes on copper and poured fills are not in it, and `docs/evidence/altium-roundtrip.md` gives the counts (c0090).
- A footprint of the model holds its graphics and free texts, and a rounded pad its corner ratio (c0126): `FootprintInstance.graphics`, `FootprintInstance.texts` (in the pad frame) and `Pad.corner_ratio` (parts per million of the pad's shorter side; `Design.validate()` reports `model.corner-ratio`). `fenolite diff` compares them as the kinds `footprint_graphic` and `footprint_text`. **Changed: the Altium import maps the tracks, arcs, fills, regions and texts of a component to its footprint (the designator and the comment become the fields `Reference` and `Value`), so `altium.import.unmapped` no longer lists `footprint-graphics` on a normal document and an imported `board.json` is larger** (the own samples grow by 41 % to 76 %; measured on the public corpus: owed). **Changed: a rewrite of an Altium board (`AltiumBackend().write`, RT-A3) keeps the lines, arcs, fills, regions, designator and comment of its footprints on the overlays and on Mechanical 1 to 16**; a graphic on copper is counted as `footprint-copper`, a loss that needs `allow_lossy`. `fenolite build --target altium` places the footprints into the model and goes through the same lowering (`lower.from_design` with `lower.LowerOptions`); every project file it writes keeps its bytes, and only `.fenolite/board.json` changes: it holds the footprint graphics and corner ratios that were written (the ten `ALTIUM` pins of the pinned builds moved for that reason alone). RT-A2 and RT-A3 compare the footprint graphics and the corner ratio; a project built before this change is skipped with `model-predates-graphics`. No KiCad output changes: a design read from KiCad holds the fields only after `backends.kicad.fpitems.with_footprint_items`. A model document of 0.2.0 reads as it is; **0.2.0 cannot read a model document that carries the new keys** (`graphics` and `texts` of a footprint, `corner_ratio` of a pad). `lens.altium.pcb_document` and `lens.altium.corner_ratios` are removed.

### Changed

- **The default output of an Altium build changes** (c0086): the Altium schematic draws each symbol's own graphics, follows the module tree at any depth, and carries port directions, buses, parameters and text outside ASCII. `--altium-symbols generic` gives the former output (one rectangle per part), byte for byte; `graphics` is the default by the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-06. New options `--altium-symbols generic|graphics` and `--altium-directions on|off`, new `result.schematic`, new info `altium.bus-flattened`. With `--altium-sheets modules` a nested module now gets its own sheet `<name>_<module path with ".">.SchDoc` instead of being drawn on the sheet of its top-level module; `flat` stays the default. Symbols and footprints from the catalog, and symbols the script authored, now build for `--target altium`. The properties of a part become hidden parameters. In the binary form a comment or a parameter value may hold Windows-1252 characters. The committed samples `blink`, `kicad_example`, `no_connect` and `routed` were regenerated; their earlier bytes, which the author reports covered, are kept under `tests/data/altium/generic/`. Nothing of this has been opened in Altium yet (Part Y of `docs/evidence/altium-schematic.md`); KiCad's importer reads the written library graphics as the model holds them.
- **`fenolite check` on Altium input runs two more stages by default, and can exit 5 where it exited 0** (c0088): `copper.clearance` (shorts, clearance and zone overlaps of the PCB document, with the Clearance rules the document holds) and `parity` (the PCB document against its schematic documents: references, values, footprints and nets), both without any tool. `--stages` selects the former set. The stage says what it could not judge and then carries `UNVERIFIED`: unpoured polygons, internal planes, Clearance rules outside the rule table, and filled zones that no clearance applies to (a pour is never judged against a default the document does not hold). `fenolite parity` takes an Altium project, project folder or PCB document. `fenolite build --target altium` judges the copper of the PCB document it is about to write and refuses a board with a short (exit 5, nothing written); `--copper-check warn` writes it, and the option is no usage error with that target any more. The result of an Altium build gains `copper_check`. The copper check judges no board-edge clearance on either backend. For Altium input only, each clearance rule is judged 5 nm lower, because a distance of an Altium document can read up to 4 nm closer after the unit conversion; KiCad boards are judged exactly as in 0.2.0.
- **`fenolite check` on an Altium PCB document can judge clearance where it judged shorts only, and exit 5 where it exited 0** (c0125): the Altium rule table reads more forms of a Clearance record. A blank object matrix and one whose every entry equals the rule's value are one clearance; the records of a clearance matrix between all net classes (`ISMATRIX` and the cell keys) are read; `ExistsOnLayer` of every copper layer of the board on both scopes gives a clearance rule with `layers`, and `OnMid` on a board without an internal signal layer applies to nothing (new reason `no-layer`, which is no unread rule). A matrix of differing clearances stays unmapped and its reason now counts the differing entries instead of quoting the matrix; the option that ignores the pads of one footprint, a layer condition for some of the copper layers and every other scope function stay unmapped too. The forms are read, never written. **On the public corpus this lifts ONE of the three PCB documents that had no clearance in force**, to the level `INFERRED`: the one whose records are a clearance matrix on a two-layer board, the only forms a neutral rule says exactly. The two others stay `UNVERIFIED` (`docs/evidence/altium-roundtrip.md`, "Light DRC over the corpus"). `map_rules`, `rulemap.lift` and `import_rules` take the board's copper layers (`layers=`); KiCad boards and every written file are unchanged.
- **The Altium import reads a Clearance matrix cell by cell, and the stage summary of a document check has one more key** (c0130): a Clearance record whose object matrix holds differing clearances gives its generic rule and one more clearance rule per cell (`<rule>/<kind>-<kind>`, with `item_kind` selectors for `track`, `pad`, `via` and `zone`), when the matrix is exact in those kinds; a matrix that tells an arc from a track or a through-hole pad from a surface pad stays unmapped and its reason names the pairs. Cells for fills, regions, text and holes are kept in the rule's bag. `summary.clearance_cells` (`judged`, `unjudged`) counts them, from the new `DesignRules.clearance_cells`. **On the public corpus this lifts one record, on the heavy document, which stays `UNVERIFIED`; the two other documents with such a matrix are not moved** (their matrices are not exact, and they carry the option that ignores the pads of one footprint). The measurement of that heavy document also found 28 false shorts of the import (vias without a pad on inner layers), listed on `docs/evidence/altium-roundtrip.md`. Read only; KiCad boards and every written file are unchanged.
- **The canonical model of an imported Altium project changes when a footprint model lists several pads for a pin** (c0123): the pads are pairs of `pin_pad_map`, no longer `pin_pads` entries of the extension bag, and `altium.import.pin-map` counts only a record without a pad and a pad that another pin holds. On the public project with such records the assignment comparison covers four more elements.
- **The output job that `fenolite build --target altium` writes now holds the Gerber settings, and every written `.OutJob` differs from the one an earlier build wrote** (c0138). No release wrote an output job (0.2.x writes none; the job is unreleased v0.3 work), so this concerns folders built from the development branch. **A rebuild into such a folder replaces the job and keeps the old one as `<name>.OutJob.bak`**, as it does for every other file the build wrote; it is refused instead (exit 7, `FEN-7001`, nothing written) only when the job was edited since that build or when the folder's `.fenolite/` record is gone, and then `--discard-layout` replaces it, again with the backup. **A rebuild of a folder built by 0.2.x adds the output job to the project file, keeping `<name>.PrjPcb.bak`**: a project file that is still as a build wrote it and does not list the job is written again with the job listed, so that Altium's Projects panel shows it; a project file that was changed since (Altium saved it, or you edited it), or that the folder's `.fenolite/` record does not know, is kept as before, whatever `--discard-layout`, with the info `altium.outjob-not-listed` and how to list the job. Why: run in Altium Designer 26, the job as written before produced drill, pick-and-place, bill-of-materials and print files and no Gerber layer file, because a Gerber output without a settings record plots nothing. The Gerber output now carries the complete record of 44 fields that the public saved jobs hold: millimetres, the decimals of the export preset's `gerbers.precision` (4 without a preset), and as plotted layers the layers of the board (silkscreen, paste, solder mask, the copper layers from top to bottom with a plane at its place, Mechanical 13 to 16). Every output, of every kind, gains the key `OutputDefault<i>=0`; no other output kind gains a settings record. **No board outline is plotted yet: the Gerber set of the written job holds no outline file**, because the PCB document holds the outline as the board shape and on no layer; `result.outjob.gerber.outline` says so, and you turn the outline on in the Gerber setup in Altium. `result.outjob` gains `gerber` (`unit`, `decimals`, `layers`, `outline`) and `result.outjob.defaults` no longer lists `gerbers.precision`. `outjob.from_preset` takes the board's copper stack (`copper=`), `write_outjob` refuses a Gerber output without the whole record, and `read_outjob` gives the settings of an output (`JobOutput.settings`, `record_fields`). Altium has not opened such a job yet: every row is `INFERRED` until Part O of the second Altium session is reported; the committed sample `tests/data/altium/outjob/blink.OutJob` changes (eight more lines).
- **The notes about a kept Altium project file name only what the file lacks** (c0138). A project file that exists in `--out` and is kept is now read with Fenolite's own project reader: `altium.pcb-not-in-project`, `altium.schlib-not-in-project`, `altium.sheets-not-in-project` and `altium.outjob-not-listed` are given only for documents the kept file really does not list, and each names those documents alone. Before, the first three were given on every rebuild into a folder with a project file, even one that listed everything. A kept file that cannot be read gets them as before, with a hint that says so.
- **`.fenolite/build.json` of an Altium build keeps a kept project file that is still as a build wrote it** (c0138). A rebuild that keeps `<name>.PrjPcb` (nothing has to change in it, or the output job is off) records the file again when its bytes are the ones the folder's record held, so that a later build still knows it as built and can add the output job to it. Before, a kept project file dropped out of the record at the first rebuild. A project file that is kept because it was changed, or that had no record, is not recorded. The record's file list is no list of what a run wrote: `result.files` and the receipt are, and they do not name a kept file.
- **An Altium board with internal planes is imported with fewer tracks.** A plane layer is stored in negative, so a line, an arc, a fill, a region or a text drawn on it cuts the plane and is no copper; the import made a track without a net of each such line. It now makes no entity of them, with or without a net: `altium.import.unmapped` counts them as `plane-cuts`, and the layer of the plane holds the count in its `altium` bag (`plane_cuts`). The ids of the other imported items do not change. The copper check on Altium input no longer filters those lines out of its view and reports the planes as before (`copper.item-unsupported` at `plane`); `equivalent --level 5` no longer counts them as copper on no net, and a rewrite of such a board (RT-A3) no longer writes them as tracks. On the two public documents with planes the import holds 74 and 43 tracks fewer, the number KiCad's own import of them holds (c0124).
- The Altium import keeps the holes of a poured region: copper inside a hole of a pour is no longer joined to it. A zone fill of an imported document is one ring that holds the outline and its holes, the form of a KiCad filled polygon (new `geometry.keyhole_ring`), so the copper check no longer reports tracks and vias in a hole as shorts and `equivalent --level 5` finds the islands of a pour. A hole outside its outline is dropped and reported by the new warning `altium.import.zone-hole-outside`. Imported boards whose pours have holes get other fill polygons than before; boards without holes are unchanged (c0122).
- **The counts of `fenolite roundtrip --level rta3` and of the stage `roundtrip.rta3` change on a document that holds vias whose drill equals their diameter** (c0128): such vias are now written by the trip and leave `unwritten.via`; no verdict changes. `AltiumBackend().write(design, rewrite=True)` (also `lower.write_design` and `lower.from_design`) is the caller's statement that the design is the reading of an Altium document and that the write gives it back; a via whose drill equals its diameter, which a saved document can hold, is then written as it was read. Without the argument nothing changes: `fenolite build --target altium`, `write_model` and a plain `write` refuse or leave out such a via as before, the committed samples keep their bytes, and `rewrite=True` is refused for a board that was not read from an Altium document. On the public document with 48 such vias the rewrite holds all 242 vias, and KiCad's importer reads it as Fenolite does.
- **The content of an imported Altium board with arcs changes** (c0127): an arc that is read from an arc record keeps the record's own centre, radius and angles in its `altium` bag (the pair `arc`, on an `Arc` and on a graphic of kind `arc`), and the write of a model (`AltiumBackend().write`, RT-A3) gives that record back while it still says the arc's three points within 2 nm. No id changes; a design read from KiCad never holds the pair; a build from a script writes the same bytes. An arc that was moved in the model is written from its points, as before, and an arc with a kept record is no longer refused when its three points lie on one line. On the heavy public document 7 of 517 arcs came back more than 2 nm away and 12 were not written; all 529 are now written and equal, RT-A3 holds on the eight public PCB documents, and `H-A-VER-RTA3` is `CORPUS-VERIFIED`. That level means that Fenolite reads its own rewrite of a public Altium PCB document back to an equal model inside the written scope, and that KiCad's importer reads the rewrite as Fenolite does at levels 1 to 5; it says nothing about Altium opening a written file (that stays `INFERRED` until the kit run of c0091 and c0092), and the scope leaves out footprint graphics (until c0126), component bodies (until c0121) and the items counted as not written in `docs/evidence/altium-roundtrip.md`. The stage `roundtrip.rta3` still reports `INFERRED`, no round-trip cell of the matrix is set, and the write stays experimental.
- The slack of the copper check on Altium input is a stated rule (c0131): one file unit (1/10 000 mil, 2.54 nm) per item of the pair, 5.08 nm for a pair, held as 5 whole nanometres and never rounded up. **No finding changes: the rule gives the 5 nm the check already used**, for Altium input only; KiCad boards are judged strictly at the nanometre, as in 0.2.0. The derivation (how far the conversion to nanometres moves a track, a via, a pad, a pour and an arc) is in `docs/formats/altium/import.md`. On the eight public PCB documents the slack removes 6 075 findings, all 1 to 4 nm short; 25 findings that are 8 to 20 nm short stay errors, and nothing is allow-listed. The rule is slightly tighter than the worst case of the conversion for a pad against a rectangular pad (5.55 to 6.36 nm against 5.08 nm) and is not widened, for lack of a public pair that shows it.
- **The JSON result of every `fenolite build --target altium` gains the key `pcb`** (c0121, repairing c0085 to its own spec): `result.pcb` holds `written`, `not_lowered` and the new `bodies`, after `copper`, and is `null` without a PCB document. The contract described the key since c0085, but only the library's build summary held it and the command never returned it. No written file changes. An Altium build writes the extruded component bodies of a board on request (`--altium-bodies extruded`, off by default); bodies that name a 3D model are reported. With the option, each extruded body of a footprint that has an outline and a height above its standoff becomes one record in both body storages of the PCB document, owned by its component, and the bodies of a footprint definition go into the PCB library; the write of a model takes the same choice (`bodies="extruded"` on `AltiumBackend.write`, `lens.altium.write_model` and `AltiumBackend.model_roundtrip`), and RT-A2 and RT-A3 then compare the bodies that were written. Without the option every file of a build is the file of before, byte for byte, and no body is ever made for a footprint that has none. **Experimental, and the evidence is thin**: the keys of the record were measured on 1272 saved bodies of five public documents of three repositories, 1265 of them from one repository; two keys (`MODELID`, `MODEL.CHECKSUM`) have no known rule and hold stand-ins; and no written body has been opened in Altium, so the option stays off until step X8 of the author report is in. New sample `tests/data/altium/body2/`.
- **Catalog symbols show a pin name only where it can be read, so some symbols look different** (c0134). The transistors, thyristors and photodetectors, `Schottky_Diode`, `Potentiometer`, `Transformer`, `Switch_SPDT`, `Pushbutton_NO`, `Relay_SPDT`, `Dual_LED_Common_Cathode`, `Optocoupler`, `Bridge_Rectifier`, `Operational_Amplifier` and `Comparator` no longer draw their pin names, which lay on each other and on the drawing in KiCad and in Altium; the amplifiers mark their two inputs with a plus and a minus stroke, the bridge its four terminals, the optocoupler its emitter. `Linear_Regulator`, `Offline_Power_Controller`, `Microcontroller` and `Power_Module` keep their names in a larger box, `Schottky_Diode` has the longer pins of `Diode`, and `Capacitor_Polarized` and `Capacitor_Electrolytic` carry their plus mark below the lead, clear of the pin number. In the example library `FenoliteDemo.kicad_sym`, `CONN2` no longer shows its pin numbers a second time as names, `MCU8` and `DUAL_OPAMP` are larger, `MCU8` has longer pins further apart with its names clear of its clock wedge and its numbers clear of its inversion bubbles, and `R_V` has longer pins. A design that uses one of these symbols gets a different schematic on its next build, once: the symbol's look, and for the enlarged ones the places of its pins. Pin numbers, pin names, nets, designators and the board do not change. A test now measures the rule on every symbol Fenolite authors, with the marks KiCad draws for inverted and clock pins, at KiCad's text size and at an estimate of Altium's that nobody has measured in Altium yet.
