- **The two LED lands of the catalog are documented as cathode-first** (c0144): `LED0603_Kingbright_APT1608SURCK` and `LED0805_Kingbright_APT2012SURCK` number their pads as the manufacturer does, pad 1 the cathode, while the symbol `Fenolite:LED` has pin 1 anode. `docs/catalog/sources.md` and `docs/catalog/coverage.md` now say, as they do for `SOD128_Nexperia_CFP5`, that the pair needs `pad_map={"1": "2", "2": "1"}`; without it the anode's net lies on the cathode's pad. Check a design of 0.2.x that uses one of these lands: nothing in a build changes, the footprints and their summaries are the same. The four kit samples that used the pair now carry the map and connect the LED by pin name, so their schematics draw it the right way round and their boards keep every pad's net.
- **The catalog applies a default pin-to-pad map to a diode or LED on a cathode-first land** (c0147). A part of `Fenolite:LED`, `Diode`, `Zener_Diode`, `Schottky_Diode` or `Photodiode` (pin 1 anode, pin 2 cathode) on `LED0603_Kingbright_APT1608SURCK`, `LED0805_Kingbright_APT2012SURCK` or `SOD128_Nexperia_CFP5` (pad 1 the cathode) that gives no `pad_map` now gets `{"1": "2", "2": "1"}` in the KiCad and the Altium build alike: the anode pin on the anode pad, the cathode pin on the cathode pad. An explicit `pad_map` always wins, and a design that authors the symbol or the land itself gets no default. **A design that uses one of these lands without a `pad_map` and wired the LED or diode by pin number gets its two pad nets swapped on the next build, and the new warning `build.pad-map-default` names the part, the land and the map.** Connect the part by pin name (`A`, `K`) and write `pad_map={"1": "2", "2": "1"}` to silence the warning, or write `pad_map={"1": "1", "2": "2"}` to keep the pad order of earlier builds. A design that gives a map, and every part on another land, builds the same files as before; the kit samples carry their map since c0144.
- The help of `build --altium-symbols` says “the output of 0.2.0 and earlier” where it said “the output before v0.4” (c0136).
- Development: the release record of 0.3.0 and its guard (`docs/release/v0.3.md`, change c0150). The milestone names of c0136: the write side of the second backend, which these pages called v0.4 before 2026-10-07, is the second part of v0.3, and v0.4 names the proposals still on other branches (the agent track, board authoring, the complex board); the record of 0.2.0 and the archived changes keep the names of their day. The proposals c0083–c0092, c0121, c0126 and c0138 were written before their code. The maintainer's Altium Designer 26 reports of 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08 are recorded on the evidence pages as author reports, which never move a write out of `experimental`: the written projects open and compile; the output job with the Gerber settings record produces every output, Gerber layer files among them; the sheets of a repeated-sheet sample are no longer black, and the bus of a `Repeat` entry does not split into the channels (an open defect); on one public board Altium's own rule check passes seven clearance pairs that Fenolite reports 8 to 9 nm short, so Fenolite's check is the stricter one there. No kit run was recorded; the maintainer's decision on the kit and the revalidation it owes are in `docs/evidence/altium-kit/README.md`. Archived the completed specs of c0089 (level 5 of `equivalent`) and c0122 (zone holes of the Altium import), with the full suite and the CI run cited in their closing tasks.

### Fixed

- **A design that names parts of the built-in catalog and declares a `Power(...)` interface builds for KiCad** (c0143; the same fix is released for the 0.2 series as 0.2.2). Since the release 0.2.0 such a design failed to build with `build.vendor-unsafe-name` ("symbol libraries 'Fenolite' and 'fenolite' differ only in letter case"), so a catalog regulator, comparator or amplifier fed from a connector could not pass KiCad's ERC. The power flag of such a design now lies in the catalog's library (`Fenolite:PWR_FLAG` in `lib/Fenolite.kicad_sym`), and ERC takes the supply as driven. A design without catalog parts keeps `lib/fenolite.kicad_sym`, and no file of a design that built before changes. A design that needs a flag and has a symbol `PWR_FLAG` of its own in such a library is refused with `build.reserved-library`.
- **Altium schematic files written by 0.3.0 now show and hide pin names and numbers as intended in Altium** (c0148). Fenolite wrote a pin's `PINCONGLOMERATE` with bits 0x08 and 0x10 as "name shown" and "number shown" and without bit 0x20, which every pin Altium saves holds; Altium Designer 26 reads the two bits as hide flags when 0x20 is clear, so it showed the names that a symbol meant hidden and hid the numbers (on the catalog LED the two names crossed over and the part looked reversed; its pads and pin map were right). **Changed: every pin of every Altium schematic document and library that a build writes now holds bit 0x20**, with 0x08 and 0x10 as show flags, the form that Altium Designer 26.5 showed as meant (author report of 2026-10-08 on the new check project `tests/data/altium/pinbits/`; 4 175 of 4 175 pins of the public corpus hold 0x20). Files written by 0.2.x show names that were meant hidden: rebuild them. The schematic reader reads the two bits as show flags with 0x20 and as hide flags without it (`Pin.name_shown`, `Pin.designator_shown`); the import's model does not change. Every committed Altium schematic sample and the ten `ALTIUM` pins of the pinned builds moved for this reason alone (measured record by record, `tests/_pin_bits.py`); no KiCad output changes.
- **`fenolite check` on an Altium PCB document no longer reports a short where a via has no pad** (c0132): Altium's "Remove Unused Pad Shapes" takes the pad of a via off the layers on which nothing touches it, the via record names those layers (a table of thirty-two bytes at offset 209, read by `read.pcbprims.via_pad_removed`), and the import keeps them in the via's `altium` bag as the pair `pad_removed`. The copper check on Altium input judges such a via with its diameter where it has a pad and with its drill as diameter where it has none: a pour around the hole is no finding, and copper inside the hole still is a short. On the one public document that holds such vias (123 of 1 770) the 28 false shorts and one false clearance finding are gone and no other finding changes. `summary.items.via` of the stage counts the parts in which such a via is judged, and a rewrite (`AltiumBackend().write`, RT-A3) writes the via with a pad on every layer and counts it under the new kind `via-pad-shape`. The model, the ids of imported items, every KiCad output and the judging of KiCad boards are unchanged; the meaning of the bytes is `INFERRED` (`H-A-IMP-VIA-PADLESS`), and nine further bytes of the long via record stay unexplained and unread.
- The font table of an Altium schematic holds each distinct font once (c0146). A drawing sheet declared the fonts of its texts without looking at the sheet's own font, so a sheet with a text of 10 points held Times New Roman 10 twice; a copy that Altium Designer 26 saved held it once (one file, observed on 2026-10-07). Such a text now names font 1. The sample of the `Repeat` statement that the import's tests read (`tests/data/altium/channels/two/`) is written by the schematic writer itself, after its first files, which held no colour at all, showed as a black page in Altium; the import reads the same circuit from it. KiCad output is not touched. **Changed: the schematic documents of `fenolite build --target altium` with a drawing sheet, and the `.SchDot` of `fenolite template build --target altium`, differ from those an earlier build wrote when the sheet holds a text of 10 points (both shipped sheet examples do): `FONTIDCOUNT` is one lower and the labels of the frame are renumbered. A build without a drawing sheet is byte-identical.**
- **`fenolite build --target altium --copper-from` accepts a KiCad board whose bottom-side footprint has pads off its X axis** (c0142). The check compared the pads as the board stores them, mirrored about the footprint's X axis on the bottom (`H-G-BOTTOM-STORE`), with the library footprint, so such a board was refused with `altium.copper-board-mismatch` ("the pads of … differ"), with or without a `pad_map`, and so was script copper on such a part; a board whose bottom footprint was stored unmirrored was accepted. The mirror is now undone before the comparison, at any rotation. No written file changes.

## [0.2.2] - 2026-10-08

### Fixed

- A design that names parts of the built-in catalog and declares a `Power(...)` interface builds for KiCad (c0143). In the releases 0.2.0 and 0.2.1 such a design failed to build with `build.vendor-unsafe-name` ("symbol libraries 'Fenolite' and 'fenolite' differ only in letter case"), so a catalog regulator, comparator or amplifier fed from a connector could not pass KiCad's ERC. The power flag of such a design now lies in the catalog's library (`Fenolite:PWR_FLAG` in `lib/Fenolite.kicad_sym`), and ERC takes the supply as driven. A design without catalog parts keeps `lib/fenolite.kicad_sym`, and no file of a design that built before changes. A design that needs a flag and has a symbol `PWR_FLAG` of its own in such a library is refused with `build.reserved-library`.

### Changed

- Development: the second patch release of the series. It is cut from the tag `v0.2.1`, not from `dev`, and holds this one fix, carried back from `dev`; `docs/release/v0.2.md` records it under “Patch releases” (c0149). Archived the completed specs of c0143 and c0149.

## [0.2.1] - 2026-10-07

### Fixed

- `build --target altium` applies `pad_map` (c0135). In the releases 0.1.0 and 0.2.0 an Altium build ignored a `pad_map` that renames pads: the PCB document carried each net on the pad of the pin's own number (for `pad_map={"1": "2", "2": "1"}`, the two nets exchanged), and the schematic said nothing of the map; the KiCad build of the same script was right. Now each net is on the mapped pad of the PCB document, the schematic and its library hold the map in the footprint model (one record for each pin whose pad is not the pad of its own number), and script copper and `--copper-from` are checked against the mapped pads. A `pad_map` that names a pin the symbol lacks or a pad the footprint lacks, or that leaves one pad to two pins (`pad_map={"1": "2"}` on a part that has a pin 2), is refused with the new error `altium.pin-pad-map-invalid` (exit 5, nothing written), as the KiCad build refuses it; 0.2.0 built such a script. A script without a `pad_map` builds the same files, byte for byte.
  - **If you built a script with a `pad_map` for Altium with 0.1.0 or 0.2.0, build it again.** The PCB document, the schematic and its library change; the files they replace are kept beside them as `.bak`. A PCB document that was edited since it was built is not replaced: the build stops with exit 7, and `--discard-layout` replaces it (the edited file is kept as `.bak`), or build into another folder. A board that was laid out from the old PCB document has the nets of the mapped pads on the wrong pads: check it against the rebuilt one.
  - `fenolite check` on such a project still reports `netlist.assignment-differs` and exits 5: this release compares the schematic by pin number and the board by pad number. The written files are right; the comparison learns the map in 0.3.0.

### Changed

- Development: the first patch release of the series. It is cut from the tag `v0.2.0`, not from `dev`, and holds this one fix; `docs/release/v0.2.md` records it under “Patch releases” (c0133). Archived the completed specs of c0133 and c0135.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-10-06

### Added

- v0.2 acceptance: the schematic of a built project judged by KiCad's own ERC, netlist and parity test on 9.0 and 10.0, round trips of the demo schematics, the BOM and placement tables, the project manifest, the inspection commands, the complete layout lens, six more rule kinds and Fenolite's own parity check; the release record `docs/release/v0.2.md` names the test behind each item, its limits and what else is in the package (c0093).
- `fenolite build` writes a schematic (`<name>.kicad_sch`, symbol libraries and `sym-lib-table`) that KiCad's ERC and parity test accept on 9.0 and 10.0; pads of unconnected pins and net names with a slash are written as KiCad stores them; `--schematic skip` keeps the previous output (c0061). A sheet holds every unit of every part, a global label per connected pin, a no-connect flag per `no_connect` mark and a power flag per supply net that nothing drives; `schematic-placements.toml` beside the script fixes symbol positions, and an edited schematic is replaced with a warning and a backup (`docs/schematic.md`). New `build` issue codes are listed in `docs/cli-contract.md`.
- The schematic gets one sheet per module and puts 2-pin parts beside the IC pins they connect to, joined by a wire; `--schematic-layout grid` writes one flat sheet. Each module with a part is written to `sheets/<module path>.kicad_sch` and named by a box without pins on the sheet above it; nets cross sheets by their global labels, so net names stay those of the script, and a footprint of a part in a module carries the path of its sheets. `fenolite netlist --source fenolite` and the build's netlist guard read the sheet tree and the wires, so a sheet that KiCad would drop in silence stops the build. `fenolite parity` and the `parity` stage of `check` read the sheet tree with Fenolite's own netlist, so a project with module sheets needs no tool there either (c0070). The Reference and the Value of a turned or mirrored symbol are written level and centred, clear of the labels at its pins.
- KiCad schematics are read into a typed sheet (symbols, labels, no-connect flags, sheet references, embedded symbols) with every other child kept in place: `fenolite.backends.kicad.sch.read_schematic`, `rebuild_schematic`, `roundtrip_schematic`, `sheet_files`, `components` and `hierarchy_components`, and the model `fenolite.model.schematic.SchematicSheet` with schema `fenolite.schematic.v0`. Round trips are proved over the demo schematics: RT0 and RT1 pass on the 125 readable demo rows and on two third-party schematics re-saved by KiCad 10 (`H-K-SCH-READ`, `H-K-SCH-RT1`), and the components read from 34 demo projects on 10.0.6 and 31 on 9.0.9 equal those of `kicad-cli sch export netlist` (`H-K-SCH-COMPONENTS-2`). The token fuzz has load checks for schematics and symbol libraries, and the token inventory covers schematic and symbol-library tokens: 49 rows and one form row of kinds `kicad_sch` and `kicad_sym`, each proved by the fuzz on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (`H-K-SCH-TOKENS`); a symbol-library row also matches inside a schematic. (c0060).
- `fenolite check` runs KiCad's ERC (`erc.kicad`) on the project's schematic and reports schematic parity findings of the DRC run (c0062). ERC violations are `kicad.erc.<type>` issues located as `REF-PIN`, `REF` or a label text; parity entries are `kicad.drc.<type>` issues, and `kicad.drc.parity-unchecked` says when KiCad could not compare the board with its schematic. The copy of a project that `kicad-cli` sees now holds the schematic, its sheet files, `sym-lib-table` and the project's symbol libraries. In a manifest, a sheet, a symbol library and the symbol table reach `native-verified` through that stage.
- `fenolite netlist`: components and nets of a KiCad project as JSON, from `kicad-cli` or from Fenolite's own reading of a generated schematic; `build` refuses a schematic whose netlist differs from the circuit; `check` compares the schematic's netlist with the model and the board (c0063). The own reading equals the export of `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 on the examples and on 25 generated designs.
- New command `parity` and `check` stage `parity`: schematic against board and symbol pins against footprint pads, without KiCad for generated projects, cross-checked with KiCad's parity test when it runs. Its counts per type equal KiCad's on the public demos of 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (c0072).
- `fenolite bom` and `fenolite pnp`: the bill of materials and the placement table of a project as neutral tables, rendered to CSV through a user-written column template (names, order, grouping, units, origin, side names, rotation rules); no template of any assembly service ships. The bill has two sources: the model (`--source model`) and `kicad`, the default, whose parts come from `kicad-cli sch export bom` on a copy of the project's schematic, one row per reference, and Fenolite groups them under the user's column template. Options `--kicad-cli` and `--timeout`; code `bom.field-unsupported`; `counts.left_out` is `null` for this source. Measured on KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: the export's rows (`H-K-BOM-CSV`), the parts of a built model equal to KiCad's (`H-K-BOM-MODEL`) and the placement rows (`H-K-POS-ROWS`) are `KICAD-VERIFIED` (c0064; guide `docs/assembly.md`).
- `fenolite manifest`: a project manifest with the SHA-256 and a state (`generated`, `checked`, `roundtrip-ok`, `native-verified`) for every design file and artefact, and `--verify` to compare it with the files on disk; `--manifest` on `export`, `render`, `bom` and `pnp` now merges into the folder's manifest instead of replacing it. Each entry says in `held` what its next state is missing. A state is read from the stages of `check` and never says more than they do: a board is `native-verified` when KiCad's DRC reports no error, a file made from it stops at `checked`, and a schematic, its symbol libraries and their table are `native-verified` when KiCad's ERC reports no error. Manifests written by v0.1 are still read (c0065; guide `docs/exports.md`).
- New commands `diff`, `roundtrip`, `fmt`, `explain`, `restore`, `net`, `region` and `neighbors`; `--limit` and `--cursor` page a command's main list, `--format concise` keeps one issue per code; receipts carry an `id` and the `undo` command (c0066). `diff` lists the exact differences between two boards, libraries or built models (a moved footprint is one change) and has a tree view of two KiCad files; `roundtrip` says whether a file survives RT0, RT1 and, through `kicad-cli`, RT2; `fmt` writes Fenolite's canonical print, a fixed point over the corpus; `explain` knows every error and issue code; `restore` puts back the backups of a confirmed write and deletes nothing. `diff` compares two schematics in its model view (`diff_sheets`: symbols by reference and unit, sheet references and embedded symbols by name, labels and no-connect flags by content), and `roundtrip` reaches RT1 on a schematic through the schematic reader's rebuild. The fixed point of `fmt` is measured over the 132 schematic rows of the corpus as well. `roundtrip --level rt2` also judges the schematic of a project (`result.rt2.schematic`): KiCad's ERC on the project and on Fenolite's re-dump of its sheets must give the same kinds of violations, the sheet, type, severity and exclusion of each (`ErcReport.kinds()`), and `exact` says whether the items agreed too. The item KiCad names for one violation can differ between two runs on one unchanged project, on 9.0.9 and on 10.0.6, so the items are not compared; a project on which the kinds do not repeat is not judged.
- `capabilities` lists `result.matrix`: for each backend and file kind, the evidence level of `detect`, `read`, `write`, `roundtrip_exact` and `roundtrip_modified`, the hypotheses behind it and the operations that are experimental. Every backend module now declares its evidence, and `docs/evidence/matrix.md` is generated from the declarations (`tools/gen_evidence_matrix.py`, with `--check`). A backend module without a declaration fails `tests/unit/backends/test_evidence_declared.py`, whose message says what to add, and every declared level is held to `docs/hypotheses.md`. The KiCad backend lists the write kind `kicad_lib_table`, and the Altium schematic reader's evidence no longer names the refuted `H-A-RD-SCH-TEXT` (c0067).
- `moved()` takes module paths and `moved_net()` renames nets without losing their routing; a footprint renamed through an alias keeps its KiCad edits (its uuids and its group memberships follow the new path); `fenolite sync --to-source` writes `placements.toml`, which `build` reads beside the script, for both targets and with `--discard-layout` too; `sync --check` fails when the committed file is stale, and `sync` reports what the next build would drop (`sync.orphan`, `sync.net-dropped`, `sync.value-differs`). `sync` also writes `schematic-placements.toml` from the symbols moved in KiCad's schematic editor, and reports a symbol it cannot hold with `sync.symbol-off-grid` (c0069).
- Six more rule kinds (hole to hole, hole clearance, annular width, courtyard, silkscreen, creepage) in the model, the `.kicad_dru` writer and reader, and `design.rules.rule()` with selectors from `fenolite.dsl.select`. Each kind is written only for the KiCad majors that enforce it: creepage rules are written for KiCad 10 only, and a build for KiCad 9 refuses them unless `--allow-lossy` drops them. A hand-written rule of one of these kinds is now read into the model instead of being kept opaque (c0071).
- Exact `Quantity` values in the DSL (`ohm("4k7")`, `farad("100n")`), typed `I2C`, `SPI`, `UART` and `USB2` interfaces with `attach` by role, and build warnings for differential-pair names KiCad does not pair (`build.diff-pair-name`) and for I2C lines without a pull-up (`build.i2c-pullup-missing`). A `Part` value given as a quantity is stored as its canonical text (`4.7kΩ`). KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 pair two net names that are equal except for a last `P`/`N` or `+`/`-` (c0073).
- `design.sheet()` and `design.title_block()` put the user's drawing sheet and title block on the board, and on the schematic when a build writes one: the build writes `<name>.kicad_wks` from a `.kicad_wks` or `*.sheet.toml` beside the script; `fenolite export --preset` applies the user's fab options from a TOML file; board outlines join endpoints closer than 10 µm and include footprint edge items, so all 21 demo boards close; stitching avoids rule areas that forbid vias and the board edge. KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 close an outline across a gap below 10 µm (c0074).
- New in the DSL: `Part.zone_connection`, `arc_to`, and `kind=` for vias; `build` reports `result.copper.arcs` and `result.preserved.pad_zones`. New command `pads` (c0068).
- DSL symbol authoring: design-attached `Symbol` definitions resolve custom component pins and are written as project-local KiCad symbol libraries with a `sym-lib-table` (c0058).
- Footprint DSL: `Footprint.pad(..., shared=True)` can author additional physical pads with the same number as an earlier pad, with stable unique IDs; accidental duplicates still fail.
- An offline catalog authored for Fenolite: 49 generic schematic symbols (passives, semiconductors, protection, power and control parts, electromechanical parts) and 100 footprint variants (chip and MELF parts, small-outline diodes and transistors, SOIC, TSSOP, MSOP and SSOP, QFN and DFN, QFP, DIP, 2.54 mm headers, connectors, buttons, radial and axial parts, mounting holes, a test point), with discovery through the API and `fenolite catalog`, resolution at build without a fetch, and project overrides. Every entry cites its public source; a symbol states a function and not a pinout, and a footprint is assigned in the script, never guessed. Land patterns are `INFERRED` starting points that need a review per part and process. Body outlines are on `F.Fab`, so silkscreen does not cross copper (c0075, c0076).
- Symbol definitions and DSL-authored symbols now carry ordered vector body graphics, which the KiCad symbol reader and writer preserve; the writer also keeps hidden pin-name and pin-number settings on a round trip (c0075, c0076).
- The `altium` backend reads `.PrjPcb`, `.SchDoc`, `.SchLib`, `.PcbDoc` and `.PcbLib` into the neutral model (nets from wires, labels, ports, power ports, buses and harnesses); a project read links footprints to schematic components by unique-id path and PCB nets by name. On four public Altium-saved projects from four repositories the schematic netlist equals the pad netlist of the PCB document, and the board import agrees with `kicad-cli pcb import` 10.0 (c0043).
- Model: `Circuit.buses` (`Bus`, `BusMember`), `PadstackLayer.offset`, and `ComponentBody` on footprint instances and definitions, with the findings `model.duplicate-bus-index` and `model.body-height`; all additive, the schemas are regenerated (c0043).
- `fenolite.backends.altium.read.bodies` decodes the component-body records of PCB documents and libraries byte for byte (c0043).
- Altium schematic reader: `fenolite.backends.altium.read.sch.read_schematic` reads `.SchDoc` and `.SchDot` files (binary and ASCII), and `read.schlib.read_schlib` reads `.SchLib` files into typed records with an owner tree. Unknown keys, records, streams and trailing bytes are preserved; streams rebuild byte for byte. Nine schematic and nine library rows join the corpus, and `tools/altium_census.py` reports their census (c0040).
- Altium PCB reader: lossless typed readers for Altium `.PcbDoc` and `.PcbLib` files preserve record bytes and keys, accept long and short record forms, and report located issues with strict mode available. Checked against eleven public files and KiCad CLI import/upgrade (c0041).
- Altium verification: `fenolite check` and `fenolite inspect` accept Altium documents and projects, read-only and without external tools; the round-trip levels RT-A0 (container copy), RT-A1 (records per stream) and RT-A2 (model → Altium → model on built projects, for what the built model holds: the circuit and the net classes); `fenolite diff A B` reads Altium inputs in its model view and gains a records view (`--view records`) for two Altium files of one kind; results over the public corpus in `docs/evidence/altium-roundtrip.md` (c0044).
- Design equivalence: `fenolite equivalent A [B] --level N` compares two designs at levels 1 to 4 (components, netlist as `REF-PIN` sets, footprints and pads, placement) with located differences, integer tolerances (`--tolerance-nm`, `--tolerance-udeg`), `--frame relative` and exclusion profiles; `--against kicad-import` compares Fenolite's read of a PCB document with `kicad-cli pcb import` (10.0 only) under a per-version exclusion list; results per corpus row in `docs/evidence/equivalence-triangle.md` (c0045).
- Altium sheet templates: `fenolite template import SRC --target kicad --out OUT` imports a `.SchDot` (or the template graphics of a `.SchDoc`) into the neutral drawing sheet and writes a `.kicad_wks`; size, lines, rectangles, texts, special strings and embedded PNG images are imported, everything else is reported, and a loss needs `--allow-lossy`. Writing a `.SchDot` is not included (c0046).

### Changed

- Built projects change once. The next build of a v0.1 project adds the schematic files, with new files under `sheets/` and new footprint paths for parts in modules, and renames, in the board file only, the nets of unconnected pads (`unconnected-(U1-PA1-Pad2)`) and of nets with a slash (`mod{slash}LED_A`). Layouts, positions, tracks and zone fills are kept, and no other net is renamed (c0061, c0070).
- `fenolite check`: the `erc.lite` stage is removed from KiCad checks (`--stages erc.lite` is now a usage error), and the hermetic stage list is `--stages model.validate,roundtrip` (c0062). `fenolite check` now treats a pad on no net as a block of its own, reads the `N/C` label of the IPC-D-356 export as no net, and names a model pin by its pad when the part has a `pad_map`.
- `check` and the copper guard of `build` now apply a zone's own clearance between a fill and other copper, as KiCad's DRC does, and place the copper of a pad with an offset drill where KiCad places it: boards may gain `copper.clearance` findings of source `zone` and lose false ones on such pads (`--copper-check warn` writes anyway). `BoardPad.hole` of such a pad is back at the pad's position. The facts are measured on KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (c0068).
- `fenolite capabilities` lists two backends, `altium` before `kicad`: select a backend by its `name`, not by its position (c0043).
- A component whose value is empty in the script is stored in the built model of an Altium build with the value its documents hold, the symbol's name (c0044).
- Examples: `examples/blink_2layer` marks its 29 unused pins with `no_connect` and `examples/blink_routed` marks its unused pins, so their schematics pass KiCad's ERC; the boards are unchanged, and the blink gets its resistor beside the controller pin on the sheet. `examples/blink_official` connects the analog supply pins of its microcontroller and marks its unused pins: KiCad's ERC reports nothing on it (it reported 29 errors and one warning). An authored symbol library for KiCad 9 now carries the 9.0 header version (`20241209`, was `20231120`).
- Development: Archived the completed specs of c0040 to c0046, c0060 to c0069, c0071 to c0076 and c0082, and cited their CI runs in the hypothesis register. A nightly job, `macos-app`, runs the KiCad tests on the `kicad-cli` of the 10.0.6 macOS disk image. The worksheet version oracle runs on fresh copies with its own configuration folders (c0082); the oracles of the Altium readers share one table of the corpus rows on which the Linux build of `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 fails in its own importer; a cost test of the Altium schematic reader counts calls instead of comparing times; cross-platform path assertions were repaired. None of this changes what the command line or the library does.

### Fixed

- `route` no longer hands a router the one-pad `unconnected-(…)` nets that a build gives unused pins since the schematic writer: with them Freerouting left one connection of the 40-part example open.
- A component read from an Altium PCB document takes the designator text the board shows as its reference, and its source designator only without one, so the components of a repeated sheet no longer share a reference in a board read on its own; the fault was found by the equivalence triangle (c0045).
- `fenolite bom` no longer puts a part marked "do not populate" (DNP) on the line of fitted parts of the same value: with `exclude_dnp = false` in the column template, a line is all DNP or all fitted, whether or not `group_by` names `dnp`, for both sources, so no quantity counts a part that is not fitted. A DNP line stands where its first reference puts it, like every line. With `--against`, the `key` of a DNP line ends with `DNP` (c0094; guide `docs/assembly.md`).

## [0.1.0] - 2026-10-05

### Added

- v0.1 acceptance: a second example board of forty parts, the loop proved on both examples and both KiCad majors, the agent guide `agent/SKILL.md`, the `wheel` and `dco` jobs, Windows and Python 3.11 to 3.13 in CI, and the release record `docs/release/v0.1.md`.
- Altium project reader (c0042): `fenolite.backends.altium.read` reads the project file (`.PrjPcb`: documents and their kinds, parameters, the `[Design]` options and the hierarchy mode), output jobs (`.OutJob`), rule files in the export and the summary form (`.RUL`) and stack-up files (`.stackup`), each given back byte for byte with its byte-order mark, line ends, encoding and unknown keys. `rules.map_rules` maps Clearance, Width, Routing Via Style and Hole Size records onto the neutral rules through closed tables and a closed scope grammar, and lists every other record with one of ten reasons; `project.load_project` reads a project folder and its text companions for the import. Twelve public files join the corpus (`uses = altium-text`); project files and output jobs are `CORPUS-VERIFIED`, the rest `INFERRED` (`docs/evidence/altium-project-read.md`).
- Board analyses: `fenolite analyze PATH` measures the current capacity of tracks, arcs and vias from one published fit (each constant recorded with its source in `docs/analyses.md`; `decimal` arithmetic, rounded down), the clearance of pairs of nets through air, and their creepage along the board surface, around cut-outs and around the board edge (`fenolite.analysis`). Fenolite measures and the user decides: requirements come from a user TOML file (`fenolite.requirements.v0`, integers only, a voltage table looked up without interpolation), no requirement value and no default thickness or temperature rise is shipped, and every reply is `INFERRED` or lower. Ten `analysis.*` codes; an error finding exits 5. A recorded probe on `kicad-cli` 10.0.6 shows that KiCad's `creepage` rule agrees with Fenolite around a slot (11 mm) and reports nothing for copper on opposite faces across the board edge.
- DSL footprint authoring: a design-scoped builder creates pads and graphics; exact-ID assignments resolve in KiCad and experimental Altium builds (c0055).
- DSL component pin-to-pad mapping and authored slotted drills; KiCad writes supported aligned oval drills, while Altium reports its unsupported slot geometry (c0056).
- Compound-file reader for the second backend: `fenolite.backends.altium.read.cfb` reads MS-CFB versions 3 and 4 with DIFAT sectors and the mini stream, returns storages and streams by path, reports located errors and notes, and is bounded against hostile files; `fenolite inspect FILE --streams` lists the stream tree; ten public files join the corpus and CI fetches them before corpus tests (c0039).
- Routing plugins: a router protocol with an entry-point registry, `fenolite route` with the built-in `direct` router and the KiCadRoutingTools plugin (an external MIT tool run as a subprocess at a pinned tag), routed copper merged into the board and kept by later builds (c0016).
- Design rules in the script: `design.rules.minimum(clearance=…, track_width=…, via_diameter=…, via_drill=…, hole_size=…, edge_clearance=…, netclass=None)` declares design-rule minimums for the board or for one net class. They reach `rules.json`, the custom rules of `<name>.kicad_dru` and the board-setup minimums of `<name>.kicad_pro`, and `kicad-cli pcb drc` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 enforce them (`H-K-DSL-MINIMUM`). The Altium build does not write them and reports them with `altium.not-lowered` (c0054).
- Freerouting plugin (c0023): `fenolite route --router freerouting` routes a board with Freerouting 2.4.1, a GPL-3.0 autorouter run as a subprocess with its analytics disabled (`FENOLITE_FREEROUTING_JAR` or `--router-path`, Java 25, `--router-option max-passes=N`; `docker:<image>` runs a container without a network). Fenolite writes the Specctra design file itself and reads the session back (`fenolite.backends.specctra`: `dsn.write_dsn`, `ses.read_session`, `ses.to_copper`, a codec and not a registered backend): one image per footprint, board-frame padstacks, nets and classes, keep-outs, existing copper as protected wiring. `RoutingJob.extra` carries the board-frame pads and the outline to plugins; `doctor` reports the jar and its Java; `route.option-ignored` is a new issue code. The router is listed with `sends_data_offsite: true` and needs `--allow-offsite` until its run without a network is recorded. ADR-0006 (`Proposed`) covers the facts taken from the Specctra reference.
- Zone fill: `fenolite fill` refills a board through `kicad-cli` 10 on a copy and writes the fills into the board for its own KiCad major, target 9 included; `check` gains the `zone.fill` stage; `--kicad-cli docker:<image>` runs `kicad-cli` in a container (c0015).
- Placement: `fenolite place` puts staged parts on the board in a deterministic grid or moves named parts, a legality check reports courtyard overlaps and parts outside the outline in `build` and `place`, and a placed part is kept by later builds. `fenolite place PATH` (`--strategy grid|manual`, `--move REF=X,Y[,ROT[,SIDE]]`, `--only`, `--pitch`, `--gap`, `--margin`, `--force`, `-o`) writes only with `--confirm` and refuses an illegal placement without `--force`; `fenolite.placement` holds `check` (exact courtyard overlap, outside-outline and edge-clearance tests on `PlacedExtent`s) and the grid placer; `backends.kicad.replace.move_footprint` translates a footprint or re-places it from its library definition with its uuid, fields, lock and pad nets kept; `backends.kicad.outline.board_outline` gives the outline as rings from the model or from `Edge.Cuts`; `fenolite build` reports `place.*` issues as warnings (`result.placement`) and never refuses for placement. Probes on `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: moved footprints read back at the requested position, rotation and side with their pad nets, and courtyards that only touch give no `courtyards_overlap`. `H-K-PLACE-MOVE` and `H-K-PLACE-TOUCH` are `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`, so a `place` reply carries `evidence.level` `KICAD-VERIFIED`; the outline rings stay `INFERRED` (`H-G-PLACE-OUTLINE`: 3 of 21 demo boards have an outline in KiCad and no ring here). KiCad's DRC judges the board (change c0022).
- Footprint fields: Reference, Value and every placed property of a footprint are typed fields (position, angle, layer, visibility, size, thickness, justification, mirror), read from 8.0 to 10.0 boards and written for 9.0 and 10.0; `Part.field(...)` and `fields(design)` place Reference and Value from the DSL, also outside the courtyard on a chosen side; rebuilds keep fields edited in KiCad unless the script locks them (`result.preserved.fields`); checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 (`fenolite.model.FootprintField`, `fenolite.backends.kicad.fields`, `fenolite.lens.fields`; c0030).
- Zone settings: zones carry typed settings (clearance, minimum thickness, pad connection solid/thermal/none/thru-hole-only, thermal gap and spoke width, island removal, smoothing, hatched fill), read from KiCad 9.0 and 10.0 boards and written per target; pads carry a zone connection override; `Design.zone()` declares pours in design scripts, and rebuilds keep zones edited in KiCad unless the script locks them; created target-9 zones write `(filled_areas_thickness no)`, so their fills are plotted as written; checked by refills on `kicad-cli` 10.0 and loads on 9.0 (c0031).
- Copper check: Fenolite now judges shorts and clearance of a board's copper itself, exactly and without `kicad-cli`. `fenolite.geometry.thick` gives thick shapes (a core swept by a disc) with exact touch, closer-than, gap and witness tests; `fenolite.checks.check_copper` judges tracks, arcs, vias, board-frame pads and zone fills pair by pair, with the clearance in force taken from the project's own rules, net classes and board minimum (`checks.clearance`, `backends.base.DesignRules` and `DesignRulesSource`, `backends.kicad.copperrules`); `fenolite check` gains the default stage `copper.clearance`, between `erc.lite` and `drc.kicad`, and six codes (`copper.short`, `copper.clearance`, `copper.zone-overlap`, `copper.rules-incomplete`, `copper.item-unsupported`, `copper.clearance-unset`); `fenolite build` judges the triad it is about to write and refuses a short or a clearance error (`--copper-check refuse`, the default) or reports it and writes (`--copper-check warn`), with `result.copper_check`. Probes on `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 show why: a via that touches a track of another net whose net owns a pad is loaded on that net and no short is reported, copper without pads is merged into one net, and 10.0.6 reports a short between two nets with pads in about half of its runs; the verdicts of `check_copper` and of KiCad's DRC agree on every parity canary (seven pair kinds under a rule, a class and a board-minimum clearance, six resolution cases, and zone fills on 10.0.6). A board that holds a real short now exits 5 under `check` (`docs/formats/kicad/copper.md`, `docs/evidence/copper-check.md`, change c0029).
- Board-frame queries and manual copper: `board_pads`/`find_pads` give every pad's board-frame position, rotation, layers, net, hole and exact copper entries (all pads sharing a number); `placed_extent` gives a placed footprint's front and back courtyard with rotation and side applied; `BoardFrame` in `backends.base`; DSL copper intents (`Part.pad`, `via_step`, `Design.track`, `Design.via`, `Design.stitch`) resolved by the build after placement, with nets inferred and checked, version-8 uuids from the caller's key, and stale script copper removed on rebuild; checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0. `fenolite build` reports `result.copper`; the routed example is `examples/blink_routed`, and the guide is `docs/copper.md` (c0028).
- Altium change order without class differences (change c0048): a net class is declared in the schematic by a Parameter Set directive with a `ClassName` parameter on each of its nets, once per sheet, and a new project file ends with `[PrjClassGen]` (`NetClassManualEnabled=1`), so "Design » Update PCB Document" no longer proposes to remove the class from the board; the PCB document holds one component class per schematic sheet (named after the module, or after the sheet for the top sheet and for the single sheet of a flat build), and a project file with a PCB document or module sheets turns the rooms off for every schematic document. "Supply Nets" rules and rooms are not written and are documented as differences Altium may still propose (`docs/altium.md`, "Change order"). The golden schematics and project files of the samples with a net class or module sheets are rebuilt. Experimental; the maintainer's report of 2026-10-04 (AD 26.5) found only the "Supply Nets" rules in the change order of a build with module sheets, and the repeat of the same day on the rebuilt samples found the same for a flat build (`H-A-ECO-SHEETCLASS`; Part E of `docs/evidence/altium-pcb.md`).
- Altium PCB copper: the experimental `<name>.PcbDoc` now holds tracks, arcs and through vias, zones as unpoured polygon pours (repour in Altium), a 2- or 4-layer stack whose inner layers are signal layers or internal planes (`design.board(..., copper=4, planes={...})`), net classes and Clearance, Width and Routing Via Style rules; `--copper-from board.kicad_pcb` copies the copper of a routed KiCad board after checking that it matches the design, and the board's placements win; blind, buried and micro vias, split planes and copper outside the stack are refused; checked by `kicad-cli pcb import`; evidence `INFERRED`, with the maintainer's Altium Designer report recorded as an author report (c0038).
- KiCad libraries: pinned fetch and verified cache of the official libraries at 10.0.6 and 9.0.9 (`tools/kicad_libs_fetch.py`, `FENOLITE_LIBS_CACHE`), a `cache` library source, opt-in `kicad_common.json` path variables (`LibraryConfig.read_common`), a directory-scan fallback (row origin `scan`), builds that resolve from the cache and report `scan` rows, a census of the fetched trees (the 10.0.6 install equals its pinned tag), demo-library corpus rows (`libs`), an `env` keyword on `KicadCli.drc`, and `kicad-cli` probes for relative paths, the versioned fallback, nested tables, the configuration folder and KiCad's own path variables. The probes refuted one rule: KiCad resolves a relative library `uri` against its working directory, not against the table's folder, so the resolver now joins a relative `uri` to the project folder for rows of every table (a nested table's `../x` now means the folder above the project, not above the nested table).
- No-connect pins: `fenolite.dsl.no_connect(*pins)` marks pins as intentionally unconnected; the model keeps them as `Circuit.no_connects` (additive, schema regenerated); the Altium schematic gets a No ERC directive (record 22) at each marked pin, in both forms; the KiCad build keeps the marks in `.fenolite/` and refuses a marked pin on a net (`build.no-connect-on-net`, `model.no-connect-on-net`); `erc.lite.floating-pin` skips marked pins; evidence `INFERRED`, with the maintainer's Altium Designer report recorded as an author report (pending: Part N of `docs/evidence/altium-schematic.md`).
- Altium hierarchy and harnesses: `fenolite build --target altium --altium-sheets modules` writes a top sheet with sheet symbols and one sheet per top-level module, with matching ports and sheet entries; `fenolite.dsl.Harness(name, members)` groups nets, drawn as harness connectors, entries and signal harness lines in the binary schematic, with `.Harness` definition files; the PCB document links parts on module sheets through their sheet symbol; the default `flat` output is unchanged; evidence `INFERRED`, with the maintainer's Altium Designer report recorded as an author report (c0037). After the maintainer's report of 2026-10-03 (AD 26.5): a harness between two neighbouring modules is drawn on the top sheet as one signal harness line between the two sheet entries, without connector blocks and labels, which removes the "multiple names" warnings; the top sheet then gets no `.Harness` file; a new project file of a `modules` build lists the schematic documents first and together (the top sheet, the module sheets, then the PCB document, the libraries and the harness files), because with the PCB document and the libraries in between Altium Designer took only the first module sheet into the hierarchy (`H-A-SCH-HIER-ORDER`, confirmed by the maintainer's second report; the earlier guess about small sheets, `H-A-SCHBIN-MINI`, is refuted and its padding parameter `FenoliteNote` is removed); a flat build keeps its project file bytes, and an existing project file is kept as it is; `CHANNELOFFSET` of a PCB component counts the parts of its own sheet; a readback test checks that every board component links to a schematic component through a sheet symbol and that every module sheet is reachable from the top sheet.
- `fenolite export` and `fenolite render`: fabrication files (Gerbers and the job file, drill, positions, IPC-D-356) and review views (SVG plots, rendered PNG) through `kicad-cli` on a copy of the project, written only with `--confirm`, with the manifest `fenolite-artifacts.json` (`schemas/fenolite.artifacts.v0.json`): per file its SHA-256 and a date-stripped `content_sha256`, because KiCad stamps the creation date into Gerber and drill files. When one kind fails nothing is written. Opt-in `render` stage in `check` (`--stages render`), which never gives an error. Commands may declare `example_tools`, and the suites run such examples against a fake `kicad-cli`. Measured on `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (`docs/evidence/kicad-export.md`); user guide `docs/exports.md` (c0024).
- Altium target writes PCB files: `fenolite build --target altium` writes `<name>.PcbLib` with the resolved KiCad footprints (SMD and through-hole pads, roundrect corners, silkscreen, fabrication and courtyard lines and arcs) and, experimentally, `<name>.PcbDoc` with the outline, two-layer stack, placed components, pads, nets and designators, linked to the schematic by unique ids; empty streams in compound files; write kinds `altium_pcblib` and `altium_pcbdoc`; new `altium.*` issue codes; kicad-cli `fp upgrade` and `pcb import` as oracles; capabilities entry `altium-pcb-writer`; evidence `INFERRED`, Altium checks as author reports.
- `fenolite check` judges KiCad DRC violations as located `kicad.drc.<type>` issues, compares the pad nets of model, board and IPC-D-356 export, and runs RT2 on request; RT0 to RT2 pass on the corpus with kicad-cli 10.0.6 and on the 9.0.9.1 demo boards in the `kicad-9` job. Findings carry KiCad's severity (which follows the project's `rule_severities`) and are located as `REF-PIN`, `REF`, a board locator or the report position; an unrouted board now exits 5. `netlist.assignment_compare` (default stage) compares partitions of `REF-PIN` elements and locates a reassigned pad; `roundtrip.rt2` (opt-in with `--stages`) fails only where KiCad repeats its own DRC report and otherwise says that RT2 is not judged, because on boards with hundreds of violations two DRC runs of one file differ. New: `checks.{drc_json,assignment_compare,rt2}`, `backends.kicad.padnets`, `KicadOracle.netlist` and `.rt2`, `NetlistOracle`, `RoundTripOracle`, `PadNetList`, `NetlistOutcome` and `Rt2Outcome` in `backends.base`, `DrcReport.entries()`, the `rt2-9` corpus rows, and four recorded measurements (token census, 10.0 byte identity, 5 MiB throughput, Edge.Cuts chaining: one demo outline is open by 33 nm) (`docs/evidence/kicad-rt2.md`, `docs/cli-contract.md`).
- Altium target writes schematic libraries: `fenolite build --target altium` writes `<name>.SchLib` with the resolved KiCad symbols (pins, units, Part Zero, synthesised body) and a generic `X.SchLib` for each Altium link library, lists them in the project file and draws the schematic from the same geometry; `cfb.Storage` for nested storages; write kind `altium_schlib`; new `altium.*` issue codes; kicad-cli `sym upgrade` round trip as oracle; evidence `INFERRED`, Altium checks as author reports (c0034).
- Layout preservation: `fenolite build` over an existing project keeps footprints moved in KiCad (matched by uuid, `fenolite.path` or a `moved(old, new)` alias, with the precedence locked `place()` > board > `place()` > staging), routing and zones on nets that still exist, fills while their digests are unchanged (`zone.fill-stale` otherwise), board-only footprints with their pins on their pads' nets, board settings, project keys and user custom rules after Fenolite's; removed parts give `layout.orphan`; `fenolite check` no longer asks footprints added in KiCad for a symbol; rebuilds and a regenerated `.fenolite/` are byte-identical; `build.layout-exists` now guards only `fp-lib-table` and vendored footprints; checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 (`fenolite.lens.preserve`, `Design.moved`, `dsl.moves`, `result.preserved`, `docs/lens.md`).
- Altium target writes the binary schematic by default: `fenolite build --target altium` writes `<name>.SchDoc` as an MS-CFB compound file (stdlib writer; `FileHeader` and `Storage` streams; deterministic bytes); `--altium-format ascii` keeps the ASCII form; write kind `altium_schdoc_binary`; issue `altium.schematic-too-large`; evidence `INFERRED`, with the maintainer's Altium 365 Viewer and Altium Designer checks recorded as author reports.
- Experimental Altium target: `fenolite build design.py --out DIR --target altium` writes an Altium project file (written once) and an ASCII schematic with one generic component per part, library and footprint links, net labels and power ports on wire stubs, a deterministic grid layout and path-keyed unique ids; listed under `result.experimental` of `fenolite capabilities`; evidence `INFERRED`, with the maintainer's Altium checks recorded as author reports. Power ports of adjacent pins alternate between short and long stubs, so they do not overlap.
- Build properties and vendoring: DSL parts take `properties={…}` (printable text, reserved KiCad and Fenolite names refused), written as hidden properties after `fenolite.path` and read back unchanged; `fenolite build` copies the placed footprints of every library row (project, global, template) into `lib/` with project rows, so built projects pass `kicad-cli`'s library check in isolation (`--vendor project` keeps the old rule); new codes `build.property-reserved`, `build.property-invalid`, `build.property-conflict`, `build.vendor-unsafe-name` and `build.library-changed`. Measured on `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: vendored global footprints give no library violation, a project row hides a global row of the same nickname, and user properties load and survive a 10.0.6 re-save.
- Read-only `fenolite check` v0 (model validation, ERC lite, KiCad DRC with a scoped rules canary, RT1) with evidence per stage, plus `fenolite inspect --summary` and `fenolite doctor`; `kicad-cli` runs only on a closed copy of the project (`fenolite.checks`, `fenolite.backends.kicad.{projectset,canary,oracle,roundtrip,helpmatrix}`, `KicadBackend.validate`, `kicad_cli_candidates`, neutral `RoundTrip`, `Validation`, `Validator`, `ProjectSet`, `DrcOutcome` and `Oracle` in `backends.base`, `docs/formats/kicad/cli.md`, `docs/evidence/kicad-check.md`). Observed on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: DRC on the copy set equals DRC on the whole folder, the canary fires once, changes nothing else and is silenced by a dropped rules file or an ignored clearance severity, help pages follow one `Usage:` grammar, and KiCad writes a DRC report for boards Fenolite refuses for trailing content. On some boards kicad-cli 10.0.6 does not repeat its own DRC report between identical runs (the partner item of a clearance violation, sometimes the count), so DRC comparisons repeat their reference run. The canary tracks change other violations of large boards on 9.0 and 10.0 (other partner items, sometimes one violation more or less), so on both majors `drc.kicad` runs DRC twice: a plain run gives the counted report and the canary run the verdict. The copy set, the canary verdict and the help grammar are `KICAD-VERIFIED` on 9.0 and 10.0, and so are the `drc.kicad` stage's oracle and `fenolite doctor`'s matrix.
- Sheet templates: a neutral drawing-sheet model with the board's paper and title block, a `.kicad_wks` reader and writer whose single output KiCad 9.0 and 10.0 load, project keys for the sheet and text variables, `fenolite template build` from closed `*.sheet.toml` files with per-number provenance, two CC0 examples, and one sheet drawn as predicted on A4 and A3 boards by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 (`fenolite.model.presentation`, `fenolite.backends.kicad.wks`, `fenolite.templates`, `pcb.project_paper`/`paper_node`/`title_block_node`, `pro.apply_sheet_keys`, `schemas/fenolite.model.v0/drawing_sheet.json`, `docs/formats/kicad/worksheet.md`, `docs/formats/sheets.md`, `docs/sheet-templates.md`, ADR-0005 `Accepted`). Observed on both majors: a `User` page is truncated to whole mils, so `H-K-PCB-PAPER` is refuted and superseded by `H-K-PCB-PAPER-2`; KiCad draws sub-micrometre sheet lengths unchanged, so `H-K-WKS-RES` is refuted and superseded by `H-K-WKS-RES-2`; numbers step past `9` in repeated labels, and legacy `%` codes still resolve.
- KiCad board-setup minimums: the design's board-wide rules now lower into `board.design_settings.rules` of `.kicad_pro` on synthesis and update, so Board Setup shows the fab rule set's values, and class clearances that a board-wide rule overrides are reported (`kicad.project.class-shadowed`). Measured on `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: the five minimum keys are read and applied, a board-wide custom rule governs below the minimums although the manuals call them absolute (`H-K-PRO-MIN-RULE` refuted, `H-K-PRO-MIN-RULE-2`), and such a rule overrides a larger class clearance (`lowering.lower_minimums`, `class_conflicts`, `pro.project_minimums`, five `kicad.project.*` codes, `pro-min-*` probes).
- Thin DSL and build: `fenolite build design.py --out DIR` turns a stdlib-only `design.py` into a self-contained KiCad 9.0 or 10.0 project (triad, vendored footprints, per-target `fp-lib-table`, `.fenolite/` cache and build record) with ids keyed by names and paths, byte-identical rebuilds, a refusal to overwrite outputs edited since the last build (`--discard-layout`), `FEN-3005` for geometry errors, refusals that list their issues, and the CC0 blink checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0.
- Roadmap to 1.0 (`docs/roadmap.md`): phases and change ranges, where KiCad ends and the second backend starts, budgets, and proposed cuts for a leaner 1.0 (pending the maintainer's decision).
- KiCad project files: an exact JSON codec, `.kicad_pro` synthesis from GUI-saved templates for KiCad 9 and 10, in-place updates of managed keys with unknown keys preserved, net classes read into and lowered from the model, and `write_triad`, which always ships board, project and rules together; `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 enforce the written net classes (`fenolite.backends.kicad.{_json,pro,proerrors,triad}`, `lowering.lower_netclass`, `KicadBackend.lower`, `docs/formats/kicad/project.md`, `docs/evidence/kicad-project.md`, `tests/data/kicad/project/empty_10.kicad_pro`, demo `project` corpus rows). Observed on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6: net-class patterns are whole-name wildcards and regular expressions, a board-setup minimum governs a lower class value, `kicad-cli` never rewrites `.kicad_pro` (10.0.6 writes a `.kicad_prl`), and a `.kicad_dru` is read even without a project file, which partly refutes `H-K-TOK-RULES-SILENT`.
- KiCad footprint and rules writers: `.kicad_mod` writing for KiCad 9 and 10, a full-dialect `.kicad_dru` reader and writer with comments kept in place, and rules lowering in priority order with a closed selector grammar, target gating and a self-check, each proved on `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 with a canary rule (`fenolite.backends.kicad.{mod,dru,rulemap,lowering}`, `docs/formats/kicad/rules.md`, `tests/data/kicad/rules/`, `tests/data/libs/Escapes_v9.pretty/`). Footprints placed in boards read as definitions (`board_footprints`); the footprint codec round trip holds over 1 674 demo and 424 upgraded third-party footprints, which settles the footprint half of `H-K-LIB-READ`, and `H-K-SEXPR-ESCAPES` now holds on 9.0.9 too. Observed on both majors: the later rule governs, every selector op and the `*` glob select the model's items, and KiCad compares net and class names without regard to case.
- KiCad board backend, part 2: a `.kicad_pcb` writer for KiCad 9.0 and 10.0 (net form per target, obsolete rows, token gating with `--allow-lossy`, KiCad 8 boards read-only with `FEN-7003`), library footprints placed on either side with key-derived uuids, DRC JSON reports read into neutral types, the global flags `--kicad-version` and `--allow-lossy`, and written boards, bottom placements and library parity checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 with probe outcomes pinned per version.
- Verification evidence: `fenolite.verify` (label grammar with qualified labels, hypothesis-register reader, cited-id scan, `release_verified`) and a guard test that keeps `docs/hypotheses.md` consistent with every hypothesis id cited in live text; refuted rows relabelled, `H-K-LIB-DRC` and `H-G-SHAPELY-GC` registered as pending, author-report rows for the earlier second-backend write validation (never release-verified), the S-0002 licence settled, and the change-id and ADR numbering rules written down.
- KiCad board backend, part 1: backend protocol and registry (`capabilities` lists backends), a package `kicad-cli` runner on temporary copies, and a typed `.kicad_pcb` reader for 8.0 and newer with lossless slots, same-version rebuild (RT1) over the demos and upgraded third-party boards, and placements and pads confirmed by `kicad-cli` exports on 9.0 and 10.0 (`fenolite.backends.{base,registry}`, `fenolite.backends.kicad.{backend,cli,ipcd356,layers,_fpmap,pcb}`, ADR-0002, `docs/formats/kicad/board.md`, `docs/evidence/kicad-board-read.md`, `tests/data/kicad/board/two_layer.kicad_pcb`). Model: `FootprintInstance.attributes`, `Via.via_type`, `ZoneFill.island`, `Zone.name`, and `model.duplicate-ref` as a warning for board-only and unannotated references. `H-K-PCB-READ` and `H-K-UNIT` are corpus-verified and `H-K-PCB-POS` verified with `kicad-cli` 9.0.9 and 10.0.6. Observed: a 10.0.6 re-save removes teardrop zones and `Footprint` properties and replaces `fp_text` items, so `H-K-UUID-KEEP` is refuted and superseded by `H-K-UUID-KEEP-2`; the IPC-D-356 `R` field of a pad is the negated stored pad angle.
- KiCad libraries: neutral library definitions (`FootprintDef`, `SymbolDef`), `.kicad_mod`/`.kicad_sym`/`.kicad_symdir` readers with slots, library-table resolution (project, global, template and nested tables, path variables, local install), and an authored CC0 mini library checked by `kicad-cli` 9.0 and 10.0 (`fenolite.model.library`, `fenolite.backends.kicad.{mod,sym,libs,liberrors}`, `schemas/fenolite.model.v0/library.json`, `docs/formats/kicad/libraries.md`, `docs/evidence/kicad-libs.md`, `tests/data/libs/`). The census of the local 10.0.6 install reads 15 450 footprints and 22 860 symbols without error; `H-K-LIB-NAME-STEM` (9.0.9, 10.0.6) and `H-K-LIB-SYMDIR` (10.0.6) verified. Observed: KiCad sorts pads, graphics, symbols and pins when it saves, and the official footprints repeat graphic uuids inside single files.
- KiCad format versions and gating (`versions.py`), token inventory `tokens.toml` with fuzz results on kicad-cli 9.0.9 and 10.0.6, `kicad-9` CI job and `kicad_min_major` test marker, error codes FEN-3003/FEN-3004/FEN-7002 (`tools/kicad_token_fuzz.py`, `tools/gen_token_docs.py`, `docs/formats/kicad/versions.md`, `docs/formats/kicad/tokens.md`, `docs/evidence/kicad/token-fuzz/`). All 184 inventory rows are verified on both majors; observed: both majors refuse future board headers, and 9.0.9 keeps pad-level tenting and drops the press-fit pad property.
- KiCad S-expression layer (spelling-preserving parser, KiCad-style and compact printers, fragment codec, tree equality), slots helper with extension-bag persistence, KiCad demo and third-party corpus rows with RT0, and the `kicad-10` Docker oracle job (`fenolite.backends.kicad`, `docs/formats/kicad/sexpr.md`, `docs/formats/kicad/corpus.md`; `FENOLITE_REQUIRE` required-resource mode; `tools/corpus_fetch.py --uses/--exclude-uses`). Verified with `kicad-cli` 10.0.6: lexical acceptance and rejections, string escapes, number reading, re-save equality; `H-K-SEXPR-NUM-WRITE` refuted (KiCad writes non-length values with up to 10 decimals).
- Geometry kernel: exact integer predicates, three-point arcs, polygons with a canonical normal form, mixed contours, deterministic µdeg transforms, STR spatial index, boolean backend protocol with a convex-only stdlib fallback, and KiCad frame and arc evidence tests (`fenolite.geometry`, `docs/geometry.md`, `docs/formats/kicad/geometry.md`; `H-G-ROT-DIR`, `H-G-BOTTOM-PLACE`, `H-G-ARC-ROUND`, `H-G-ARC-DIR` and `H-G-PTS-ARC` verified with `kicad-cli` 10.0.6).

### Changed

- Packaging (change c0052): the `route` extra is removed (it named `freerouting-client`, which no code imports; routers run as separate programs); the package metadata gains `[project.urls]` and the classifier `Development Status :: 3 - Alpha`; the source distribution is built from an allowlist (`src`, `tests`, `docs`, `examples`, `schemas`, `tools` and the project documents) and no longer holds `openspec/`, the agent instruction files, `packaging/`, `uv.lock` or the `Makefile`. The wheel is unchanged.
- `fenolite capabilities`: the KiCad backend's evidence is computed from what `read` and `write` return and is checked against the hypothesis register: a level stronger than a row it names fails `tests/unit/test_capability_evidence.py`. The level stays `INFERRED`, and `docs/cli-contract.md` explains why the rows it names are stronger.
- Documents (change c0052): `docs/cli-contract.md` has a `template` section, and the consistency suite fails when a registered command is missing from that page; `docs/provenance.md` explains the private residue gate (the three `FENOLITE_RESIDUE_*` variables, where the gate is on, and that CI runs with it off); the register rows `H-G-ANGLE` and `H-A-UNIT` record what was observed, both still `INFERRED`; two skip guards for things that exist are removed from the tests.
- Source audit of S-0142: the unpinned AltiumSharp entry is marked not used (version 2 cites non-public decompiled material); the Altium fact rows that cited it are re-sourced (S-0130, S-0131, S-0132, S-0134, S-0143, S-0145, S-0150 at version 1) or kept as `INFERRED` hypotheses with no permitted source, and the `Storage` weight-key contradiction with version 1 is recorded. No written byte changes.
- Roadmap (`docs/roadmap.md`): the gaps found by a board built only through the public API are proposed as c0026–c0031; change sizes are design-days, and a pace measured from the git history replaces the old time ratio as the forecast; the implementation order now runs c0026 → c0027 → c0013 → c0019 → c0020 → c0021 → c0012 → c0028–c0031.
- CLI: `--fields` now also restricts the `plan` that the dispatcher adds to `result` on `--dry-run` or without `--confirm`, as the contract says; a refused command lists the `issues` of the raised error in its envelope; `GeometryError` maps to `FEN-3005` (exit 3).
- The KiCad capability report lists `kicad_pro` in `write_kinds` and `lower` in `operations` (`KicadBackend.lower` returns the board, project and rules files).
- Development: the test suite runs on parallel workers (`make check`, and `make check-fast` for the tests that need no external tool), the CI jobs record the outcome of every oracle test, internal helpers of the KiCad footprint writer were reorganised, and the repository ignores local build folders. None of this changes what the command line or the library does.

### Fixed

- Windows: `kicad-cli.exe` is found on `PATH` and in KiCad's default install folders, which KiCad's installer does not put on `PATH`; files Fenolite writes keep LF line ends; a board on another drive than the working folder can be routed; a drawing-sheet path starting with `/` counts as absolute.
- `fenolite route` reads the net classes from the project file beside the board. Before, every net was routed with the default track width, clearance and via size, whatever its class said: a KiCad board holds no net class.
- `fenolite route --router freerouting` lists a net under `unrouted` when Freerouting reports that it left one of its connections open. Before, a net with any copper counted as routed, so a partly routed net was reported as done; KiCad's DRC in `fenolite check` still found it.
- A board whose rotated pad or rotated footprint carries a slotted hole is read and written again, and its footprints can be written as library files: the writer refused them (`KiCad writer cannot represent this slot rotation`, `KiCad oval drills can encode only horizontal or vertical slot axes`). `Padstack.hole_rotation` is now the slot's axis in the pad's own frame everywhere; on a board it used to include the pad's angle.
- Geometry: the documented and tested bound of two successive `Transform.apply` calls is `(1 + |cos θ| + |sin θ|)/2` nm per axis, at most `(1 + √2)/2 ≈ 1.2071` nm, not 1 nm; `test_composition_rounds_once` no longer fails at random, and a test reaches the bound exactly. No code changes (c0050).
- Script copper in the Altium build (change c0053): `fenolite build --target altium` now writes the tracks, vias and stitching vias that the design script declares (`Design.track`, `Design.via`, `Design.stitch`) into the experimental `<name>.PcbDoc`; before, they were left out without an issue. An intent that does not resolve refuses the build, an intent that creates nothing is reported, and with `--copper-from` the board wins with one `altium.not-lowered` info.
- `fenolite check` no longer reports `kicad.drc.rules-not-loaded` for rules that were loaded on a board with 499 or more clearance violations: `kicad-cli` stops reporting clearance violations near 499 per run and can leave the canary's own out, so such a run is now `inconclusive` with the reason `clearance-limit` (`kicad.drc.rules-unchecked`). The two-run canary test compares two DRC runs against what was measured to repeat, and the contract says which part of `check`'s output repeats between runs (c0051).
- Altium PCB document in the form Altium saves: `<name>.PcbDoc` now holds the whole `Board6` record (board keys, the outline as a polygon, sheet, sub-stack, drill pair, routing keys and snap grid, written by rule in `docboard.py` on top of `libboard.py`), net and component records with the common keys and unique ids, `UniqueIDPrimitiveInformation`, the option storages (`Advanced Placer Options6`, `Pin Swap Options6`, `Design Rule Checker Options6`, `PadViaLibrary`, `PadViaLibraryCache`, `LayerKindMapping`, `ConstraintManager`, `SignalClasses`) and 13 more empty storages; `write_pcbdoc` takes the file name. The first form gave a "catastrophic" error in Altium Designer 26.5 (maintainer's report, c0035); the new one opens there and its change order from the schematic reports no difference (author report of 2026-10-03). Texts, pads, tracks and arcs keep their short records.
- Altium PCB library opens in Altium Designer's form: `<name>.PcbLib` now holds the 53-byte `FileHeader` (version and library id), the whole board record in `Library/Data` (layer stack, grid, layer sets and view keys, written by rule in `libboard.py`) and the `Library` side streams (`EmbeddedFonts`, `ModelsNoEmbed`, `Textures`, `ComponentParamsTOC`, `PadViaLibrary`); `FileHeaderSix` of `<name>.PcbDoc` carries its id. The first form gave a "catastrophic" error in Altium Designer (maintainer's report, c0035); the new one opens in Altium Designer 26.5 (author report of 2026-10-03).

## [0.0.1.dev0] - 2026-09-30

Pre-alpha repository bootstrap; not usable for real boards yet. Published to PyPI as `fenolite` and its
alias `phenolite` on 2026-09-30 (`pip install --pre fenolite`).

### Added

- Repository bootstrap: package skeleton (`src/fenolite`, stdlib-only core, closed extras), Apache-2.0 licence with NOTICE and SPDX headers, `LEGAL.md`/`LEGAL-ANNEX.md`, DCO, ADR-0004, evidence registers and the `unit` CI baseline (ruff, pyright strict, pytest on ubuntu and macos).
- CLI contract v0: JSON envelope (`schemas/fenolite.envelope.v0.json`) and typed stderr errors (`schemas/fenolite.error.v0.json`), exit codes 0–7, `--fields`, mutation protocol (`--dry-run`/`--confirm`, atomic writes with `.bak`, receipts), `--seed`/`--timestamp`, the `capabilities` command, a consistency test over every command and a package-layering test.
- IP hygiene: ADR-0003 (clean-room and provenance), four explicit prohibitions in `LEGAL.md`, provenance rules and test, residue scan (`tools/residue/scan.py`: structural patterns, blob hashes, private token and hash lists kept outside the repository, UTF-16/cp1252/zip awareness, `--staged` and `--history` modes) with a pre-commit hook and CI step, copyleft-dependency and no-ported-code guards, corpus manifest with the embeddable rule and `tools/corpus_fetch.py`.
- Design model v0: `fenolite.core` (integer nm/µdeg units with exact parsing and half-even conversion to 1/10 000 mil, ids, evidence levels, errors and issues, provenance, coordinates, atomic I/O) and `fenolite.model` (entity header with native ids, provenance and extension bags; circuit, board, rules, manufacturing and findings layers; `Design` with indexes, validation and entity replacement; canonical JSON with a validating decoder; JSON Schemas in `schemas/fenolite.model.v0/`), ADR-0001 and `docs/design-model.md`.
- Name reservation: alias distribution `phenolite` (installs `fenolite`) and a release workflow that publishes both to PyPI with trusted publishing when a GitHub Release is published.
